# Malaria RCT Brief

Automated weekly scan for new malaria randomized controlled trials.
Sources: PubMed, ClinicalTrials.gov, Europe PMC (incl. medRxiv preprints and
Cochrane review metadata). Most recent week on top. The structured dataset lives
in data/trials.csv.

---

<!-- New weekly entries are inserted directly below this line -->

## Week of 2026-07-28

### Efficacy of a volatile pyrethroid spatial emanator (SE) in reducing Anopheles host-seeking in outdoor kitchens in Southern Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (41196912)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, placebo-controlled
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-11-06
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households with outdoor kitchens; local mosquito collectors conducting human landing collections
- **N:** 41
- **Impact:** Spatial emanator devices reduced Anopheles host-seeking activity in outdoor kitchens by approximately 65-70%, though efficacy declined to 20-25% by four weeks after deployment and was not significant during early evening hours (18:00-21:00).
- **Effect:** reduction in mosquito host-seeking activity 65-70% (declining to 20-25% by 4 weeks)
- **Species:** Anopheles (species not specified, malaria vector)
- **Follow-up:** 15 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41196912/

### Efficacy of ceiling-mounted mosquito nets for malaria vector control in a Peruvian Amazon riverine community: A stepped-wedge cluster randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (41118358)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Peru
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-10-21
- **Trial period:** 2024 to 2024
- **Population:** Households in a riverine Amazonian community
- **N:** 69
- **Impact:** Ceiling-mounted mosquito nets significantly reduced indoor Anopheles mosquito counts and biting rates but had no significant effect on peridomestic mosquito indices.
- **Effect:** percent reduction in indoor Anopheles counts 55% (95% CI 33%-74%)
- **Follow-up:** March to December 2024 (approximately 9-10 months)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41118358/

### The role of seasonal malaria chemoprevention in the effect of azithromycin on child mortality: A secondary analysis of the CHAT cluster randomized clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (41021553)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nouna District, Burkina Faso
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, placebo-controlled, secondary analysis
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-09-29
- **Class:** SMC; MDA
- **Population:** children under 5
- **N:** 341
- **Impact:** Mass azithromycin distribution was associated with reduced child mortality during SMC season and in areas with lower SMC coverage, but the interaction between AZ effect and SMC season/coverage was not statistically significant.
- **Effect:** mortality rate ratio 0.77 (SMC season); 0.89 (non-SMC season); 0.73 (<80% SMC coverage); 1.0 (≥80% SMC coverage) (95% CI 0.60-0.98; 0.68-1.15; 0.56-0.96; 0.59-1.69)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41021553/

### HLA-G 14 bp polymorphism: Implications and its associated risk to malaria severity among iron-fortified Ghanaian infants and young children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (40953010)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-09-15
- **Population:** infants and young children aged 6-36 months consuming semi-solid foods
- **N:** 432
- **Impact:** Iron-fortified children carrying the HLA-G 14 bp insertion/deletion polymorphism had an elevated risk of severe malaria, which in turn predisposed them to anaemia.
- **Effect:** association/risk
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40953010/

### Physical integrity and residual bio-efficacy of PBO-pyrethroid synergist-treated and pyrethroid-only LLINs after 1.5 years of field use in Western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (40794684)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** prospective observational field study, cross-sectional household surveys
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-08-12
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households with LLINs distributed for malaria control
- **Impact:** Both PBO-pyrethroid and pyrethroid-only LLINs showed significant declines in physical integrity and bioefficacy over 18 months, with PBO-LLINs maintaining better physical integrity but both net types losing effectiveness against susceptible and resistant Anopheles gambiae.
- **Effect:** percentage of torn nets and mosquito mortality 45.2% pyrethroid-LLINs vs 21.8% PBO-LLINs torn at 18 months; mortality dropped from 97.6% to 18.4% (pyrethroid) and 98.6% to 18.5% (PBO) against susceptible mosquitoes, and from 36.9% to 6.8% (pyrethroid) and 55.6% to 11.8% (PBO) against resistant mosquitoes (95% CI 1.685-16.816)
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40794684/

### R21/Matrix-M malaria vaccine drives diverse immune responses in pre-exposed adults: insights from a phase IIb controlled human malaria infection trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (40692795)
- **Journal:** Frontiers in immunology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase IIb
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** controlled human malaria infection (CHMI), phase IIb clinical trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-07-07
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** semi-immune healthy adult volunteers with varying prior malaria exposure
- **N:** 19
- **Impact:** R21/Matrix-M vaccination induced broad, heterogeneous, and sustained antibody and memory B cell responses along with increased circulating Tfh cells, which may contribute to protection against malaria in previously exposed adults.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40692795/

### Feasibility and effectiveness of tailored interventions for two populations at high-risk of malaria in Senegal: Koranic school children and gold miners.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (40299948)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Senegal
- **Design:** controlled pre/post survey, cluster-randomized
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-04-29
- **Trial period:** 2021 to 2022
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; diagnostic
- **Population:** Koranic school children (talibés) and gold miners
- **N:** 3940
- **Impact:** The intervention package significantly reduced malaria prevalence and increased LLIN use among talibés but showed no measurable effect among gold miners.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk difference -12.6 percentage points (RDT prevalence, talibés); +44.0 percentage points (net use, talibés) (95% CI -2.7 to -22.4 (prevalence); 36.3 to 51.6 (net use))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 4 months (Oct 2021 to Feb 2022)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40299948/

### LLIN Evaluation in Uganda Project
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT04566510)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, NA
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2025-02-26
- **Trial period:** 2020-10-17 to 2023-03-31
- **Intervention(s):** long lasting insecticidal nets with alpha-cypermethrin + pyriproxyfen (PPF LLIN, Royal Guard)); long lasting insecticidal nets with deltamethrin + piperonyl butoxide (PBO LLIN, PermaNet 3.0)); Social behaviour change communication
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** residents of all ages in communities surrounding public health facilities
- **N:** 32895
- **Impact:** There was no evidence of a difference in malaria incidence or parasite prevalence between pyrethroid-PBO and pyrethroid-pyriproxyfen LLINs two years after mass distribution in Uganda, and LLIN ownership was low in both arms.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio 1.06 (95% CI 0.91-1.22)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 24 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04566510

### Indoor Residual Spraying With Fludora Fusion for Malaria Control in Pyrethroid-Resistant Areas of Gujarat, India: A Community-Randomized Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39949897)
- **Journal:** Journal of tropical medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** India (Gujarat: Kheda, Vadodara, Panchmahal districts)
- **Design:** community-randomized, entomological field trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-01-29
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2018
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** villages/houses in pyrethroid-resistant malaria vector areas
- **N:** 15
- **Impact:** Fludora Fusion showed longer residual efficacy (6 months) than bendiocarb (3-4 months) and reduced indoor resting density, parous rate, and entomological inoculation rate of Anopheles culicifacies compared to bendiocarb-sprayed houses.
- **Effect:** Daily entomological inoculation rate (EIR) declined from 1.275 (prespray) to 0.5225 (Fludora Fusion arm) and 0.3802 (bendiocarb arm) postspray
- **Follow-up:** May 2017 to February 2018
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39949897/

### Targeted distribution of long-lasting insecticidal nets by community health workers to sustain household coverage: A pilot feasibility study in Western Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39854605)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** pilot feasibility study, cluster-randomized (village-level, two villages), non-blinded
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2025-01-24
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households with children under five, community health worker-served villages
- **Impact:** Targeted LLIN distribution by community health workers increased the proportion of households meeting universal coverage and the proportion of children under 5 sleeping under an LLIN in the intervention village, while both measures declined in the control village.
- **Effect:** change in percentage of households meeting universal coverage increase from 4.0% to 6.5% (intervention) vs decrease from 7.8% to 1.8% (control)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** not specified (post mass distribution campaign period)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39854605/

### Safety and Efficacy of Incorporating Actellic® 300 CS into Soil Wall Plaster for Control of Malaria Vectors in Rural Northeastern Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39852655)
- **Journal:** Tropical medicine and infectious disease
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized, controlled, cluster-randomized
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-12-24
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** rural households in mud-walled huts
- **N:** 360
- **Impact:** Huts treated with Hut Decoration for Malaria Control (HD4MC) and IRS showed significantly reduced malaria vector density and feeding rates compared to control huts, with HD4MC being safe and comparably effective to IRS.
- **Effect:** reduction in vector density and feeding rate
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39852655/

### Effectiveness of Monthly Versus Two-Dose Regimen of Sulphadoxine-Pyrimethamine for Intermittent Preventive Treatment of Malaria in Pregnancy in Southeastern Nigeria: A Randomised Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39736161)
- **Journal:** West African journal of medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-08-01
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women at gestational ages between 13-24 weeks attending antenatal clinic
- **Impact:** Monthly IPT-SP significantly reduced placental and peripheral malaria parasitaemia, maternal anaemia, and neonatal parasitaemia/anaemia compared to the standard two-dose regimen, though low birth weight incidence did not differ.
- **Effect:** relative risk (placental parasitaemia) RR: 0.44 (18% vs 40.6%) (95% CI 0.27 - 0.72)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39736161/

### Evaluating artesunate monotherapy and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine as potential antimalarial options for prevaccination radical cures during future malaria vaccine field efficacy trials.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39695728)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-12-18
- **Class:** vaccine; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 1.5-12 years
- **N:** 513
- **Impact:** Both artesunate monotherapy and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine effectively cleared P. falciparum infections, with artesunate showing earlier time to reinfection and higher incidence of clinical malaria but likely minimal interference with vaccine efficacy evaluation.
- **Effect:** proportion free of P. falciparum infection at day 21 87.3% (AS) vs 91.3% (DHAPQ)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39695728/

### Genetic Characterization of Plasmodium falciparum Histidine-Rich Protein 2 Deletions and Their Impact on Malaria Interventions in Odisha, India.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39689364)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Odisha, India
- **Design:** observational genetic characterization study with modeling of intervention impact
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-12-17
- **Class:** IRS; diagnostic
- **Population:** individuals in malaria-endemic villages with subpatent P. falciparum infections
- **Impact:** Mass screening and treatment combined with additional vector control interventions reduced the relative risk of infection for both pfhrp2-deleted and pfhrp2-intact P. falciparum parasites compared with mass screening and treatment by RDT alone.
- **Effect:** adjusted relative risk ratio 0.3 (pfhrp2-deleted); 0.4 (pfhrp2-intact) (95% CI 0.1-1.0 (deleted); 0.2-1.1 (intact))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39689364/

### Safety and efficacy of the blood-stage malaria vaccine RH5.1/Matrix-M in Burkina Faso: interim results of a double-blind, randomised, controlled, phase 2b trial in children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39672183)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2b
- **Place:** Nanoro, Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-12-10
- **Trial period:** 2023
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children aged 5-17 months
- **N:** 361
- **Impact:** RH5.1/Matrix-M vaccine given in a delayed third-dose regimen showed 55% vaccine efficacy against clinical malaria and was safe and well tolerated, while the monthly regimen showed 40% efficacy that was not statistically significant.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy 55% (delayed third-dose regimen); 40% (monthly regimen) (95% CI 20 to 75% (delayed); -3 to 65% (monthly))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months after third vaccination
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39672183/

### Long-lasting residual efficacy of a new indoor residual spraying product, VECTRON™ T500 (broflanilide), against pyrethroid-resistant malaria vectors and its acceptance in a community trial in Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39580476)
- **Journal:** Parasites & vectors
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase III
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** Phase III community trial, non-inferiority
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-11-23
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** Community households in Vallée du Kou; wild and laboratory Anopheles mosquito strains used for bioassay assessment
- **Impact:** VECTRON™ T500 achieved 100% mortality in susceptible and resistant Anopheles strains for 12 months post-spraying, outperforming Actellic® 300CS which induced <80% mortality, and showed non-inferiority to the standard reference product with no reported adverse events.
- **Effect:** Relative risk of mortality (delayed mortality after 72-h holding period) RR = 0.51 (95% CI 95% CI 0.31-0.6)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39580476/

### Azithromycin in severe malaria bacterial co-infection in African children (TABS-PKPD): a phase II randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39506794)
- **Journal:** BMC medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-11-06
- **Trial period:** 2020 to 2022
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** Ugandan children with severe malaria (and non-severe malaria controls)
- **N:** 155
- **Impact:** There was no evidence that azithromycin exposure was associated with reduction in CRP, and no clear dose-dependent difference in outcomes among the 10, 15, and 20 mg/kg arms.
- **Effect:** odds ratio (composite outcome, 20 vs 10 mg/kg) 0.50 (95% CI 0.04-5.79)
- **Follow-up:** 90 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39506794/

### The effect of intermittent preventive treatment for malaria with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine on vaccine-specific responses among schoolchildren in rural Uganda (POPVAC B): a double-blind, randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39424572)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Global health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-11-01
- **Trial period:** 2021 to 2021
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody; chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** schoolchildren aged 9-17 years
- **N:** 341
- **Impact:** Monthly IPT with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine reduced malaria prevalence but did not improve peak vaccine-specific responses compared with placebo.
- **Effect:** geometric mean ratio 1.09 (BCG); 1.19 (yellow fever PRNT50); 1.24 (yellow fever PRNT90); 1.09 (typhoid); 0.72 (HPV-16); 0.71 (HPV-18); 1.22 (tetanus); 0.97 (diphtheria) (95% CI 0.93-1.29 (BCG); 0.91-1.54 (YF PRNT50); 0.97-1.58 (YF PRNT90); 0.81-1.46 (typhoid); 0.44-1.77 (HPV-16); 0.47-1.09 (HPV-18); 0.91-1.62 (tetanus); 0.83-1.13 (diphtheria))
- **Follow-up:** 52 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39424572/

### Intermittent preventive treatment for malaria in pregnancy and infant growth: a mediation analysis of a randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39418986)
- **Journal:** EBioMedicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-10-16
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** infants born to mothers enrolled in a trial of monthly IPTp for malaria in pregnancy
- **N:** 633
- **Impact:** SP increased mean LAZ from birth through 4 months while DP increased mean WLZ from 2 to 8 months compared to each other among infants of multigravidae, with different mediating biological pathways, but no differences were observed among primigravidae.
- **Effect:** mean difference in Z-scores (LAZ and WLZ) SP increased LAZ by 0.18-0.28 Z; DP increased WLZ by 0.11-0.28 Z (95% CI confidence intervals excluded 0)
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39418986/

### Why does malaria transmission continue at high levels despite universal vector control? Quantifying persistent malaria transmission by Anopheles funestus in Western Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39402598)
- **Journal:** Parasites & vectors
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** secondary analysis of a phase III cluster randomized trial; observational cohort with entomological surveillance
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-10-14
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** children aged 1-14 years
- **N:** 1237
- **Impact:** Despite high ITN coverage and use, children in Western Province, Zambia continue to experience substantial residual malaria transmission from Anopheles funestus, sufficient to sustain high malaria burden.
- **Effect:** personal protective efficacy of ITN against An. funestus bites ~90%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 month transmission season
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39402598/

### Evaluation of bio-efficacy of field-aged novel long-lasting insecticidal nets (PBO, chlorfenapyr or pyriproxyfen combined with pyrethroid) against Anopheles gambiae (s.s.) in Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39399651)
- **Journal:** Current research in parasitology & vector-borne diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Misungwi, Tanzania
- **Design:** cluster-randomised controlled trial, durability study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-09-23
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** field-aged long-lasting insecticidal nets used in community households
- **N:** 1950
- **Impact:** Next-generation nets (Interceptor G2, Olyset Plus, Royal Guard) showed higher bio-efficacy than standard pyrethroid-only Interceptor nets against resistant mosquitoes, but this superiority diminished over time and was not sustained for the full three years.
- **Effect:** mortality (72h tunnel test) and sterility rate Interceptor G2 mortality 52-20%; Olyset Plus mortality 84-33%; Royal Guard sterility <10% after 12 months
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 36 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39399651/

### Artemisinin combination therapy at delivery to prevent postpartum malaria: A randomised open-label controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39396742)
- **Journal:** International journal of infectious diseases : IJID : official publication of the International Society for Infectious Diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-10-11
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** healthy postpartum mothers immediately following delivery
- **N:** 183
- **Impact:** A course of artemisinin combination therapy given at delivery roughly halved the incidence of postpartum malaria compared to no treatment over 6 months.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio 0.49 (95% CI 0.27-0.90)
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39396742/

### Efficacy, Immunogenicity and Safety Study of GSK Biologicals' Candidate Malaria Vaccine Evaluating Different Dose Schedules in a Sporozoite Challenge Model in Healthy Malaria-naïve Adults
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03162614)
- **Journal:** JCI insight
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** United States
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2024-10-08
- **Trial period:** 2017-05-24 to 2018-07-09
- **Intervention(s):** RTS,S/AS01E; RTS,S/AS01B; Sporozoite-infected mosquitoes challenge.
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** Healthy malaria-naive adults
- **N:** 154
- **Impact:** Vaccine-induced antibody measures, particularly IgG1 NANP binding magnitude combined with serum NANP and N-junction Ig binding magnitude-avidity composite, were significant predictors of protection against controlled human malaria infection across RTS,S/AS01 trials.
- **Effect:** Odds ratio / ROC AUC Odds ratios 2.6-3.1; ROC AUC combined predictor (95% CI 95% CI for ROC AUC 0.693-0.834)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03162614

### Efficacy and Safety of KAF156 in Combination With LUM-SDF in Adults and Children With Uncomplicated Plasmodium Falciparum Malaria
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03167242)
- **Journal:** Journal of clinical pharmacology
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Gabon, India, Kenya, Mali, Mozambique, Thailand, Uganda, Vietnam
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2024-09-29
- **Trial period:** 2017-08-02 to 2021-06-14
- **Intervention(s):** KAF156; Coartem; Lumefantrine Solid Dispersion Formulation
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adults, adolescents, and children with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria, body weight ≥10 kg
- **N:** 524
- **Impact:** Ganaplacide combined with LUM-SDF was effective and well tolerated for treating uncomplicated falciparum malaria, with drug exposures needed for efficacy achieved using 400 mg ganaplacide plus 960 mg lumefantrine once daily for 3 days under fasted conditions.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03167242

### Comparing approaches for chemoprevention for school-based malaria control in Malawi: an open label, randomized, controlled clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39318787)
- **Journal:** EClinicalMedicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** open-label, randomized, controlled, three-arm, individually randomized clinical trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-09-16
- **Trial period:** 2022 to 2022
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** primary school students aged 5-19 years
- **N:** 746
- **Impact:** Both intermittent preventive treatment (IPT) and intermittent screening and treatment (IST) reduced the prevalence of Plasmodium falciparum infection compared to controls, but only IPT also reduced clinical malaria incidence and anaemia prevalence.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio for Pf infection IPT aOR 0.18; IST aOR 0.27 (95% CI IPT: 0.11-0.27; IST: 0.18-0.40)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6-8 weeks after final intervention (approximately 6 months from start)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39318787/

### Enhancing preventive treatment for malaria in pregnancy: insights from a trial on targeted information transfer.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39304229)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Global health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-10-01
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **Impact:** (no result stated yet)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39304229/

### Mosquitocidal efficacy and pharmacokinetics of single-dose ivermectin versus three-day dose regimen for malaria vector control compared with albendazole and no treatment: An open-label randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39245314)
- **Journal:** International journal of infectious diseases : IJID : official publication of the International Society for Infectious Diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-09-06
- **Class:** MDA; endectocide
- **Population:** healthy volunteers
- **N:** 36
- **Impact:** Single-dose ivermectin showed superior and more durable mosquitocidal effect at 10 days compared with the three-day regimen, while albendazole had no mosquitocidal effect.
- **Effect:** adjusted hazard ratio (mosquito mortality, single-dose ivermectin vs control) 3.91 (95% CI 1.93-7.93)
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39245314/

### Population pharmacokinetics of amodiaquine and piperaquine in African pregnant women with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum infections.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39228131)
- **Journal:** CPT: pharmacometrics & systems pharmacology
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-09-03
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women in second and third trimester with uncomplicated P. falciparum infections
- **N:** 1526
- **Impact:** Exposure to desethylamodiaquine and piperaquine in pregnant women was similar to that in non-pregnant women, indicating no need for dose adjustment.
- **Effect:** relative bioavailability change with gestational age 1.25% increase per week
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** day 7
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39228131/

### The bioefficacy of a novel VECTRON™ T500 indoor residual spray formulation in an experimental huts trial against Anopheles gambiae s.l. populations.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39218380)
- **Journal:** Acta tropica
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Design:** comparative experimental hut trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-08-31
- **Trial period:** June 2022 to December 2022
- **Class:** IRS
- **Population:** laboratory-reared and wild Anopheles gambiae s.l. mosquitoes
- **N:** 3840
- **Impact:** VECTRON T500 showed extended efficacy (mortality over 80% for 6 months) against malaria vector mosquitoes on cement and mud walls, performing similarly to the positive control Klypson 500 WG.
- **Effect:** mortality rate 97.8-98.1% (laboratory) and 83.2-95.0% (wild) with VECTRON T500; 89.6-99.0% (wild) and 99.0-99.3% (laboratory) with Klypson 500 WG
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39218380/

### Safety, Immunogenicity, and Protective Efficacy of Radiation Attenuated Plasmodium Falciparum NF54 Sporozoites (PfSPZ Vaccine) During Malaria Transmission Season in Healthy African Adult Women of Childbearing Potential in Mali
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03989102)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2024-08-14
- **Trial period:** 2019-07-03 to 2022-04-05
- **Intervention(s):** PfSPZ Vaccine; Normal Saline
- **Class:** vaccine; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** MLSPZV3: adults aged 18-35 years; MLSPZV4: non-pregnant women aged 18-38 years anticipating conception within a year of enrolment
- **N:** 324
- **Impact:** PfSPZ Vaccine was safe and well tolerated, and in MLSPZV4 (with presumptive antimalarial treatment before dose one) showed significant efficacy against P falciparum parasitaemia, clinical malaria, and pregnancy malaria over two transmission seasons, whereas in MLSPZV3 (without presumptive antimalarial pretreatment before dose one) it showed no efficacy.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy against parasitaemia MLSPZV4: 41% (9x10^5, year1), 61% (9x10^5, year2), 54% (1.8x10^6, year1), 45% (1.8x10^6, year2); MLSPZV3: 27% (4-week year1), 42% (4-week year2), 16% (16-week year1), -14% (16-week year2) (95% CI MLSPZV4: 15-59, 36-77, 34-69, 13-65; MLSPZV3: -18 to 55, -5 to 68, -34 to 48, -95 to 33)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 malaria transmission seasons (2 years)
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03989102

### Mosquito Shield™, a transfluthrin passive emanator, protects against pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae sensu lato in central Benin.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39085888)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-07-31
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; spatial repellent
- **Population:** households in Ganhoua village, Zakpota District, central Benin
- **N:** 30
- **Impact:** Mosquito Shield™, a transfluthrin passive emanator, significantly reduced human landing rates of pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae s.l. compared to placebo over the product's lifespan.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 34.2% (95% CI 22.1-44.4%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 32 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39085888/

### Plasmodium falciparum infection status in children less than 10 years old under seasonal malaria chemoprevention and risk of clinical malaria in the Koulikoro health district, Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (39070642)
- **Journal:** Research square
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** prospective cohort, observational
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-07-18
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2020
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under 10 years old receiving seasonal malaria chemoprevention
- **N:** 2178
- **Impact:** Children with asymptomatic P. falciparum infection at the time of SMC drug intake had a significantly higher risk of subsequent clinical malaria compared to uninfected children.
- **Effect:** relative risk (RR) RR=3.05 (2019), RR=1.43 (2020) (95% CI 2.04-4.72 (2019); 1.04-1.97 (2020))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 4 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39070642/

### Efficacy of Interceptor G2, Royal Guard and PermaNet 3.0 against pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae s.l. from Za-Kpota, southern Benin: an experimental hut trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38992693)
- **Journal:** Parasites & vectors
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** experimental hut trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-07-11
- **Trial period:** 2021 to 2021
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** wild free-flying Anopheles gambiae mosquitoes
- **Impact:** Interceptor G2 and Royal Guard nets showed superior efficacy against pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae compared to standard PermaNet 2.0, while PermaNet 3.0 remained effective in this resistant setting.
- **Effect:** 72-h mortality rate 90.11% (Interceptor G2) vs 56.04% (PermaNet 2.0)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 72 hours post-exposure
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38992693/

### A randomised trial of malaria vaccine R21/Matrix-M™ with and without antimalarial drugs in Thai adults.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38971837)
- **Journal:** NPJ vaccines
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Thailand
- **Design:** randomised, open-label
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-07-06
- **Class:** vaccine; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** healthy Thai adult volunteers
- **N:** 120
- **Impact:** The R21/Matrix-M vaccine was well tolerated alone and in combination with antimalarials, with no significant differences in vaccine immunogenicity or piperaquine pharmacokinetics between groups.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38971837/

### Extension of efficacy range for targeted malaria-elimination interventions due to spillover effects.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38965434)
- **Journal:** Nature medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Namibia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-07-04
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT; IRS
- **Population:** residents living near confirmed malaria index cases
- **Impact:** Combined chemoprevention and indoor residual spraying produced no clear direct effect on recipients but substantially reduced malaria incidence, prevalence and seroprevalence among non-recipients living up to 3 km away, indicating strong spillover effects.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in incidence/prevalence/seroprevalence 43% reduction in incidence (within 1 km); 79% reduction in infection prevalence (within 3 km); 34% reduction in seroprevalence (within 3 km) (95% CI 20-59% (incidence); 6-95% (prevalence); 20-45% (seroprevalence))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38965434/

### A cluster-randomized trial of client and provider directed financial interventions to align incentives with appropriate case management in private medicine retailers: Results of the TESTsmART trial in Lagos, Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38954701)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria, Lagos
- **Design:** cluster-randomized trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-07-02
- **Class:** PMC; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** clients seeking care for fever/malaria at private medicine retailers, including children with fever
- **N:** 48
- **Impact:** The intervention offering free ACTs to test-positive clients and provider incentives did not improve testing rates or targeting of ACTs to test-positive clients compared to the control arm, though overall mRDT testing reduced ACT overconsumption among test-negative clients.
- **Effect:** proportion of test-negative clients purchasing an ACT 26% (intervention/test-negative) vs 58% (untested clients)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38954701/

### Pathways through which intermittent preventive treatment for malaria in pregnancy influences child growth faltering: a mediation analysis.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38947035)
- **Journal:** medRxiv : the preprint server for health sciences
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized trial, mediation analysis
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-06-10
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** infants born to mothers enrolled in a randomized trial of IPTp; stratified by gravidity
- **N:** 633
- **Impact:** Among infants of multigravidae, SP increased LAZ from birth through 4 months compared to DP, while DP increased WLZ from 2-8 months compared to SP, with distinct mediating pathways for each regimen; no differences were observed among primigravidae.
- **Effect:** difference in Z-scores SP increased LAZ by 0.18-0.28 Z; DP increased WLZ by 0.11-0.28 Z
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38947035/

### Effectiveness, Safety, and Acceptability of Primaquine Mass Drug Administration in Low-Endemicity Areas in Southern Thailand: Proof-of-Concept Study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38922648)
- **Journal:** JMIR public health and surveillance
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Thailand
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-06-26
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** residents of low P. vivax transmission areas in southern Thailand
- **N:** 1536
- **Impact:** Primaquine mass drug administration was safe, well-tolerated, and highly accepted, but did not show a statistically significant impact on reducing P. vivax transmission compared to control.
- **Effect:** proportion of P. vivax infections per 1000 population pMDA 8.4 vs control 5.6 at month 3 post-pMDA (95% CI 95% CI 4.6-15.1 (pMDA), 95% CI 2.6-11.5 (control))
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 3 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38922648/

### LLIN Evaluation in Uganda Project (LLINEUP)-effects of a vector control trial on Plasmodium infection prevalence and genotypic markers of insecticide resistance in Anopheles vectors from 48 districts of Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38914669)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** cluster-randomised trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-06-24
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2019
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households/vector populations across 104 health sub-districts, cross-sectional entomological surveys
- **N:** 5395
- **Impact:** PBO LLINs did not reduce the proportion of infective mosquitoes compared to conventional LLINs, and pyrethroid resistance markers increased over time regardless of net type, suggesting persistence of transmission potential and evolution of resistance.
- **Effect:** proportion of Plasmodium-infected mosquitoes no significant change over time
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38914669/

### Aggressive Antipyretics for Fever Reduction in CNS Malaria
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03399318)
- **Journal:** JAMA neurology
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Malawi, Zambia
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2024-08-01
- **Trial period:** 2019-01-07 to 2022-12-02
- **Intervention(s):** Acetaminophen; Ibuprofen; placebo for acetaminophen; placebo for ibuprofen
- **Population:** children aged 2 to 11 years with CNS malaria
- **N:** 256
- **Impact:** Aggressive antipyretic therapy with scheduled acetaminophen and ibuprofen significantly reduced maximum temperature and lowered odds of multiple or prolonged seizures compared with usual care, without affecting parasite clearance.
- **Effect:** difference in Tmax (°C) -0.62 (95% CI -0.82 to -0.42)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 72 hours
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03399318

### Expanding community case management of malaria to all ages can improve universal access to malaria diagnosis and treatment: results from a cluster randomized trial in Madagascar.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38853263)
- **Journal:** BMC medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Madagascar
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-06-10
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2021
- **Population:** rural community members of all ages, including children under 5 and individuals 6-13 years and older, in Farafangana District
- **N:** 30
- **Impact:** Expanding community case management of malaria to all ages improved access to malaria diagnosis and treatment, particularly for individuals over 5 years and those living further from health centers, though significance was mainly seen in health system data.
- **Effect:** rate ratio RR for RDTs done in 6-13-year-olds = 1.65; RR per km distance = 1.21 (95% CI 1.45-1.87; 1.19-1.23)
- **Follow-up:** 36 months (2019-2021)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38853263/

### Trimethoprim-Sulfamethoxazole Plus Azithromycin to Prevent Malaria and Sexually Transmitted Infections in Pregnant Women With HIV (PREMISE): A Randomized, Double-Masked, Placebo-Controlled, Phase IIB Clinical Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38807754)
- **Journal:** Open forum infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Cameroon
- **Design:** randomized, double-masked, placebo-controlled, phase IIB clinical trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-05-08
- **Trial period:** 2018 to 2020
- **Population:** pregnant women with HIV at gestational age <28 weeks
- **N:** 308
- **Impact:** Adding monthly azithromycin to daily TMP-SMX prophylaxis did not reduce the risk of malaria or bacterial STI at delivery in pregnant women with HIV in Cameroon.
- **Effect:** relative risk 1.24 (malaria); 0.72 (STI) (95% CI .71-2.16 (malaria); .26-2.03 (STI))
- **Follow-up:** through delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38807754/

### Intermittent preventive treatment with sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine but not dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine modulates the relationship between inflammatory markers and adverse pregnancy outcomes in Malawi.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38753813)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial, secondary analysis, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-05-16
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 1319
- **Impact:** SP, but not DP-based regimens, was associated with a reduced risk of adverse birth outcomes among women with elevated AGP, suggesting SP modifies the relationship between inflammation and pregnancy outcomes.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk ratio (aRR) 1.95 (interaction: DP/DP+AZ vs SP for AGP-adverse outcome association) (95% CI 1.21-3.13)
- **Follow-up:** from enrollment (16-28 gestation weeks) to delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38753813/

### Efficacy, Safety and Immunogenicity Study of GSK Biologicals' Candidate Malaria Vaccine (SB257049) Evaluating Schedules With or Without Fractional Doses, Early Dose 4 and Yearly Doses, in Children 5-17 Months of Age
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03276962)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Ghana, Kenya
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2024-05-06
- **Trial period:** 2017-09-28 to 2019-11-04
- **Intervention(s):** RTS,S/AS01E (Full dose); RTS,S/AS01E (1/5th dose); Rabies vaccine
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children aged 5-17 months
- **N:** 1500
- **Impact:** All four RTS,S/AS01E dosing regimens significantly and similarly reduced malaria infections compared with a rabies control vaccine, with greater efficacy in children infected at the time of first vaccination.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy (1 - hazard ratio) against first new infection 25-43% (95% CI 9-53 (95% CI union); infected subgroup 68% (50-80); uninfected subgroup 37% (23-48))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 20 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03276962

### Persistent and multiclonal malaria parasite dynamics despite extended artemether-lumefantrine treatment in children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38714692)
- **Journal:** Nature communications
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-05-07
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children with and without HIV
- **N:** 303
- **Impact:** Extended 5-day artemether-lumefantrine treatment significantly lowered the risk of recurrent ring-stage parasitemia compared to the standard 3-day regimen, though multiclonal parasite dynamics persisted despite effective treatment.
- **Effect:** risk of recurrent ring-stage parasitemia significantly lower with 5-day regimen
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38714692/

### Innate immune activation restricts priming and protective efficacy of the radiation-attenuated PfSPZ malaria vaccine.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38687615)
- **Journal:** JCI insight
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-04-30
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** African infants
- **Impact:** Prevaccination innate immune activation and myeloid signatures correlated with protection in placebo controls but with susceptibility to parasitemia in infants receiving the highest, most protective dose of PfSPZ vaccine, suggesting innate stimulation both protects against infection and dampens the CD8+ T cell response induced by whole-sporozoite vaccination.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38687615/

### Measuring effects of ivermectin-treated cattle on potential malaria vectors in Vietnam: A cluster-randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38683855)
- **Journal:** PLoS neglected tropical diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Vietnam
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-04-29
- **Class:** endectocide
- **Population:** local zebu cattle in six rural villages (vector surveillance study)
- **N:** 6
- **Impact:** Treating cattle with ivermectin did not significantly reduce total anopheline mosquito captures compared to untreated control villages.
- **Effect:** difference-in-differences in total Anopheles captures not statistically significant reduction
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 6 nights before and 6 nights after intervention
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38683855/

### Subcutaneous Administration of a Monoclonal Antibody to Prevent Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38669354)
- **Journal:** The New England journal of medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-04-26
- **Class:** monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** children 6 to 10 years of age
- **N:** 225
- **Impact:** Subcutaneous administration of the monoclonal antibody L9LS significantly protected children against P. falciparum infection and clinical malaria over a 6-month malaria season compared with placebo.
- **Effect:** efficacy against P. falciparum infection 66% (150-mg dose), 70% (300-mg dose) (95% CI 45 to 79 (150-mg); 50 to 82 (300-mg))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 24 weeks (6 months)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38669354/

### Monthly Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine During Pregnancy Prevents Febrile Respiratory Illnesses: A Secondary Analysis of a Malaria Chemoprevention Trial in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38585183)
- **Journal:** Open forum infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** secondary analysis of a randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-03-13
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women without HIV infection, enrolled between 12 and 20 gestational weeks
- **N:** 654
- **Impact:** Monthly IPTp-SP reduced incident nonmalarial febrile illnesses, especially respiratory illnesses, compared to IPTp-DP, and was associated with higher birthweight for gestational age.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio 0.74 (all NMFIs); 0.69 (respiratory NMFIs) (95% CI 0.58-0.95 (all NMFIs); 0.48-1.00 (respiratory NMFIs))
- **Follow-up:** from enrollment (12-20 gestational weeks) through delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38585183/

### Feasibility, safety, and impact of the RTS,S/AS01E malaria vaccine when implemented through national immunisation programmes: evaluation of cluster-randomised introduction of the vaccine in Ghana, Kenya, and Malawi.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38583454)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 4
- **Place:** Ghana, Kenya, Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-04-04
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2021
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children aged 1-59 months, vaccine eligible children aged 5-9 months for primary doses
- **N:** 158
- **Impact:** RTS,S introduction was associated with a 32% reduction in hospital admissions for severe malaria and a 9% reduction in all-cause mortality (excluding injury), with no evidence of previously observed safety signals such as excess meningitis, cerebral malaria, or excess female mortality.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio / percent reduction 32% reduction in severe malaria hospital admission; 9% reduction in all-cause mortality (95% CI 5-51% (severe malaria); 0-18% (mortality))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 24 months (interim analysis within a planned 4-year evaluation)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38583454/

### Efficacy of artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine and prevalence of molecular markers of anti-malarial drug resistance in children in Togo in 2021.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38570791)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Togo
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-04-03
- **Trial period:** 2021 to 2022
- **Class:** SMC; IPTp; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 357
- **Impact:** Both artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine were highly effective against uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Togo, with high cure rates and no validated artemisinin resistance markers detected.
- **Effect:** PCR-adjusted cure rate (ACPR) 96.4-100% (95% CI 89.1-100)
- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Insecticide-treated bed nets and residual indoor spraying reduce malaria in areas with low transmission: a reanalysis of the Maltrials study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38439099)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ethiopia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-03-04
- **Trial period:** 2014
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** households and persons residing in study clusters
- **N:** 34548
- **Impact:** Combined ITN plus IRS and ITN alone significantly reduced malaria incidence compared to IRS alone or routine control, with a synergistic effect seen when ITN and IRS were combined.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio ITN+IRS 0.63; ITN alone 0.78; IRS alone 0.90
- **Follow-up:** 121 weeks

### Pyronaridine Artesunate 3:1 Granule Formulation vs. Coartem© Crushed Tablets in P. Falciparum Malaria Pediatric Patients
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT00541385)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** registration

- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Côte d’Ivoire, Democratic Republic of the Congo, Gabon, Kenya, Mali, Mozambique, Philippines

- **Impact:** Pyronaridine-artesunate pediatric granules were well tolerated and showed clinical efficacy comparable to artemether-lumefantrine, with high Day 28 ACPR rates in trials and effectiveness confirmed in real-world use.
- **Effect:** Day 28 PCR-adjusted ACPR (per-protocol) 97.1% and 100% for PA vs 98.8% and 98.4% for AL (95% CI 94.6-98.6; 99.3-100 (PA); 95.7-99.9; 95.5-99.7 (AL))

- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT04428385)

- **Population:** Clients with fever seeking care at Private Medicine Retailers, including children
- **N:** 2205
- **Impact:** RDT testing helped target ACTs to confirmed malaria cases, but the conditional ACT subsidy and testing incentive did not further improve testing rates or appropriate ACT dispensing compared to control.

### Mass Azithromycin Distribution to Prevent Child Mortality in Burkina Faso: The CHAT Randomized Clinical Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38349371)

- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-02-01
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2023
- **Class:** SMC
- **Population:** children aged 1 to 59 months
- **N:** 68246
- **Impact:** Mortality in children was lower with biannual mass azithromycin distribution alongside seasonal malaria chemoprevention, but the difference was not statistically significant.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio for mortality 0.82 (95% CI 0.67-1.02)
- **Follow-up:** 3 years (August 2019 to February 2023)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38349371/

### Safety and efficacy of malaria vaccine candidate R21/Matrix-M in African children: a multicentre, double-blind, randomised, phase 3 trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38310910)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Kenya, Mali, Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-02-01
- **Trial period:** 2021
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** children aged 5-36 months
- **N:** 4878
- **Impact:** R21/Matrix-M vaccine was well tolerated and provided high protective efficacy against clinical malaria in African children at both seasonal and standard transmission sites over 12 months.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy against time to first clinical malaria episode 75% (seasonal sites), 68% (standard sites) (95% CI 71-79 (seasonal), 61-74 (standard))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12 months (up to 18 months at seasonal sites)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38310910/

### Impact of a spatial repellent intervention on Anopheles kdr insecticide resistance allele in Sumba, Indonesia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38254131)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Indonesia (Sumba Island)
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, placebo-controlled
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-01-22
- **Trial period:** 2015 to 2018
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; spatial repellent
- **Population:** Anopheles mosquito populations (entomological study)
- **Impact:** A statistically significant increase in kdr mutant allele frequency occurred over the 3-year study in both spatial repellent-treated and placebo clusters, with unclear attribution to the SR intervention itself.
- **Effect:** change in kdr allele frequency increase in both arms
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 3 years (June 2015 to August 2018)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38254131/

### Artemisinin-naphthoquine plus lower-dose primaquine to treat and prevent recurrence of Plasmodium vivax malaria: an open-label randomized and non-inferiority trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38254128)
- **Journal:** Parasites & vectors
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** China-Myanmar border, Myanmar
- **Design:** open-label, randomized, non-inferiority, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-01-22
- **Trial period:** 2017
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with P. vivax malaria
- **N:** 288
- **Impact:** ANPQ3 showed similar radical cure efficacy to CQPQ14, cleared fever and parasites faster, was safer with no severe adverse effects, and had better patient adherence.
- **Effect:** difference in recurrence-free patients (percentage points) 3.5 (95% CI -8.6 to 1.5)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 365 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38254128/

### PvDBPII elicits multiple antibody-mediated mechanisms that reduce growth in a Plasmodium vivax challenge trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38184681)
- **Journal:** NPJ vaccines
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Design:** controlled human malaria infection, heterologous challenge trial, delayed dosing regimen
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-01-06
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** healthy adult volunteers immunized with PvDBPII vaccine and challenged with P. vivax
- **Impact:** PvDBPII immunization with Matrix-M adjuvant elicited polyfunctional antibody responses, including antibody titre, receptor-binding inhibitory titre, complement C1q and Fc gamma receptor binding, that correlated with reduced parasite multiplication rate and cross-reacted with diverse P. vivax variants.
- **Effect:** parasite multiplication rate (PMR) reduction
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38184681/

### The impact of pyrethroid-pyriproxyfen and pyrethroid-chlorfenapyr long-lasting insecticidal nets on density of primary malaria vectors Anopheles gambiae s.s. and Anopheles coluzzii in Benin: a secondary analysis of a cluster randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38178161)
- **Journal:** Parasites & vectors
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2024-01-04
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** mosquito vector populations (An. gambiae s.s. and An. coluzzii) collected via human landing catches in study clusters
- **N:** 60
- **Impact:** Pyrethroid-pyriproxyfen and pyrethroid-chlorfenapyr LLINs did not significantly reduce indoor or outdoor densities of An. gambiae s.s. or An. coluzzii compared to pyrethroid-only LLINs in Benin.
- **Effect:** density ratio (DR) DR = 0.62 (pyriproxyfen, An. coluzzii); DR = 0.56 (chlorfenapyr, An. coluzzii); DR = 0.52 (pyriproxyfen, An. gambiae s.s.); DR = 0.53 (chlorfenapyr, An. gambiae s.s.) (95% CI 0.21-1.77; 0.19-1.62; 0.18-1.46; 0.19-1.46)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 2 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38178161/

### Factors associated with malaria infection among children after distribution of PBO-pyrethroid synergist-treated nets and indoor residual spraying in north-western Tanzania.

- **Impact:** Use of PBO synergist LLINs reduced the risk of malaria infection compared to standard LLINs, while other factors such as male sex, older age, open eaves, no IRS, low education and low socioeconomic status increased risk.

- **N:** 8560
- **Impact:** Community health workers effectively supported follow-up in a phase IIIb/IV cohort event monitoring study assessing real-world effectiveness and safety of pyronaridine-artesunate in African settings.

### Factors affecting haemoglobin dynamics in African children with acute uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria treated with single low-dose primaquine or placebo.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37858129)
- **Journal:** BMC medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Democratic Republic of the Congo, Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-10-20
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6 months-11 years with acute uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria and day 0 Hb ≥ 6 g/dL
- **N:** 1137
- **Impact:** Single low-dose primaquine did not adversely affect posttreatment haemoglobin changes, and G6PD-deficient children had favourable Hb recovery, supporting SLDPQ deployment without G6PD screening in Africa.
- **Effect:** association with Hb recovery (D42 Hb > D0 Hb) not significantly affected by SLDPQ; G6PDd status associated with favourable Hb recovery
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37858129/

### Community evaluation of VECTRON™ T500, a broflanilide insecticide, for indoor residual spraying for malaria vector control in central Benin; a two arm non-inferiority cluster randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37857762)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-10-19
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** Household inhabitants in communities with pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae s.l., surveyed via human landing catches
- **N:** 16
- **Impact:** VECTRON™ T500 was non-inferior to Fludora® Fusion in reducing human biting rates of pyrethroid-resistant malaria vectors and showed prolonged residual efficacy over 24 months with high acceptability.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio (IRR) for human biting rate IRR 0.54 (indoors), IRR 0.75 (outdoors) (95% CI 0.22-1.35 (indoors), 0.41-1.38 (outdoors))

### Efficacy and safety of ivermectin for the treatment of Plasmodium falciparum infections in asymptomatic male and female Gabonese adults - a pilot randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled single-centre phase Ib/IIa clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37839134)

- **Place:** Gabon

- **Status:** published

- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2020
- **Class:** endectocide
- **Population:** asymptomatic male and female Gabonese adults with P. falciparum parasitaemia (200-5000 parasites/μl)
- **N:** 49
- **Impact:** Ivermectin at doses up to 300 μg/kg once daily for three days was well tolerated but did not significantly reduce time to 90% parasite reduction compared to placebo.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio for time to 90% parasite reduction 1.38 (95% CI 0.64 to 2.97)

### Targeted malaria elimination interventions reduce Plasmodium falciparum infections up to 3 kilometers away.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37790419)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Namibia
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, factorial design, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-11-30
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT; IRS
- **Population:** individuals near confirmed malaria index cases, including intervention recipients and non-recipients (nearby residents)
- **Impact:** The combined chemoprevention and vector control intervention reduced malaria incidence, infection prevalence, and seroprevalence among non-recipients up to 3 km from index cases.
- **Effect:** percent reduction in incidence 43% (95% CI 20-59%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37790419/

### A cluster-randomized trial of client and provider-directed financial interventions to align incentives with appropriate case management in retail medicine outlets: results of the TESTsmART Trial in western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37745516)
- **Journal:** medRxiv : the preprint server for health sciences
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Kenya

- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-09-15
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** clients of retail medicine outlets seeking malaria testing/treatment
- **N:** 39
- **Impact:** Client-directed and combined client+provider-directed financial incentive interventions did not significantly improve testing rates or targeting of ACTs to test-positive clients compared to control, though overall mRDT use reduced ACT overconsumption among untested/test-negative clients.
- **Effect:** risk difference RD=0.01 (dispensing of ACTs to test-positive clients); RD=0.09 (testing rates) (95% CI -0.14 to 0.16 (dispensing); -0.08 to 0.26 (testing rates))

### Durability of the deltamethrin-treated polypropylene long-lasting net LifeNet® in a pyrethroid resistance area in south western Benin: A phase III trial.

- **Impact:** LifeNet® LLINs met WHO criteria for bio-efficacy throughout the 36-month study period, showed lower physical integrity and insecticide retention over time, and were well accepted by the population though with more reported side effects than PermaNet®.
- **Effect:** proportion of LLINs meeting WHO efficacy criteria at 36 months 100% of both LifeNet® and PermaNet® met WHO efficacy criteria after 36 months

### Larviciding intervention targeting malaria vectors also affects Culex mosquito distribution in the city of Yaoundé, Cameroon.

- **Impact:** Larviciding intervention targeting malaria vectors led to a 69% reduction in aquatic habitats with Culex larvae and a 36.65% reduction in adult Culex densities in houses, with no change in Culex species composition.

- **Impact:** Both DHA-PQ and SP-AQ significantly reduced the incidence of clinical malaria compared with control, with DHA-PQ causing fewer adverse events than SP-AQ.

### Efficacy of the spatial repellent product Mosquito Shield™ against wild pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles arabiensis in south-eastern Tanzania.

- **Impact:** Mosquito Shield spatial repellent significantly reduced mosquito landings and blood-feeding by wild pyrethroid-resistant An. arabiensis over one month compared to control huts.
- **Effect:** Landing inhibition and blood-feeding inhibition (IRR) Landing inhibition 70%; blood-feeding inhibition 69% [IRR 0.30 and 0.31] (95% CI Landing 57-78% (IRR 0.22-0.43); blood-feeding 56-79% (IRR 0.21-0.44))

- **N:** 352
- **Impact:** Both artesunate+amodiaquine and artemether+lumefantrine were safe and highly efficacious for treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in children, with ACPR rates of 100% and 96% (97.7% PCR-corrected) respectively.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected ACPR rate 100% (ASAQ), 97.7% (AL) (95% CI 98.8-100% (ASAQ); 95.4-100% (AL))

### Seasonal Malaria Vaccination (RTS,S/AS01) and Seasonal Malaria Chemoprevention (SP/AQ) Extension Study
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT04319380)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Mali
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2023-08-22
- **Trial period:** 2020-04-01 to 2022-03-31
- **Intervention(s):** Tetanus/diphtheria toxoids; SMC with SP+AQ; RTS,S/AS01; SMC placebo
- **Class:** vaccine; SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children enrolled at age 5-17 months, followed to age 5 years
- **N:** 5098
- **Impact:** Combining seasonal RTS,S/AS01E vaccination with seasonal malaria chemoprevention sustained substantial protection against clinical malaria over 5 years compared with either intervention alone, with no safety signals detected.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 57.7% vs SMC alone; 59.0% vs RTS,S/AS01E alone (95% CI 53.3 to 61.7 (vs SMC); 54.7 to 62.8 (vs RTS,S/AS01E))

### Efficacy and Safety Study of Tafenoquine (TQ) Co-administered With Dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine (DHA-PQP) for the Radical Cure of Plasmodium Vivax (P. Vivax) Malaria
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT02802501)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Indonesia
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2023-05-23
- **Trial period:** 2018-04-08 to 2019-08-19
- **Intervention(s):** Tafenoquine; Matched-Placebo for Tafenoquine; Primaquine; Matched-Placebo for Primaquine; Dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine (DHA-PQP); ACT plus PQ (Rescue medication); PQ (End of study treatment)
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase-normal Indonesian soldiers with microscopically confirmed P vivax malaria
- **N:** 150
- **Impact:** Tafenoquine plus dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine was statistically superior to dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine alone for radical cure of P vivax malaria, but the improvement in relapse-free efficacy was not clinically meaningful.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio for relapse (6-month relapse-free efficacy) 0.44 (11% vs 21% relapse-free efficacy) (95% CI 0.29-0.69)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02802501

### Differential impact of dual-active ingredient long-lasting insecticidal nets on primary malaria vectors: a secondary analysis of a 3-year, single-blind, cluster-randomised controlled trial in rural Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37164513)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Planetary health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-05-01
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2021
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households in rural Misungwi district, indoor mosquito collections
- **N:** 84
- **Impact:** Chlorfenapyr pyrethroid LLINs were the most effective dual active-ingredient net against the main malaria vector An funestus sl over 3 years, significantly reducing vector density and malaria transmission intensity, while none of the dual active-ingredient LLINs controlled An arabiensis.
- **Effect:** adjusted density ratio (aDR) 0.26 (chlorfenapyr pyrethroid LLIN vs pyrethroid LLIN for An funestus density) (95% CI 0.17-0.14)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 3 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37164513/

### Housing modification for malaria control: impact of a "lethal house lure" intervention on malaria infection prevalence in a cluster randomised control trial in Côte d'Ivoire.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37143050)
- **Journal:** BMC medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Côte d'Ivoire
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-05-04
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** residents of villages in malaria-endemic area
- **N:** 40
- **Impact:** House screening and EaveTubes (SET) substantially reduced malaria infection prevalence 18 months after installation compared to control villages.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 0.57 (95% CI 0.45-0.71)
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37143050/

- **Impact:** (no result stated yet)
