### Predicting adherence to postdischarge malaria chemoprevention in Malawian pre-school children: A prognostic multivariable analysis.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37068085)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** cluster randomized implementation trial, prognostic multivariable analysis
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-04-17
- **Class:** PDMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pre-school children recently discharged from hospital following recovery from severe anemia
- **N:** 357
- **Impact:** No conclusive set of predictors for adherence to PDMC was identified, though children with four or more malaria infections in the past year had reduced adherence.
- **Effect:** relative risk
- **Follow-up:** 3 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37068085/

### Therapeutic response to artemisinin combination therapies among individuals with Plasmodium falciparum single infection vs mixed Plasmodium species infections: a retrospective posthoc analysis in Kisumu County, western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (37061211)
- **Journal:** International journal of infectious diseases : IJID : official publication of the International Society for Infectious Diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Kisumu County, Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-04-14
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** individuals with Plasmodium falciparum single or mixed species infections
- **N:** 173
- **Impact:** Artesunate-mefloquine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine had faster parasite clearance than artemether-lumefantrine, and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine provided the longest duration of protection against reinfection, with no differences in clearance based on species composition.
- **Effect:** mean parasite clearance time 16.4 hours (artesunate-mefloquine), 17.6 hours (dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine), 21.0 hours (artemether-lumefantrine)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37061211/

### Safety and Efficacy of NF135 CPS Immunization
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03813108)
- **Journal:** BMC medicine
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Netherlands
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, NA
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2023-04-07
- **Trial period:** 2019-04-01 to 2021-02-01
- **Intervention(s):** CPS-immunization; malaria challenge infection, P. falciparum NF135.C10; malaria challenge infection, P. falciparum NF54; CPS-immunization (A/L); Atovaquone / Proguanil Oral Tablet [Malarone]
- **Class:** vaccine; chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** healthy, malaria-naïve adults
- **N:** 43
- **Impact:** NF135 CPS immunization was poorly tolerated, frequently requiring rescue treatment for breakthrough infections, and induced only modest sterile protection (29%) against homologous controlled human malaria infection challenge.
- **Effect:** sterile protective efficacy 29% (5/17)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 19 weeks
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03813108

### Protective efficacy of holed and aging PBO-pyrethroid synergist-treated nets on malaria infection prevalence in north-western Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36962517)
- **Journal:** PLOS global public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** cohort study, cross-sectional surveys
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-10-17
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households and residents in malaria-endemic communities
- **N:** 757
- **Impact:** PBO LLINs remained more protective against malaria infection than standard pyrethroid LLINs as both types aged over 3 years, despite similar functional survival times and net degradation.
- **Effect:** median functional survival time 1.6 years (PBO) vs 1.9 years (standard) (95% CI 95% CI 1.38-1.87 (PBO); 95% CI 1.67-2.06 (standard))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 36 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36962517/

### Association between malaria and household air pollution interventions in a predominantly rural area of Ghana.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36959655)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-03-23
- **Class:** spatial repellent
- **Population:** pregnant women and their infants (maternal-infant pairs)
- **N:** 1414
- **Impact:** Household air pollution interventions (LPG or Biolite cookstoves) were not associated with an increased risk of placental malaria or malaria parasitaemia in infants during their first year of life compared to control.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 0.88 (95% CI 0.59-1.30)
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36959655/

### Effects of maternal antenatal treatment with two doses of azithromycin added to monthly sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine for the prevention of low birth weight in Burkina Faso: an open-label randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36932435)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-03-17
- **Trial period:** 2018
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women, 16 to 35 years of age, 12 to 24 weeks of gestation
- **N:** 992
- **Impact:** Adding two doses of azithromycin to monthly sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine did not significantly reduce the incidence of low birth weight compared to sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine alone.
- **Effect:** incidence of low birth weight 8.7% (SPAZ) vs 9.4% (SP) (95% CI 0.17-3.35)
- **Follow-up:** until birth (delivery outcome assessment)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36932435/

### Interplay among malnutrition, chemoprevention, and the risk of malaria in young Ugandan children: Longitudinal pharmacodynamic and growth analysis.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36919202)
- **Journal:** CPT: pharmacometrics & systems pharmacology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-03-14
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** HIV-exposed and HIV-unexposed young children, followed from age 6-24 months on chemoprevention and then off chemoprevention until age 36 months
- **N:** 579
- **Impact:** Stunted children had a higher malaria hazard, dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine chemoprevention reduced malaria hazard compared to no chemoprevention, and each malaria episode was associated with reduced growth rate for height.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio 0.65 (DP vs no chemoprevention); 2.1 (stunted vs non-stunted among DP recipients); 17% increased hazard for stunted vs ZHA=0 (95% CI 0.41-0.97; 1.6-3.0; 10-23%)
- **Follow-up:** from age 6 to 36 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36919202/

### Ultra-short course, high-dose primaquine to prevent Plasmodium vivax infection following uncomplicated pediatric malaria: A randomized, open-label, non-inferiority trial of early versus delayed treatment.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36906121)
- **Journal:** International journal of infectious diseases : IJID : official publication of the International Society for Infectious Diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-03-09
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 0.5 to 12 years with uncomplicated malaria and normal G6PD activity
- **N:** 219
- **Impact:** Early ultra-short high-dose primaquine was non-inferior to delayed treatment in preventing P. vivax infection at day 42, though associated with more abdominal pain and vomiting, with no severe adverse events.
- **Effect:** risk difference in P. vivax parasitemia at day 42 -5.4% (95% CI -13.7 to 2.8)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 84 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36906121/

### The economic impacts of house screening against malaria transmission: Experimental evidence from eastern Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36827904)
- **Journal:** Social science & medicine (1982)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** eastern Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-02-19
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2020
- **Population:** households (adults and children) in rural and peri-urban Zambia
- **N:** 800
- **Impact:** House screening reduced malaria prevalence, sick days, and episodes, and increased labor supply and household income, with private benefits exceeding costs.
- **Effect:** reduction in malaria prevalence and increase in labor supply/income
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36827904/

### Effects of anti-malarial prophylaxes on maternal transfer of Immunoglobulin-G (IgG) and association to immunity against Plasmodium falciparum infections among children in a Ugandan birth cohort.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36812236)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-02-22
- **Class:** monoclonal antibody; IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women and their infants (birth cohort)
- **N:** 637
- **Impact:** Malaria prophylaxis with SP or DP in pregnancy did not affect cord blood antibody levels against P. falciparum antigens, and higher cord IgG levels against several antigens were associated with increased, not decreased, risk of malaria in the first year of life.
- **Effect:** Adjusted Hazard Ratio (AHR) 1.09-1.83 across antigens (Rh4.2, PfSEA, Etramp5Ag1, AMA1, GLURP, EBA175) (95% CI 1.02-1.17 (Rh4.2); 1.00-1.74 (PfSEA); 0.97-1.52 (Etramp5Ag1); 0.98-1.60 (AMA1); 1.15-2.93 (GLURP); 1.03-1.78 (EBA175))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** first birth year (12 months)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36812236/

### Proof-of-concept study for a long-acting formulation of ivermectin injected in cattle as a complementary malaria vector control tool.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36788608)
- **Journal:** Parasites & vectors
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-02-14
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; endectocide
- **Population:** Cattle (local breed) used to feed Anopheles coluzzii mosquitoes
- **N:** 8
- **Impact:** Both long-acting ivermectin formulations injected in cattle significantly reduced Anopheles coluzzii mosquito survival for up to 210 days post-treatment, suggesting potential as a complementary malaria vector control tool.
- **Effect:** median survival time post-feeding 4-5 days
- **Follow-up:** 210 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36788608/

### The Impact of Antenatal Azithromycin and Monthly Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine on Maternal Malaria during Pregnancy and Fetal Growth: A Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36780896)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-02-13
- **Trial period:** 2003 to 2006
- **Population:** pregnant women, 14-26 gestation weeks
- **N:** 1320
- **Impact:** Monthly SP and monthly SP plus azithromycin both reduced malaria parasitemia during pregnancy compared to two-dose SP, but neither intervention affected fetal biparietal diameter or femur length growth velocity, and azithromycin added no significant additional benefit over monthly SP alone.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 0.64 (monthly SP vs control), 0.50 (AZI-SP vs control) for combined trimesters (95% CI 0.42-0.98 (monthly SP); 0.32-0.76 (AZI-SP))
- **Follow-up:** until delivery (visits every 4 weeks until 36 weeks gestation, then weekly)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36780896/

### Artemether-lumefantrine efficacy among adults on antiretroviral therapy in Malawi.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36707795)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-01-27
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Adults (≥18 years) living with HIV on antiretroviral therapy (ART) for ≥6 months with undetectable viral load and CD4 ≥250/mm3, who developed uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 186
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine achieved a PCR-corrected efficacy of 94% meeting WHO acceptable treatment criteria, but day 7 lumefantrine levels were frequently low, especially among those on efavirenz-based ART, and low levels were associated with treatment failure.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response (ACPR) rate 94% (95% CI 92-97)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days per episode, over 5 years total study duration
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36707795/

### Effect of a fifth round of seasonal malaria chemoprevention in children aged 5-14 years in Dangassa, an area of long transmission in Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36704118)
- **Journal:** Parasite epidemiology and control
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** open-label randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-01-02
- **Class:** SMC
- **Population:** children aged 5-14 years
- **Impact:** An additional fifth round of seasonal malaria chemoprevention significantly reduced malaria incidence by 51% in children aged 5-14 years compared to control.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio (IRR) 0.49 (51% reduction) (95% CI 0.26-0.94)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** August to December (single transmission season)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36704118/

### Cluster-randomized controlled trial of intermittent preventive treatment in infancy using sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine (SP-IPTi): a pilot study in Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36633492)
- **Journal:** Journal of tropical pediatrics
- **Type:** trial

- **Class:** vaccine; PMC
- **Population:** infants receiving routine immunizations
- **N:** 1379
- **Impact:** SP-IPTi was safe and well tolerated but showed no significant benefit in reducing asymptomatic parasitemia, fever, or hospitalization compared to controls.
- **Effect:** risk difference no statistically significant difference
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 9 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36633492/

### Malaria Diagnostic Testing and Conditional Subsidies to Target ACTs in the Retail Sector: the TESTsmART Trial AIM 1
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03810014)
- **Journal:** BMJ open
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, FACTORIAL, NA
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2022-12-06
- **Trial period:** 2019-03-28 to 2019-10-31
- **Intervention(s):** Conditional ACT subsidy, Arm 1 levels; Conditional ACT subsidy, Arm 2 levels; Conditional ACT subsidy, Arm 3 levels; Conditional ACT subsidy, Arm 4 levels
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** Drug shop clients with malaria-like illness, including children over 1 year accompanied by a parent or guardian
- **N:** 842
- **Impact:** Subsidizing malaria diagnostic testing reduced client expenditures by an amount roughly equal to the subsidy value, while subsidizing ACT treatment did not significantly change spending compared to free treatment.
- **Effect:** Difference in expenditure (Ksh) -23 (95% CI -34.6 to -10.7)
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03810014

### Study to Investigate the Clinical and Parasiticidal Activity and Pharmacokinetics of Different Doses of Artefenomel and Ferroquine in Patients With Uncomplicated Plasmodium Falciparum Malaria
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03660839)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Benin, Burkina Faso, Gabon, Kenya, Uganda
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2023-01-03
- **Trial period:** 2018-09-11 to 2019-11-06
- **Intervention(s):** Artefenomel (OZ439); Ferroquine (SSR97193)
- **Population:** Patients aged 14-69 years with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria (3000-50,000 parasites/µL)
- **N:** 140
- **Impact:** The contribution of artefenomel exposure to Day 28 PCR-adjusted ACPR was not demonstrated, though parasite clearance was faster with ferroquine/artefenomel combinations than ferroquine alone.
- **Effect:** odds ratio (contribution of artefenomel AUC0-∞ to Day 28 ACPR) 1.1 (95% CI 0.98-1.2)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03660839

### Screening eaves of houses reduces indoor mosquito density in rural, western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36494664)

- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized, paired-house comparison
- **Status:** published

- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** rural households
- **N:** 240
- **Impact:** Eave screening of houses significantly reduced indoor densities of Anopheles funestus, Anopheles gambiae Complex, and Culex species compared to unscreened houses.
- **Effect:** Rate ratio (RR) RR = 0.40 (An. funestus), 0.46 (An. gambiae Complex), 0.53 (Culex spp.) (95% CI 0.29-0.55; 0.34-0.62; 0.45-0.61)
- **Species:** mixed

### A randomized controlled trial showing safety and efficacy of a whole sporozoite vaccine against endemic malaria.

- **Journal:** Science translational medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase II
- **Place:** Balonghin, Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-12-07
- **Class:** vaccine; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** malaria-experienced adults
- **N:** 80
- **Impact:** A three-dose regimen of PfSPZ Vaccine was safe and provided significant but partial protection against P. falciparum infection in Burkinabe adults, with efficacy declining from 38% at 6 months to 15% at 18 months.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy (1 - risk ratio) 38% at 6 months; 15% at 18 months

### Persistence of Plasmodium falciparum HRP-2 antigenaemia after artemisinin combination therapy is not associated with gametocytes.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36474274)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-12-06
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** P. falciparum infected, gametocyte positive individuals
- **N:** 100
- **Impact:** Persistence of residual gametocytes after ACT treatment (with or without primaquine) was not associated with prolonged HRP-2 antigenaemia or RDT positivity.
- **Effect:** Hazard ratio (time to test negativity) uRDT HR 0.79, RDT HR 0.77, ELISA HR 0.88 (95% CI uRDT 0.52-1.21; RDT 0.51-1.15; ELISA 0.59-1.32)
- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Safety of age-dosed, single low-dose primaquine in children with glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase deficiency who are infected with Plasmodium falciparum in Uganda and the Democratic Republic of the Congo: a randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled, non-inferiority trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36462528)

- **Place:** Uganda, Democratic Republic of the Congo
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-11-30
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2019
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 11 years with acute uncomplicated P falciparum infection, including G6PD-deficient children
- **N:** 1137
- **Impact:** Age-dosed, single low-dose primaquine was well tolerated in children with and without G6PD deficiency, with a safety profile similar to placebo.
- **Effect:** risk difference in severe anaemia incidence -0.66% (G6PD-deficient group); -0.014% (non-G6PD-deficient group) (95% CI -1.96 to 0.63 (G6PD-deficient); -0.68 to 0.65 (non-G6PD-deficient))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 21 days

### Therapeutic efficacies of artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine for the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum and chloroquine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine for uncomplicated Plasmodium vivax infection in Ethiopia.

- **Status:** published

### Comparative effect of artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine on gametocyte clearance in children with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Madagascar.

- **Status:** published

- **Population:** children with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria

- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine cleared gametocytes more rapidly than artesunate-amodiaquine, with gametocytes undetectable by day 14 in the AL arm versus persisting until day 21 in the ASAQ arm.

### Human behavior determinants of exposure to Anopheles vectors of malaria in Sumba, Indonesia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36374859)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Indonesia
- **Design:** observational analysis nested within a larger clinical trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-11-14
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; spatial repellent
- **Population:** households in Sumba, general community members
- **Impact:** Human behavior-adjusted exposure analysis showed approximately 28% less exposure to Anopheles bites in the spatial repellent intervention arm compared to placebo, despite no significant difference in raw vector biting rates alone.
- **Effect:** reduction in human behavior adjusted exposure 28%
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36374859/

### Effects of ivermectin mass drug administration for malaria vector control on ectoparasites and soil-transmitted helminths: a cluster randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36336245)
- **Journal:** International journal of infectious diseases : IJID : official publication of the International Society for Infectious Diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** The Gambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-11-03
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2021
- **Class:** MDA; endectocide
- **Population:** children aged 3 to 14 years
- **Impact:** Ivermectin MDA reduced Strongyloides stercoralis prevalence shortly after administration but had no lasting effect after 2 years and did not reduce scabies prevalence.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 0.4 (S. stercoralis after MDA) (95% CI 0.16-0.94)
- **Follow-up:** 2 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36336245/

### Participatory development of practical, affordable, insecticide-treated mosquito proofing for a range of housing designs in rural southern Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36335363)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, three-arm, participatory field trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-11-05
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** rural households
- **N:** 54
- **Impact:** Screening houses, whether treated with pirimiphos-methyl or not, almost entirely excluded the malaria vector Anopheles arabiensis but was much less effective against the nuisance mosquito Culex quinquefasciatus, and insecticide treatment did not add extra protection at the household level.
- **Effect:** Relative reduction in mosquito density ≥98% for An. arabiensis; ≤46% for Cx. quinquefasciatus
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 5 months after screening (8 months for insecticide residual efficacy)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36335363/

### Improved adherence to test, treat, and track (T3) malaria strategy among Over-the-Counter Medicine Sellers (OTCMS) through interventions implemented in selected rural communities of Fanteakwa North district, Ghana.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36335323)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-11-05
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** Over-the-Counter Medicine Sellers (OTCMS) and children under 10 years with fever visiting OTCMS in rural communities
- **N:** 12
- **Impact:** Interventions targeting OTCMS significantly improved adherence to malaria testing and treatment protocols compared to control, and increased testing rates though the difference in end-line testing rate was not statistically significant.
- **Effect:** proportion tested (end-line vs baseline) 30.8% vs 10.5%
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36335323/

### Knowledge, practices and perceptions of communities during a malaria larviciding randomized trial in the city of Yaoundé, Cameroon.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36327271)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Cameroon, Yaoundé
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-11-03
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; larval source management
- **Population:** community members living in intervention and non-intervention urban areas of Yaoundé
- **Impact:** People living in larviciding intervention areas were more likely to know about malaria transmission and mosquito breeding habitats, reported reduced mosquito nuisance and malaria cases, and continued to use bed nets, indicating good acceptance of the larviciding program.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 2.64 (knowledge of malaria transmission mode); 1.3 (knowledge of mosquito breeding habitats) (95% CI 1.82-3.84; 1.06-1.56)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36327271/

### Safety and Efficacy of a Monoclonal Antibody against Malaria in Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36317783)
- **Journal:** The New England journal of medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-10-31
- **Class:** monoclonal antibody; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** healthy adults
- **N:** 330
- **Impact:** A single intravenous infusion of the monoclonal antibody CIS43LS protected healthy adults in Mali against P. falciparum infection over a 6-month malaria season, with efficacy of 88.2% at the 40 mg/kg dose and 75.0% at the 10 mg/kg dose compared with placebo.
- **Effect:** efficacy 88.2% (40 mg/kg); 75.0% (10 mg/kg) (95% CI 79.3 to 93.3 (40 mg/kg); 61.0 to 84.0 (10 mg/kg))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months (24 weeks)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36317783/

### Economic evaluation of postdischarge malaria chemoprevention in preschool children treated for severe anaemia in Malawi, Kenya, and Uganda: A cost-effectiveness analysis.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36313146)
- **Journal:** EClinicalMedicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase III
- **Place:** Malawi, Kenya, Uganda
- **Design:** Markov decision model cost-effectiveness analysis using data from a placebo-controlled trial and a delivery mechanism trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-10-01
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2018
- **Class:** PDMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Preschool children discharged after hospitalisation for severe anaemia
- **N:** 1424
- **Impact:** Both community-based and facility-based postdischarge malaria chemoprevention were cost-saving compared to standard of care in Malawi, Kenya, and Uganda, with community-based delivery being the most cost-effective strategy.
- **Effect:** Incremental cost savings per child treated US$22.10 (Malawi), US$38.52 (Kenya), US$26.23 (Uganda)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months postdischarge
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36313146/

### Effect of Malaria and Malaria Chemoprevention Regimens in Pregnancy and Childhood on Neurodevelopmental and Behavioral Outcomes in Children at 12, 24, and 36 Months: A Randomized Clinical Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36219705)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2023-02-01
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women and their children
- **N:** 305
- **Impact:** Malaria in pregnancy and early childhood was associated with worse cognitive, behavioral, and executive function outcomes in children, but more effective malaria chemoprevention regimens did not improve neurodevelopmental outcomes.
- **Effect:** association with neurodevelopmental scores
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 36 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36219705/

### Enhancing Preventive Therapy of Malaria In Children With Sickle Cell Anemia in East Africa (EPiTOMISE)
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03178643)
- **Journal:** PLoS medicine
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 4
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE4
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2022-10-10
- **Trial period:** 2018-01-23 to 2020-12-16
- **Intervention(s):** Proguanil Oral Tablet; Sulfadoxine/Pyrimethanine-Amodiaquine (SP-AQ); Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine (DP)
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 1 to 10 years with homozygous hemoglobin S (sickle cell anemia)
- **N:** 246
- **Impact:** Compared to daily Proguanil, monthly SP-AQ and monthly DP chemoprevention did not significantly reduce clinical malaria incidence, though DP was associated with reduced dactylitis and P. falciparum parasitization.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio (IRR) for clinical malaria SP-AQ IRR 3.05; DP IRR 1.36 (95% CI SP-AQ 95% CI 0.36-26.14; DP 95% CI 0.21-8.85)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03178643

### Effect of long-lasting insecticidal nets with and without piperonyl butoxide on malaria indicators in Uganda (LLINEUP): final results of a cluster-randomised trial embedded in a national distribution campaign.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36174592)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-09-26
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2019
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** children aged 2-10 years in households across 48 districts of eastern and western Uganda
- **N:** 104
- **Impact:** PBO LLINs reduced malaria parasite prevalence more effectively than conventional pyrethroid-only LLINs for up to 25 months after distribution.
- **Effect:** prevalence ratio (adjusted for baseline) 0.80 (95% CI 0.69-0.93)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 25 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36174592/

### Safety and immunogenicity of BK-SE36 in a blinded, randomized, controlled, age de-escalating phase Ib clinical trial in Burkinabe children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36119062)
- **Journal:** Frontiers in immunology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase Ib
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-08-31
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** children aged 12-24 months and 25-60 months
- **N:** 108
- **Impact:** BK-SE36 was well tolerated and immunogenic via both intramuscular and subcutaneous routes, with higher immune response observed in the younger age cohort.
- **Effect:** fold change in antibody geometric mean titers more than 2-fold change from pre-vaccination; 2-fold and 4-fold higher GMT in younger vs older cohort after 2 and 3 doses respectively
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months after first vaccination (through Week 26 and beyond)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36119062/

### Community-Based Malaria Testing Reduces Polypharmacy in a Population-Based Survey of Febrile Illness in Western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36090831)
- **Journal:** International journal of public health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-08-25
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** febrile individuals in the community, including children and adults
- **N:** 5756
- **Impact:** Community-based access to malaria diagnostic testing reduced dual consumption of ACTs and antibiotics for fever, particularly among those seeking care outside the formal healthcare sector.
- **Effect:** proportion with dual ACT and antibiotic consumption declined from 22.1% at baseline to 16.6% at endline
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36090831/

### Efficacy and immunogenicity of R21/Matrix-M vaccine against clinical malaria after 2 years' follow-up in children in Burkina Faso: a phase 1/2b randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36087586)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 1/2b
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-09-07
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** children aged 5-17 months at enrolment (booster given at 1 year later)
- **N:** 409
- **Impact:** A booster dose of R21/Matrix-M maintained high efficacy against clinical malaria, with 80% efficacy in the high-dose adjuvant group over 12 months following the booster.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy 71% (low-dose adjuvant), 80% (high-dose adjuvant) (95% CI 60-78 (low-dose); 72-85 (high-dose))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 years (12 months post-booster reported)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36087586/

### Outdoor residual spraying for malaria vector-control in Kayin (Karen) state, Myanmar: A cluster randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36083983)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Myanmar (Kayin/Karen state)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-09-09
- **Trial period:** July 2018 to April 2019
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** Villages in Karen (Kayin) state; humans and cows sampled for mosquito biting rates
- **N:** 12
- **Impact:** Outdoor residual spraying rapidly and substantially reduced malaria mosquito biting rates compared to control villages over a 6-month period.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in biting rate 72% (Month 0-3), 79% (Month 4-6) (95% CI 63-79 (Month 0-3); 62-88 (Month 4-6))
- **Species:** Anopheles (malaria vectors, species not specified as P. falciparum/vivax)
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36083983/

### Efficacy of three anti-malarial regimens for uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Cambodia, 2009-2011: a randomized controlled trial and brief review.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (36071520)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Cambodia
- **Design:** randomized, open-label, parallel group-controlled trial, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-09-07
- **Trial period:** 2009 to 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** subjects at least 5 years old with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 211
- **Impact:** DHA/PPQ and ATQ/PG had acceptable efficacy against uncomplicated falciparum malaria, while AS/MQ efficacy was only 80.6% with evidence of mefloquine resistance.
- **Effect:** per-protocol PCR-adjusted efficacy AS/MQ 80.6%, DHA/PPQ 97.2%, ATQ/PG 92.9% (95% CI AS/MQ 70.8-90.5%; DHA/PPQ 93.3-100%; ATQ/PG 86.1-99.6%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36071520/

### Overall and Gender-Specific Effects of Intermittent Preventive Treatment of Malaria with Artemisinin-Based Combination Therapies among Schoolchildren in Mali: A Three-Group Open Label Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35995135)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-08-22
- **Trial period:** 2007 to 2013
- **Class:** IPTsc; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** schoolchildren aged 6-13 years
- **N:** 305
- **Impact:** Intermittent preventive treatment with SP plus artesunate or AQ plus artesunate reduced P. falciparum infection compared with control, with SP plus artesunate improving hemoglobin and AQ plus artesunate improving GPA, and girls given SP plus artesunate showing greater GPA improvement than boys.
- **Effect:** odds ratio for P. falciparum infection SP+artesunate OR 0.33; AQ+artesunate OR 0.46 (95% CI 0.26-0.43 (SP+artesunate); 0.36-0.59 (AQ+artesunate))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** September 2007 to February 2013 (approximately 5.5 years)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35995135/

### Impact of mobile phone intervention on intermittent preventive treatment of malaria during pregnancy in Burkina Faso : A pragmatic randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35989210)
- **Journal:** Revue d'epidemiologie et de sante publique
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-08-19
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal clinic visits
- **N:** 248
- **Impact:** The mobile phone intervention did not significantly increase the proportion of women receiving three or more doses of IPTp-SP, but it did increase the proportion of timely ANC visits.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio 0.86 (95% CI 0.49-1.51)
- **Follow-up:** from first ANC visit until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35989210/

### Laboratory and experimental hut trial evaluation of VECTRON ™ T500 for indoor residual spraying (IRS) against insecticide resistant malaria vectors in Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35950163)
- **Journal:** Gates open research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** experimental hut trial, laboratory cone bioassay study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-07-25
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** wild free-flying Anopheles gambiae s.l. mosquitoes and laboratory mosquito strains (susceptible and pyrethroid resistant)
- **N:** 19552
- **Impact:** VECTRON T500 showed good residual efficacy against susceptible and resistant Anopheles strains in laboratory tests and induced 55-73% mortality of wild An. gambiae s.l. in experimental huts, suggesting potential as an IRS product for resistance management.
- **Effect:** mortality rate 55-73%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 14 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35950163/

### Daily Trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole or Weekly Chloroquine Among Adults on ART in Malawi
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT01650558)
- **Journal:** AIDS (London, England)
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, FACTORIAL, NA
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2022-07-15
- **Trial period:** 2012-11 to 2018-07-31
- **Intervention(s):** Standard of Care prophylaxis; Chloroquine (CQ) prophylaxis
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Adults living with HIV on antiretroviral therapy (ART) with undetectable viral load and ≥250 CD4+ cells/μl
- **N:** 1499
- **Impact:** Chemoprophylaxis with daily trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole or weekly chloroquine reduced malaria infection and clinical malaria incidence by approximately 90% compared to no prophylaxis, but malaria did not affect HIV viral load or CD4+ cell count.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio TS: IRR 0.11; CQ: IRR 0.09 (95% CI TS: 95% CI 0.08-0.15; CQ: 95% CI 0.06-0.13)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01650558

### LLIN evaluation in Uganda project (LLINEUP): The fabric integrity, chemical content and bioefficacy of long-lasting insecticidal nets treated with and without piperonyl butoxide across two years of operational use in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35734077)
- **Journal:** Current research in parasitology & vector-borne diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** pragmatic, cluster-randomised trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-05-25
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2018
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households receiving LLINs during Uganda's mass distribution campaign
- **N:** 104
- **Impact:** PBO-treated LLINs (Olyset Plus and PermaNet 3.0) were as physically durable as pyrethroid-only nets and initially more effective against pyrethroid-resistant mosquitoes, but their bioefficacy and PBO content declined substantially over 25 months of operational use.
- **Effect:** Knockdown rate at 25 months Olyset Plus 45.72%, PermaNet 3.0 78.57% (95% CI 22.84-68.62% (Olyset Plus); 63.57-93.58% (PermaNet 3.0))
- **Follow-up:** 25 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35734077/

### Cellular and antibody response in GMZ2-vaccinated Gabonese volunteers in a controlled human malaria infection trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35715803)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Gabon
- **Design:** controlled human malaria infection trial, randomized, phase 2
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-06-17
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** Gabonese adults
- **N:** 50
- **Impact:** GMZ2 vaccination induced antibody but limited cellular immune responses, and broad antibody recognition of multiple parasite antigens rather than the GMZ2-specific response was associated with protection against controlled human malaria infection.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35715803/

### Impact of a spatial repellent product on Anopheles and non-Anopheles mosquitoes in Sumba, Indonesia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35659231)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Sumba, East Nusa Tenggara, Indonesia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-06-03
- **Trial period:** 2015 to 2018
- **Class:** spatial repellent
- **Population:** households/communities in Sumba (general population, human-landing mosquito catch collectors)
- **Impact:** The spatial repellent reduced human biting rates for Anopheles (16.4%) and non-Anopheles mosquitoes (Aedes, Armigeres, Culex) both indoors and outdoors, though most reductions were not statistically significant except for some individual species affecting malaria infection hazard rate.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in human biting rate 16.4% (Anopheles overall); 44.3% indoors/35.6% outdoors (Aedes); 38.3% indoors/39.1% outdoors (Armigeres); 36.0% indoors/32.3% outdoors (Culex)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 24 months intervention period (April 2016 to April 2018)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35659231/

### Effectiveness of pyronaridine-artesunate against Plasmodium malariae, Plasmodium ovale spp, and mixed-Plasmodium infections: a post-hoc analysis of the CANTAM-Pyramax trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35654079)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Microbe
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Democratic Republic of the Congo, Gabon
- **Design:** post-hoc analysis, phase 3b/4 clinical trial
- **Status:** published

- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with mixed-Plasmodium infections or non-falciparum mono-infections
- **N:** 192
- **Impact:** Pyronaridine-artesunate showed high day 28 cure rates (96.3% or higher) against P malariae, P ovale curtisi, P ovale wallikeri, and mixed-Plasmodium infections.
- **Effect:** cure rate 96.3% or higher (99.2% for P malariae, 97.9% for P ovale curtisi, 96.3% for P ovale wallikeri) (95% CI 81.0-99.9 to 95.7-100)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35654079/

### Efficacy, T cell activation and antibody responses in accelerated Plasmodium falciparum sporozoite chemoprophylaxis vaccine regimens.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35641527)
- **Journal:** NPJ vaccines
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase II

- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-05-31
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody; chemoprevention
- **Population:** malaria-naive volunteers
- **Impact:** Both the 10-day and 28-day accelerated PfSPZ-CVac vaccination regimens were similarly efficacious against controlled human malaria infection, with the 10-day regimen inducing stronger T cell and antibody responses.

### Effectiveness of screened ceilings over the current best practice in reducing malaria prevalence in western Kenya: a cluster randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35437129)

- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** cluster-randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-04-19
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** children 7 months to 10 years old (epidemiological surveys); households (entomological surveys)
- **N:** 2235
- **Impact:** Houses with insecticide-treated ceiling nets plus bed nets had significantly lower malaria prevalence and mosquito density than houses with bed nets alone.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk ratio 0.53 (PCRPfPR); 0.41 (anopheline density) (95% CI 0.41-0.71 (PCRPfPR); 0.29-0.90 (anopheline density))

- **Impact:** A single oral dose of azithromycin did not reduce malaria RDT positivity compared to placebo, though caregiver-reported fever as an adverse event was less common in the azithromycin arm.

### Study to Compare the Triple ACT AL+AQ With the ACT AL in Cambodia and Vietnam
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03355664)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Cambodia, Vietnam
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2022-03-08
- **Trial period:** 2018-03-19 to 2020-03-04
- **Intervention(s):** ACT; TACT
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** male and female patients aged 2-65 years with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 310
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine plus amodiaquine showed similarly high 42-day PCR-corrected efficacy compared with artemether-lumefantrine alone, with no statistically significant difference, but was associated with more mild gastrointestinal adverse events and bradycardia.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio for recrudescence 0.6 (95% CI 0.2-1.9)

### The positive effect of malaria IPTp-SP on birthweight is mediated by gestational weight gain but modifiable by maternal carriage of enteric pathogens.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35217408)
- **Journal:** EBioMedicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-02-23
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal care
- **N:** 105
- **Impact:** IPTp-SP was associated with greater maternal gestational weight gain and reduced EAEC prevalence compared to IPTp-DP, and this weight gain mediated SP's positive effect on infant birthweight, though maternal enteric pathogen carriage diminished this benefit.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio (EAEC infection) and adjusted mean birthweight difference ORadj = 0.07 (EAEC infection after 3+ doses); adjusted mean birthweight difference SP vs DP = 156.0 g (95% CI OR CI95 = 0.12-0.39; birthweight difference CI95 = -18.0 to 336.9 g)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** from first antenatal visit until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35217408/

### Serological evaluation of the effectiveness of reactive focal mass drug administration and reactive vector control to reduce malaria transmission in Zambezi Region, Namibia: Results from a secondary analysis of a cluster randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35198913)
- **Journal:** EClinicalMedicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Namibia
- **Design:** cluster randomised trial, secondary analysis, 2x2 factorial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-02-14
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2017
- **Class:** monoclonal antibody; MDA
- **Population:** residents of study clusters in a low-transmission region tested via reactive case detection
- **Impact:** Reactive focal mass drug administration and reactive vector control, alone and combined, significantly reduced Etramp5.Ag1 sero-prevalence, with serological endpoints providing improved precision and statistical power compared to qPCR prevalence.
- **Effect:** adjusted prevalence ratio (aPR) for Etramp5.Ag1 sero-prevalence 0.78 (rfMDA), 0.79 (RAVC), 0.59 (combined rfMDA plus RAVC) (95% CI 0.65-0.91 (rfMDA); 0.67-0.92 (RAVC); 0.46-0.76 (combined))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35198913/

### Seasonal Malaria Vaccination (RTS,S/AS01) and Seasonal Malaria Chemoprevention (SP/AQ)
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03143218)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Mali
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2022-02-22
- **Trial period:** 2017-04-17 to 2020-03-31
- **Intervention(s):** RABIPUR®; RTS,S/AS01; SMC with SP+AQ; SMC placebo
- **Class:** vaccine; SMC
- **Population:** young children
- **N:** 5920
- **Impact:** Combining seasonal RTS,S/AS01E vaccination with seasonal malaria chemoprevention substantially reduced malaria incidence and modestly reduced wasting prevalence compared to either intervention alone, but had little effect on other nutritional indicators like stunting.
- **Effect:** prevalence ratio 0.88 (2017), 0.79 (2018) for wasting prevalence, combined vs SMC alone (95% CI 95% CI 0.75-1.03 (2017); 0.62-1.01 (2018))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** three malaria transmission seasons (2017-2019)
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03143218

### Multi-Dose Priming Regimens of PfSPZ Vaccine: Safety and Efficacy against Controlled Human Malaria Infection in Equatoguinean Adults.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35130487)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Equatorial Guinea
- **Design:** randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, phase 2
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-02-07
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** 18- to 32-year-old adults
- **N:** 104
- **Impact:** Only the multi-dose priming regimen given on days 1, 8, and 29 provided significant protection against controlled human malaria infection, with 51.3% vaccine efficacy.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy 51.3%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6-7 weeks after vaccination
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35130487/

### Efficacy of 3-day low dose quinine plus clindamycin versus artemether-lumefantrine for the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Kenyan children (CLINDAQUINE): an open-label randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (35109841)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-02-02
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 384
- **Impact:** 3-day low dose quinine plus clindamycin was markedly less effective than artemether-lumefantrine for treating uncomplicated falciparum malaria in Kenyan children, with no evidence to support its use.
- **Effect:** treatment difference in PCR-corrected ACPR rate -53.1% (95% CI -43.5% to -62.7%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35109841/

### Efficacy and safety of artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine for the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria and prevalence of molecular markers associated with artemisinin and partner drug resistance in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34952573)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase IV
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized, open-label, phase IV clinical trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-12-24
- **Trial period:** 2018 to 2019
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Children aged 6 months to 10 years with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **Impact:** DP remained highly effective and safe for treating uncomplicated malaria in Uganda, while AL showed common recurrent infections with PCR-corrected efficacy falling below 90% at two sites.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected cumulative efficacy AL: 87.2-94.4% (day 28); DP: 98.9-100% (day 28), 92.1-97.5% (day 42)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days (AL), 42 days (DP)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34952573/

### Bioefficacy and durability of Olyset® Plus, a permethrin and piperonyl butoxide-treated insecticidal net in a 3-year long trial in Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34930459)
- **Journal:** Infectious diseases of poverty
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-12-20
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2017
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households in a malaria endemic rice cultivation zone
- **Impact:** Olyset® Plus retained WHO efficacy criteria longer (about 2 years) than Olyset® Net (1-1.5 years), but neither net met the 3-year WHO efficacy criteria, with comparable physical durability and survivorship between the two nets.
- **Effect:** proportion meeting WHO efficacy criteria (knockdown/mortality/blood-feeding inhibition) Over 80% of Olyset® Plus and Olyset® Net passed WHO efficacy criteria up to 18 and 12 months respectively (95% CI 95-80)

- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34930459/

### Safety of KAE609 in Adults With Uncomplicated Plasmodium Falciparum Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT03334747)

- **Type:** registration

- **Place:** Gabon, Ghana, Mali, Rwanda, Uganda

- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adults with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria

- **Impact:** Cipargamin (KAE609) at doses of 10-150 mg was well tolerated with hepatic adverse event rates similar to artemether-lumefantrine and no significant difference between groups.
- **Effect:** proportion with ≥2 CTCAE Grade increase in ALT/AST 2/135 (cipargamin) vs 2/51 (artemether-lumefantrine)

### Mechanistic Modeling of Primaquine Pharmacokinetics, Gametocytocidal Activity, and Mosquito Infectivity.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34905220)
- **Journal:** Clinical pharmacology and therapeutics
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized, placebo-controlled, dose-ranging pharmacokinetic-pharmacodynamic modeling study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2022-01-22
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adult Malians with P. falciparum gametocytemia
- **N:** 81
- **Impact:** A single low dose of primaquine (0.25 mg/kg) added to dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine rapidly and dose-dependently sterilized P. falciparum gametocytes, preventing mosquito infectivity.
- **Effect:** dose-dependent gametocytocidal effect 0.25 mg base/kg dose rapidly prevented transmissibility
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34905220/

### Tafenoquine exposure assessment, safety, and relapse prevention efficacy in children with Plasmodium vivax malaria: open-label, single-arm, non-comparative, multicentre, pharmacokinetic bridging, phase 2 trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34871570)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Child & adolescent health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Vietnam, Colombia
- **Design:** open-label, single-arm, non-comparative, multicentre, pharmacokinetic bridging, phase 2 trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-12-03
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2020
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 2-15 years weighing 5 kg or more with P vivax malaria and G6PD activity more than 70% of local population median
- **N:** 60
- **Impact:** Single-dose tafenoquine, including a dispersible formulation, achieved adequate exposure across weight-based dosing groups and provided high recurrence-free efficacy with safety consistent with adults, aside from post-dose vomiting with the 50 mg dispersible tablet.
- **Effect:** 4-month recurrence-free efficacy 94.7% (95% CI 95% CI 84.6-98.3)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 4 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34871570/

- **Status:** published

- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** healthy adults
- **N:** 600
- **Impact:** 52-week tafenoquine prophylaxis was well tolerated with no significant difference in serious ophthalmic events or overall adverse events compared to placebo, supporting its long-term safety.
- **Effect:** Serious Ophthalmic Safety Event incidence 18.2% (tafenoquine) vs 19% (placebo)
- **Follow-up:** 64 weeks

- **Impact:** Both artesunate-amodiaquine and artemether-lumefantrine showed PCR-corrected therapeutic efficacies above the WHO acceptable cut-off of 90%, with no molecular evidence of artemisinin resistance detected.

### The impact of mosquito proof lids of underground tanks "tanka" on the breeding of Anopheles stephensi in a village in western Rajasthan, India.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34666794)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** India (western Rajasthan)
- **Design:** controlled intervention study, non-randomized, two-village comparison (intervention vs control)
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-10-19
- **Trial period:** 2018 to 2019
- **Population:** underground water storage tanks (tankas) and village community, adult mosquito density assessments
- **N:** 262
- **Impact:** Replacement of damaged tanka lids with mosquito-proof PVC lids led to complete prevention of Anopheles stephensi breeding and a significant reduction in adult mosquito density in the intervention village compared to no reduction in the control village.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in Anopheles stephensi density per man-hour 97.8%

- **Follow-up:** 1 year (pre-intervention May 2018 to follow-up May 2019)

### A three-antigen Plasmodium falciparum DNA prime-Adenovirus boost malaria vaccine regimen is superior to a two-antigen regimen and protects against controlled human malaria infection in healthy malaria-naïve adults.

- **Design:** randomized controlled trial

- **Impact:** The three-antigen ChAd63 CAT vaccine (CSP, AMA1, ME-TRAP) significantly protected against controlled human malaria infection compared to infectivity controls and the two-antigen CA vaccine, which showed no protective efficacy.

### Therapeutic Efficacy of Artemisinin-Based Combination Therapies in Democratic Republic of the Congo and Investigation of Molecular Markers of Antimalarial Resistance.

- **Impact:** A single dose of oral azithromycin did not reduce malaria parasitaemia compared to placebo at 14 days or 6 months follow-up.

### Unanticipated CNS Safety Signal in a Placebo-Controlled, Randomized Trial of Co-Administered Atovaquone-Proguanil and Amodiaquine.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34453327)
- **Journal:** Clinical pharmacology and therapeutics
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase II
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-09-14
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** healthy adult males and females of Black sub-Saharan African origin
- **N:** 44
- **Impact:** Co-administration of atovaquone-proguanil and amodiaquine showed no clinically relevant pharmacokinetic interactions but caused an unexpectedly high incidence of extrapyramidal adverse effects, leading to a recommendation against further development of the combination for malaria prophylaxis in African children.
- **Effect:** incidence of extrapyramidal adverse effects 2 of 8 (25%) in ATV-PG/AQ group
- **Follow-up:** 3 days of treatment with follow-up assessments

### Imatinib augments standard malaria combination therapy without added toxicity.

### High efficacy of microbial larvicides for malaria vectors control in the city of Yaounde Cameroon following a cluster randomized trial.

- **Status:** published

- **Trial period:** September 2018 to November 2020
- **Class:** larval source management
- **Population:** urban residents in clusters with mosquito breeding habitats
- **N:** 26
- **Impact:** Larviciding with VectoMax reduced adult anopheline biting density by 68% and entomological inoculation rate by 79% in the intervention clusters compared to non-intervention clusters.

### Effectiveness of a long-lasting insecticide treatment kit (ICON® Maxx) for polyester nets over three years of household use: a WHO phase III trial in Tanzania.

- **Impact:** ICON Maxx-treated polyester nets retained sufficient insecticidal efficacy over 36 months of household use, meeting WHO durability criteria and earning full WHO recommendation, outperforming conventional treated nets.

### Two chemoattenuated PfSPZ malaria vaccines induce sterile hepatic immunity.

- **Population:** rural community population (general population in study area)
- **N:** 25000
- **Impact:** Community-led implementation of house improvement and larval source management in rural Malawi cost $27.04, $25.06, and $33.44 per person per year respectively, with total programme costs of $626,152, driven mainly by staff, transport, and labour costs.
- **Effect:** incremental economic cost per person per year $27.04 (HI), $25.06 (LSM), $33.44 (HI+LSM)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34120608/

### Exploratory analysis of the effect of helminth infection on the immunogenicity and efficacy of the asexual blood-stage malaria vaccine candidate GMZ2.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34061838)
- **Journal:** PLoS neglected tropical diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 1
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-06-01
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** healthy, lifelong malaria-exposed adult volunteers
- **N:** 50
- **Impact:** Helminth infection, particularly with Schistosoma haematobium and soil-transmitted helminths, was associated with earlier malaria episodes and reduced vaccine immunogenicity, suggesting helminth infection may impair naturally acquired and vaccine-induced protection against malaria.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 35 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34061838/

### Persistent Submicroscopic Plasmodium falciparum Parasitemia 72 Hours after Treatment with Artemether-Lumefantrine Predicts 42-Day Treatment Failure in Mali and Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34060901)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Mali, Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial, multi-center, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-07-16
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria treated with artemether-lumefantrine and other ACTs
- **N:** 552
- **Impact:** Patients with qPCR-detectable submicroscopic parasitemia at 72 hours after artemether-lumefantrine treatment were significantly more likely to experience recurrent parasitemia by day 42 and had a shorter interval to their next clinical episode compared to those with complete clearance or other drug regimens.
- **Effect:** proportion with qPCR-detectable parasitemia at 72h 17.7%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34060901/

### Therapeutic efficacy of artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine for the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Mali, 2015-2016.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34034754)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Sélingué, Mali
- **Design:** randomized, in vivo therapeutic efficacy study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-05-25
- **Trial period:** 2015 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children 6-59 months with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 449
- **Impact:** Both artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine remained effective treatments for uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Sélingué, Mali, with ASAQ showing significantly higher efficacy than AL.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected efficacy at day 28 AL 91.0%, ASAQ 97.1% (95% CI AL 86.0-95.9%; ASAQ 93.6-100%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34034754/

### The effect of community-driven larval source management and house improvement on malaria transmission when added to the standard malaria control strategies in Malawi: a cluster-randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34022912)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-05-22
- **Trial period:** 2016
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; larval source management
- **Population:** villagers/households in Malawi (all ages)
- **N:** 20013
- **Impact:** Neither house improvement, larval source management, nor their combination further reduced malaria transmission or prevalence beyond reductions seen across all four arms over the trial period.
- **Effect:** EIR comparison between arms no significant difference between arms (95% CI 0.006-0.015)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 years (including 1 baseline year)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34022912/

### Heterologous protection against malaria by a simple chemoattenuated PfSPZ vaccine regimen in a randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33947856)
- **Journal:** Nature communications
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-05-04
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody; chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** malaria-naive volunteers
- **N:** 18
- **Impact:** 10 of 13 vaccinated participants were sterilely protected against heterologous malaria challenge while all 5 placebo recipients developed parasitemia.
- **Effect:** risk difference 77%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33947856/

### Malaria vaccine becomes first to achieve 75% efficacy goal in trial of children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33893146)

- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-04-23
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children
- **Impact:** A malaria vaccine achieved 75% efficacy, becoming the first to meet the WHO's efficacy goal in a trial of children.
- **Effect:** efficacy 75%

- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33893146/

### Effectiveness of Intermittent Preventive Treatment With Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaqunine Against Malaria in Pregnancy in Tanzania: A Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33891721)
- **Journal:** Clinical pharmacology and therapeutics

- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-06-17
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** malaria-free pregnant women from moderate malaria transmission areas
- **N:** 956
- **Impact:** Monthly IPTp with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine significantly reduced placental malaria and low birth weight compared with IPTp-SP.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 69% (95% CI 42-84)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** through delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33891721/

### Improved housing versus usual practice for additional protection against clinical malaria in The Gambia (RooPfs): a household-randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33838737)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Planetary health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** The Gambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-04-01
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2017
- **Class:** SMC; ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 13 years living in households with insecticide-treated nets, indoor residual spraying, and seasonal malaria chemoprevention for under-5s
- **N:** 805
- **Impact:** Improved housing (metal roofs and screened doors/windows) did not reduce clinical malaria incidence and was in fact associated with higher incidence compared with unmodified traditional houses in an area with high coverage of insecticide-treated nets, indoor residual spraying, and seasonal malaria chemoprevention.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio 1.68 (95% CI 1.11-2.55)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33838737/

### Efficacy of interceptor® G2, a long-lasting insecticide mixture net treated with chlorfenapyr and alpha-cypermethrin against Anopheles funestus: experimental hut trials in north-eastern Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33836778)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-04-09
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** wild, free-flying pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles funestus mosquitoes entering experimental huts
- **Impact:** Interceptor G2 caused significantly higher mortality in pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles funestus than the standard pyrethroid-only LLIN, with no significant loss of efficacy after 20 washes.
- **Effect:** risk ratio (mortality, unwashed IG2 vs unwashed Interceptor LN) 2.27 (95% CI 1.13-4.56)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33836778/

### Impact and cost-effectiveness of a lethal house lure against malaria transmission in central Côte d'Ivoire: a two-arm, cluster-randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33640067)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Côte d'Ivoire
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-02-01
- **Trial period:** Sept 26, 2016 to April 10, 2019
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 10 years, households in village-level clusters
- **N:** 2560
- **Impact:** Screening plus Eave Tubes combined with insecticide-treated nets significantly reduced clinical malaria incidence in children compared with insecticide-treated nets alone.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio 0.62 (95% CI 0.51-0.76)
- **Follow-up:** 2 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33640067/

### Social group and health-care provider interventions to increase the demand for malaria rapid diagnostic tests among community members in Ebonyi state, Nigeria: a cluster-randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33607030)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Global health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria (Ebonyi state)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-03-01
- **Trial period:** 2018
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** children under 5, children aged 5-17 years, and adults (excluding pregnant women) in the community
- **N:** 18
- **Impact:** Both the social group intervention and the social group plus provider intervention significantly increased the proportion of children and adults receiving an MRDT compared with control, with no significant difference between the two intervention arms.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk difference 28.8% (children <5, social group arm); 32.7% (children <5, social group plus provider arm); 25.6% (children ≥5, social group arm); 28.0% (children ≥5, social group plus provider arm) (95% CI 21.9-35.7; 24.9-40.5; 16.8-34.4; 19.5-36.5)
- **Follow-up:** 3 months after end of interventions
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33607030/

### Influence of cytochrome P450 (CYP) 2C8 polymorphisms on the efficacy and tolerability of artesunate-amodiaquine treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Zanzibar.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33588856)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial

- **Design:** pharmacogenetic analysis of samples from randomized clinical trials
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-02-15
- **Trial period:** 2002 to 2005
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under five years of age with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 618
- **Impact:** CYP2C8*2 and CYP2C8*3 alleles did not significantly affect treatment efficacy but were significantly associated with increased occurrence of non-serious adverse events.
- **Effect:** proportion with adverse events 44.9% vs 28.1% (95% CI 36.1-54.0 vs 21.9-35.0)
- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Randomized Controlled Trial of the Electrocardiographic Effects of Four Antimalarials for Pregnant Women with Uncomplicated Malaria on the Thailand-Myanmar Border.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33495217)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Thailand-Myanmar border
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-03-18
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women with uncomplicated malaria
- **N:** 277
- **Impact:** None of the four antimalarial regimens produced QT prolongation exceeding conventional thresholds for torsade de pointes risk in pregnant women, though dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine and chloroquine caused significantly greater QTcP prolongation than artemether-lumefantrine.
- **Effect:** adjusted predicted mean difference in QTcP (ms) 17.84 ms (DP vs AL+); 18.31 ms (chloroquine vs AL+); 2.45 ms (ASMQ vs AL+) (95% CI 11.58 to 24.10 (DP); 8.78 to 27.84 (chloroquine); -4.20 to 9.10 (ASMQ))
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 7 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33495217/

- **Impact:** This pilot dataset describes treatment outcomes and lumefantrine pharmacokinetics when AL is co-administered with ciprofloxacin for malaria-typhoid co-infection, providing baseline data to support a future randomized efficacy trial.

### Improving malaria preventive practices and pregnancy outcomes through a health education intervention: A randomized controlled trial.

- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal care

- **Impact:** The health education intervention significantly improved reported ITN use, IPTp uptake, and haematocrit levels, but had no significant effect on reported malaria diagnosis or birth weight.

### Anti-malarial efficacy and resistance monitoring of artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine shows inadequate efficacy in children in Burkina Faso, 2017-2018.

- **Impact:** Both artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine showed inadequate efficacy in some sites in Burkina Faso, with no evidence of Pfk13-associated artemisinin resistance.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected per protocol efficacy AL: 74-92%; DP: 84-97% (95% CI AL: 64-96%; DP: 75-99%)

### Small-scale field evaluation of the entomological efficacy and the residual activity of Fludora® Fusion WP-SB indoor residual spraying against Anopheles culicifacies s.l. in Gujarat, India.

- **Place:** India, Gujarat
- **Design:** randomized, small-scale field trial, entomological study
- **Status:** published

- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** households in rural villages, Anopheles culicifacies s.l. mosquito populations
- **Impact:** Fludora Fusion WP-SB showed residual efficacy lasting 7 months and performed equal or better than deltamethrin and bendiocarb alone against pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles culicifacies.

### The Duration of Protection from Azithromycin Against Malaria, Acute Respiratory, Gastrointestinal, and Skin Infections When Given Alongside Seasonal Malaria Chemoprevention: Secondary Analyses of Data from a Clinical Trial in Houndé, Burkina Faso, and Bougouni, Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33417683)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Houndé, Burkina Faso, Bougouni, Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-10-01
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** SMC; MDA; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 3-59 months receiving seasonal malaria chemoprevention
- **N:** 30977
- **Impact:** Azithromycin added to seasonal malaria chemoprevention provided broad but short-lived protection against malaria, gastroenteritis, pneumonia, and nonmalaria fevers with skin conditions, mostly within the first 2 weeks post-administration, with no evidence of protection against hospital admissions or deaths.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 24.2% (malaria, first 2 weeks); 29.9% (gastroenteritis); 34.3% (pneumonia); 46.3% (nonmalaria fever with skin condition, up to 28 days) (95% CI 17.8-30.1% (malaria); 21.7-37.3% (gastroenteritis); 14.9-49.3% (pneumonia); 35.1-55.6% (skin condition fevers))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** up to 28 days post-administration per cycle over 3 years (2014-2016)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33417683/

### Efficacy and safety of malarial prophylaxis with mefloquine during pregnancy in Kisangani, Democratic Republic of Congo: A randomized clinical trial.

- **Impact:** Intermittent preventive treatment with mefloquine (split dose with meal) significantly reduced placental malaria, maternal peripheral parasitaemia, and low birth weight compared to sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine, with similar tolerability.
- **Effect:** risk ratio (placental malaria) 0.4315 (95% CI 0.2201-0.8460)

### RTS,S/AS01E malaria vaccine induces IgA responses against CSP and vaccine-unrelated antigens in African children in the phase 3 trial.

- **Population:** children aged 5-17 months at first vaccination from RTS,S/AS01E phase 3 trial sites with differing malaria transmission intensity
- **N:** 95
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01E vaccination induced increased serum IgA responses against CSP and multiple vaccine-unrelated P. falciparum antigens compared to a comparator vaccine, but no significant association between IgA levels and protection was found.
- **Effect:** fold-increase in IgA antibody levels 1.2 to 2-fold increase in IgA to CSP constructs

### Cost-Effectiveness of PBO versus Conventional Long-Lasting Insecticidal Bed Nets in Preventing Symptomatic Malaria in Nigeria: Results of a Pragmatic Randomized Trial.

- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** village populations in areas with confirmed pyrethroid resistance
- **Impact:** PBO nets reduced symptomatic malaria cases by 33.4% compared with conventional LLINs and were highly cost-effective and cost-saving from a health systems perspective.
