### Cost-effectiveness of intermittent preventive treatment with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine versus single screening and treatment for the control of malaria in pregnancy in Papua, Indonesia: a provider perspective analysis from a cluster-randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33220216)

- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 1000
- **Impact:** Intermittent preventive treatment with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine was cost-effective compared with single screening and treatment, averting more DALYs at an incremental cost of $53 per DALY averted in a moderate transmission setting.

- **Follow-up:** lifetime horizon (modelled)

### Subsidise the test, the treatment or both? Results of an individually randomised controlled trial of the management of suspected malaria fevers in the retail sector in western Kenya.

- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic

- **Impact:** RDT uptake was high across all arms, and RDT subsidies modestly increased testing uptake, while ACT subsidy conditional on a positive RDT did not affect test purchasing behaviour.

### Evaluating the impact of larviciding with Bti and community education and mobilization as supplementary integrated vector management interventions for malaria control in Kenya and Ethiopia.

- **Status:** published

- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2015
- **Class:** larval source management

- **Impact:** Combining LLINs with larviciding using Bti and community education and mobilization significantly reduced malaria prevalence by 50% in the low-prevalence Tolay site in Ethiopia, but not in the higher-prevalence Kenyan sites, and there was no significant reduction in adult anopheline density at any site.

### Evaluating effectiveness of screening house eaves as a potential intervention for reducing indoor vector densities and malaria prevalence in Nyabondo, western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32950061)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nyabondo, western Kenya, Kenya
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, controlled trial with permuted block randomization
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-09-19
- **Population:** residents of study households in a high malaria transmission area
- **N:** 160
- **Impact:** House eave screening significantly reduced indoor vector densities and malaria parasite prevalence compared to unscreened control houses.
- **Effect:** Percentage reduction in malaria prevalence 100%, 63.6%, 100% at 1st, 2nd, and 3rd follow-up surveys respectively (95% CI 4.2-7.5)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 years (three cross-sectional surveys at 3-month intervals)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32950061/

### Comparative functional survival and equivalent annual cost of 3 long-lasting insecticidal net (LLIN) products in Tanzania: A randomised trial with 3-year follow up.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32946451)
- **Journal:** PLoS medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-09-18
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households and volunteers sleeping under LLINs
- **N:** 10571
- **Impact:** Median LLIN functional survival was significantly lower for Olyset (2.0 years) than for PermaNet 2.0 (2.5 years) and NetProtect (2.6 years), and all nets had a functional lifespan of less than 3 years, affecting cost-effectiveness.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio for net failure (functional survival) HR 0.73 (PermaNet vs Olyset); HR 0.70 (NetProtect vs Olyset) (95% CI 0.64-0.85; 0.62-0.77)
- **Follow-up:** 36 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32946451/

### Evaluation of the effectiveness of topical repellent distributed by village health volunteer networks against Plasmodium spp. infection in Myanmar: A stepped-wedge cluster randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32817632)
- **Journal:** PLoS medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Myanmar
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-20
- **Trial period:** 2015-04 to 2016-06
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; spatial repellent; diagnostic
- **Population:** village residents and forest dwellers, median age 18 years
- **N:** 116
- **Impact:** Topical repellent distributed by village health volunteers showed no significant protection against RDT-detectable infection but was associated with protection against PCR-detectable P. falciparum infection, not P. vivax.
- **Effect:** adjusted relative risk ratio (ARRR) for PCR-detectable P. falciparum infection 0.67 (95% CI 0.47-0.95)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 15 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32817632/

### A cluster randomized trial of delivery of intermittent preventive treatment of malaria in pregnancy at the community level in Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32758233)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-05
- **Trial period:** 2017 to 2018
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 734
- **Impact:** Community delivery of IPTp by community health workers increased the number of IPTp doses received and IPTp3/IPTp4 coverage compared to standard health facility delivery, without decreasing ANC coverage, though most differences were not statistically significant.
- **Effect:** Difference in differences (DiD) in coverage 17.6% (IPTp3), 20.0% (IPTp4), 12.6% (4+ ANC visits) (95% CI -16.3 to 51.5 (IPTp3); -7.2 to 47.3 (IPTp4))
- **Follow-up:** March 2017 to July-August 2018
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32758233/

### Efficacy and Safety of a Naphthoquine-Azithromycin Coformulation for Malaria Prophylaxis in Southeast Asia: A Phase 3, Double-blind, Randomized, Placebo-controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32687174)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** China-Myanmar border, Southeast Asia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-10-01
- **Population:** residents 5-65 years of age in malaria-endemic villages
- **N:** 631
- **Impact:** Monthly single-dose naphthoquine-azithromycin coformulation was well tolerated and provided over 93% protective efficacy against Plasmodium infections compared with placebo.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 93.62% (ITT), 93.04% (per-protocol) (95% CI 95% CI: 91.72%-95.52% (ITT); 90.98%-95.1% (per-protocol))
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** weekly follow-up during trial period (duration not specified beyond monthly dosing)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32687174/

### Impacts of Seasonal Malaria Chemoprevention on Malaria Burden among under Five-Year-Old Children in Borno State, Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32665781)
- **Journal:** Journal of tropical medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria (Borno State)
- **Design:** cluster-randomized
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-07-01
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 3-59 months
- **N:** 399
- **Impact:** SMC significantly reduced malaria prevalence and anaemia prevalence among children under five in Borno State, Nigeria.
- **Effect:** malaria prevalence 4.9% (SMC) vs 15.9% (non-SMC)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 weeks after 4th SMC cycle (approximately 4 months total)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32665781/

### Adherence to Mass Drug Administration with Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine and Plasmodium falciparum Clearance in Southern Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618267)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** General population in households targeted for mass drug administration
- **N:** 336821
- **Impact:** High adherence to the 3-day DHAp regimen (84.4% completion) was observed, and DHAp effectively cleared P. falciparum asexual-stage infections, with none of 45 followed participants positive at days 3/7 post-treatment.
- **Effect:** Proportion fully adhering to treatment / parasite clearance rate 84.4% adherence; 45/45 cleared at day 3/7 follow-up
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** up to 69 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618267/

### Moving from Malaria Burden Reduction toward Elimination: An Evaluation of Mass Drug Administration in Southern Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618265)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Trial period:** December 2014 to February 2016
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; IRS; diagnostic
- **Population:** households/residents in health facility catchment areas along Lake Kariba
- **N:** 60
- **Impact:** This introductory article describes a cluster RCT evaluating mass drug administration and focal mass drug administration with DHAP for malaria elimination in Southern Province, Zambia, and introduces a collection of related articles on the intervention's impact.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618265/

### Prevalence of Plasmodium falciparum and Non-falciparum Infections by Photo-Induced Electron Transfer-PCR in a Longitudinal Cohort of Individuals Enrolled in a Mass Drug Administration Trial in Southern Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618252)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Class:** MDA; ITN/LLIN; diagnostic
- **Population:** individuals enrolled in a mass drug administration trial cohort
- **Impact:** PCR testing showed Plasmodium falciparum as the dominant species (98.3% of positives), RDTs had high specificity but low sensitivity compared to PCR, and MDA/fMDA appeared to reduce P. malariae prevalence while P. ovale prevalence remained unchanged.
- **Effect:** sensitivity/specificity of RDT vs PCR sensitivity 53.0%, specificity 98.5%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618252/

### Treatment Coverage Estimation for Mass Drug Administration for Malaria with Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine in Southern Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618251)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** general population in Southern Province, Zambia
- **N:** 300000
- **Impact:** Household visit coverage ranged from 62.9% to 77.4% across four MDA rounds, with treatment coverage of 58.8% in MDA arm and 13.3% in fMDA arm based on capture-recapture estimates.
- **Effect:** treatment coverage percentage 58.8% (MDA) vs 13.3% (fMDA) (95% CI 60.0-65.8)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618251/

### Cost-Effectiveness of Focal Mass Drug Administration and Mass Drug Administration with Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine for Malaria Prevention in Southern Province, Zambia: Results of a Community-Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618249)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** community residents in malaria-endemic areas of Southern Province, Zambia
- **Impact:** Mass drug administration (MDA) with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine was more cost-effective than focal MDA and standard of care in reducing malaria in Southern Province, Zambia, and met WHO cost-effectiveness thresholds in 90% of simulations compared to about 50% for fMDA.
- **Effect:** cost per person targeted and reached (USD) fMDA US$9.01 vs MDA US$8.49
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618249/

### Impact of Four Rounds of Mass Drug Administration with Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine Implemented in Southern Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618247)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia, Southern Province
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** community members including children, in areas of varying malaria transmission
- **Impact:** Four rounds of mass drug administration with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine significantly reduced malaria parasite prevalence, especially in lower transmission areas.
- **Effect:** reduction in malaria parasite prevalence 72% (95% CI 12-91%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 14 months (December 2014 to February 2016)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618247/

### A Longitudinal Cohort to Monitor Malaria Infection Incidence during Mass Drug Administration in Southern Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618245)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Class:** MDA
- **Population:** community residents in households in Southern Province, Zambia
- **N:** 2026
- **Impact:** MDA was associated with reduced risk of first infection and reduced cumulative infection incidence during the first rainy season, but no significant effect was found for fMDA or for either arm over the full 18-month study period.
- **Effect:** hazards ratio (first infection); incidence rate ratio (cumulative infection, first rainy season) HR: 0.36; IRR: 0.34 (95% CI HR 95% CI: 0.16-0.80; IRR 95% CI: 0.12-0.95)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618245/

### Assessment of the Acceptability of Testing and Treatment during a Mass Drug Administration Trial for Malaria in Zambia Using Mixed Methods.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32618242)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-08-01
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** MDA
- **Population:** households and community members (adults and children) in health catchments
- **Impact:** The mass drug administration campaign was highly accepted, with acceptability of prophylactic treatment for malaria-negative individuals increasing markedly from baseline to follow-up.

- **Follow-up:** 2014 to 2016
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32618242/

### Efficacy and safety of fixed dose combination of arterolane maleate and piperaquine phosphate in comparison with chloroquine phosphate in children with acute uncomplicated Plasmodium vivax malaria: A phase III, randomised, multicentric study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (34472504)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** India
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-01-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 12 years with acute uncomplicated P. vivax malaria
- **N:** 164
- **Impact:** Fixed dose combination of arterolane maleate and piperaquine phosphate showed efficacy and safety comparable to chloroquine in treating uncomplicated P. vivax malaria in children.
- **Effect:** difference in proportion aparasitaemic and afebrile at 72 hours (ITT) -1.8% (95% CI -6.33 to 5.08)

### Efficacy and safety of artesunate-amodiaquine and artemether-lumefantrine and prevalence of molecular markers associated with resistance, Guinea: an open-label two-arm randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32580771)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-06-24
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 421
- **Impact:** Both artesunate-amodiaquine and artemether-lumefantrine showed high efficacy and safety with minimal molecular markers of resistance in Guinea.
- **Effect:** Day 28 corrected efficacy (Kaplan-Meier estimate) 100% (Maferinyah, both arms); 99% (Labé, both arms) (95% CI 97.2-100% (ASAQ, Labé); 97.1-100% (AL, Labé))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days

### The impact of gravidity, symptomatology and timing of infection on placental malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32580739)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-06-24
- **Population:** pregnant women receiving intermittent preventive therapy during pregnancy
- **N:** 228
- **Impact:** Primigravidity, higher number of malaria episodes, and timing of infection (varying by gravidity) were significantly associated with increased odds of placental malaria.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio aOR 8.90 (primigravidity); aOR 2.35 (per malaria episode); aOR 1.14 (per week gestation at first infection in multigravidas); aOR 12.19 (primigravidity in asymptomatic women) (95% CI 4.34-18.2; 1.69-3.26; 1.02-1.27; 5.23-28.43)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** enrollment at 12-20 weeks gestation through delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32580739/

### Epitope-based sieve analysis of Plasmodium falciparum sequences from a FMP2.1/AS02A vaccine trial is consistent with differential vaccine efficacy against immunologically relevant AMA1 variants.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32571720)
- **Journal:** Vaccine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-06-20
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** volunteers from a malaria vaccine efficacy field trial who later developed malaria
- **Impact:** Sieve analysis found a significantly greater proportion of 3D7-like T-cell epitopes mapping to the AMA1 c1L region in controls versus vaccinees, and higher IC50/lower binding affinity in vaccinee-derived epitopes, consistent with allele-specific vaccine efficacy of the FMP2.1/AS02A vaccine.
- **Effect:** proportion of 3D7-like T-cell epitopes in c1L region greater in control vs vaccinee group
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32571720/

### Antibody responses to the RTS,S/AS01E vaccine and Plasmodium falciparum antigens after a booster dose within the phase 3 trial in Mozambique.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32550014)
- **Journal:** NPJ vaccines
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Mozambique
- **Design:** phase 3, immunogenicity substudy of pediatric trial with fourth booster dose
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-06-04
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** children and infants (pediatric subjects in phase 3 trial, subset analyzed)
- **N:** 50
- **Impact:** The booster dose of RTS,S/AS01E increased IgG, IgG1, IgG3 and IgG4 (but not IgG2 or IgM) against vaccine antigens, with responses lower than the primary immunization peak, and increased some naturally acquired antibody responses such as anti-Rh5 antibodies.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 1 month post booster dose
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32550014/

### Efficacy of artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine as first line therapy of uncomplicated malaria in Burkina Faso, 11 years after policy change.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32537072)
- **Journal:** The Pan African medical journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-03-10
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** participants with uncomplicated malaria, age over 6 months
- **N:** 281
- **Impact:** Artesunate-amodiaquine showed significantly higher adequate clinical and parasitological response than artemether-lumefantrine, whose efficacy fell below the WHO threshold of 90% when uncorrected by PCR.
- **Effect:** Adequate clinical and parasitological response rate 97% (ASAQ) vs 85.2% (AL) (95% CI AL day 28 failure risk 20.14% (95% CI 13-30.47); ASAQ 5.16% (95% CI 1.91-13.54))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32537072/

### Efficacy and safety of artemisinin-based combination therapy and the implications of Pfkelch13 and Pfcoronin molecular markers in treatment failure in Senegal.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32483161)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase III
- **Place:** Senegal
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-06-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with suspected uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 496
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine, artesunate-amodiaquine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine remained highly efficacious and well tolerated for treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Senegal, with no Pfk13 or Pfcoronin resistance mutations detected.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected ACPR 98.8-100% (AL/ASAQ in Diourbel and Kedougou), 97.6% (DP in Kedougou)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days (AL, ASAQ) or 42 days (DP)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32483161/

### Protease Inhibitors to Reduce Malaria Morbidity in HIV-Infected Pregnant Women
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT00993031)
- **Journal:** Current developments in nutrition
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2020-04-25
- **Trial period:** 2009-12-15 to 2013-07
- **Intervention(s):** Lopinavir/ritonavir; Efavirenz; Zidovudine; Lamivudine
- **Population:** pregnant women living with HIV, 12-28 weeks gestation
- **N:** 389
- **Impact:** Anemia during pregnancy was associated with greater risk of small for gestational age, and higher vitamin D concentrations predicted lower risk of infant wasting, among pregnant WLHIV on cART.
- **Effect:** risk ratio 1.88 (anemia and SGA); 0.94 (vitamin D and infant wasting) (95% CI 1.28-2.77; 0.89-0.99)
- **Follow-up:** postpartum
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT00993031

### Efficacy of a Spatial Repellent for Control of Malaria in Indonesia: A Cluster-Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32431275)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Indonesia (Sumba)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-05-14
- **Trial period:** 2015 to 2018
- **Class:** spatial repellent
- **Population:** children aged ≥6 months to ≤5 years following radical cure
- **N:** 1341
- **Impact:** The spatial repellent (transfluthrin passive emanator) reduced malaria infection risk, with statistically significant protective effects observed in subgroup analyses (moderate-to-high risk and baseline-infected clusters), though the overall 24-cluster effect was not statistically significant.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 27.7% (first infection) and 31.3% (overall infections) in 24 clusters; 33.3% and 40.9% in 19 clusters; 60% in 12 moderate-to-high risk clusters
- **Follow-up:** 24 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32431275/

### Delayed iron does not alter cognition or behavior among children with severe malaria and iron deficiency.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32403115)
- **Journal:** Pediatric research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-05-13
- **Population:** children with cerebral malaria, severe malarial anemia, or community children, with iron deficiency
- **N:** 239
- **Impact:** Delaying iron therapy by 28 days after severe malaria did not worsen cognitive or behavioral outcomes compared to immediate iron therapy at 12-month follow-up.
- **Effect:** mean difference ranged from -0.2 (SEM 0.39) to 0.98 (SEM 0.5)
- **Follow-up:** 12 months

### Plasmodium falciparum Isolates Carrying pfk13 Polymorphisms Harbor the SVMNT Allele of pfcrt in Northwestern Indonesia.

- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Indonesia
- **Design:** randomized, cross-sectional survey with embedded treatment comparison, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-07-22
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** symptomatic and asymptomatic P. falciparum-infected residents of three regencies in Sumatera
- **N:** 117
- **Impact:** Current ACT regimens (dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine and artemether-lumefantrine) remain effective in Sumatera, but submicroscopic persistent parasites at day 28/42 were significantly associated with the pfmdr1 NF haplotype, suggesting emerging concerns for drug susceptibility.
- **Effect:** odds ratio (linkage disequilibrium between pfcrt SVMNT and pfmdr1 YY haplotypes) 26.7 (95% CI 5.96 to 239.4)

- **Follow-up:** 42 days

### Impact of Intermittent Mass Testing and Treatment on Incidence of Malaria Infection in a High Transmission Area of Western Kenya.

### Effects of Biannual Azithromycin Mass Drug Administration on Malaria in Malawian Children: A Cluster-Randomized Trial.

- **Population:** children aged 1-59 months
- **N:** 1200
- **Impact:** Biannual azithromycin mass drug administration did not reduce malaria parasitemia, gametocytemia, or anemia compared to placebo at either the community or individual level.

### Effect of long-lasting insecticidal nets with and without piperonyl butoxide on malaria indicators in Uganda (LLINEUP): a pragmatic, cluster-randomised trial embedded in a national LLIN distribution campaign.

### Multifunctional Antibodies Are Induced by the RTS,S Malaria Vaccine and Associated With Protection in a Phase 1/2a Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32236404)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 1/2a
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2021-10-01
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** healthy malaria-naive adults
- **N:** 45
- **Impact:** Participants with higher levels of Fc-mediated functional anti-CSP antibodies induced by RTS,S/AS01 had a reduced probability of developing parasitemia after homologous challenge, while nonprotected subjects had higher anti-CSP IgM levels.
- **Effect:** association between functional antibody levels and protection from parasitemia not specified
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32236404/

### Interactions Between Antenatal Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine, Drug-Resistant Plasmodium falciparum Parasites, and Delivery Outcomes in Malawi.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32221555)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-07-01
- **Class:** IPTp; diagnostic
- **Population:** pregnant women with placental Plasmodium falciparum infections
- **N:** 125
- **Impact:** The effectiveness of IPTp-SP in improving birth weight is compromised in women with A581G-bearing dhps resistance mutant parasites, without evidence that antenatal SP worsens the adverse effects of these parasites.
- **Effect:** mean difference in birth weight 252 g (A581G vs wild-type); interaction effect Pinteraction=.033 (95% CI 46-457)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32221555/

### A Phase II Pilot Trial to Evaluate CoBaT-Y017 Safety and Efficacy against Uncomplicated Falciparum Malaria versus Artemether-Lumefantrine in Benin Subjects.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32215047)
- **Journal:** Evidence-based complementary and alternative medicine : eCAM
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** single-blind, randomized, phase 2, pilot trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-02-17
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Adult males with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 25
- **Impact:** CoBaT-Y017 showed similar antimalarial efficacy to Artemether-Lumefantrine for parasite clearance with no statistically significant difference, and had a better safety/tolerability profile.
- **Effect:** Hazard ratio for parasite clearance 1.08 (95% CI 0.47-2.51)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32215047/

### Immunogenicity and safety of the RTS,S/AS01 malaria vaccine co-administered with measles, rubella and yellow fever vaccines in Ghanaian children: A phase IIIb, multi-center, non-inferiority, randomized, open, controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32192811)

- **Place:** Ghana

- **Status:** published

- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** infants/young children receiving EPI vaccinations at 6-10.5 months of age
- **N:** 709
- **Impact:** Co-administration of RTS,S/AS01 with Vitamin A at 6 months and with yellow fever and measles-rubella vaccines at 9 months did not impair immune responses to any vaccine antigen and had an acceptable safety profile.
- **Effect:** non-inferiority of antibody responses non-inferiority demonstrated for all antigens
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 4.5 months

### Effect of adding azithromycin to the antimalarials used for seasonal malaria chemoprevention on the nutritional status of African children.

- **Status:** published

- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** young children receiving seasonal malaria chemoprevention
- **N:** 19578
- **Impact:** Adding azithromycin to seasonal malaria chemoprevention did not improve nutritional outcomes compared to placebo in children in Burkina Faso and Mali.
- **Effect:** difference in nutritional status indicators no significant difference between arms
- **Follow-up:** three malaria transmission seasons (2014-2016)

### Does improving appropriate use of malaria medicines change population beliefs in testing and treatment? Evidence from a randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32129851)
- **Journal:** Health policy and planning
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-06-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** general population
- **Impact:** The intervention improving RDT access via community health workers and appropriate ACT use increased population beliefs that a negative test result is correct and that ACTs are effective.
- **Effect:** mediation proportion explained 62.4% (belief in negative test correctness) and 96.8% (belief in ACT effectiveness)
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32129851/

### Large-scale field trial of attractive toxic sugar baits (ATSB) for the control of malaria vector mosquitoes in Mali, West Africa.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32059671)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** cluster field trial (non-randomized allocation of villages), phase not applicable
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-02-14
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2017
- **Population:** village populations/mosquito vector populations in villages using LLINs
- **N:** 14
- **Impact:** ATSB deployment combined with LLINs drastically reduced mosquito density, proportion of older females, sporozoite infection rates, and EIR compared to control villages, indicating a significant reduction in malaria transmission.
- **Effect:** percent reduction in EIR 77.76-100.00% indoors, 84.95-100.00% outdoors
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** April 2016 to December 2017 (approximately 20 months)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32059671/

### Usefulness of day 7 lumefantrine plasma concentration as a predictor of malaria treatment outcome in under-fives children treated with artemether-lumefantrine in Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32046718)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-02-11
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated malaria aged 6-59 months
- **Impact:** Day 7 plasma lumefantrine concentration using the recommended cut-off of ≥200 ng/ml failed to predict malaria treatment outcome in under-five children treated with artemether-lumefantrine in Tanzania, while nutritional status was found to be a significant determinant of recurrent malaria.
- **Effect:** adjusted hazard ratio (nutritional status and recurrent malaria) 3 (95% CI 1.1-8.2)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32046718/

### Population pharmacokinetics of artesunate and dihydroartemisinin in pregnant and non-pregnant women with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Burkina Faso: an open label trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32025570)
- **Journal:** Wellcome open research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** open label, non-randomized comparative pharmacokinetic study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-03-07
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant (second and third trimester) and non-pregnant women with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 48
- **Impact:** Pregnant women had 21% higher elimination clearance of dihydroartemisinin than non-pregnant women, leading to substantially lower drug exposure and a potentially increased risk of treatment failure and drug resistance.
- **Effect:** difference in elimination clearance of dihydroartemisinin 21% higher in pregnant women
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32025570/

### Mass drug administrations with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine and single low dose primaquine to eliminate Plasmodium falciparum have only a transient impact on Plasmodium vivax: Findings from randomised controlled trials.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (32023293)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Myanmar, Vietnam, Cambodia, Lao PDR
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-02-05
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2017
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** residents of villages with high prevalence of P. falciparum infections
- **N:** 3790
- **Impact:** Mass drug administration with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine and single low-dose primaquine had only a transient reduction in P. vivax prevalence, with no significant reduction in cumulative incidence or recurrence.
- **Effect:** Odds ratio 0.29 to 1.36 (cumulative incidence); 0.34 (recurrent infection) (95% CI 0.29-1.36; 0.08-1.42)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** quarterly surveys over study period (2013-2017)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32023293/

### Effect of group versus individual antenatal care on uptake of intermittent prophylactic treatment of malaria in pregnancy and related malaria outcomes in Nigeria and Kenya: analysis of data from a pragmatic cluster randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31996209)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria, Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-01-29
- **Trial period:** 2017
- **Class:** IPTp; ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal care
- **N:** 1494
- **Impact:** Group antenatal care was associated with higher mean number of IPTp-SP doses received compared to individual antenatal care in both Nigeria and Kenya, and higher infant ITN use in Nigeria.
- **Effect:** mean number of IPTp doses Nigeria: 3.45 vs 2.14; Kenya: 3.81 vs 2.72
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31996209/

### Optimal timing of primaquine to reduce Plasmodium falciparum gametocyte carriage when co-administered with artemether-lumefantrine.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31964380)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-01-21
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children and adolescents with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria, G6PD normal
- **N:** 111
- **Impact:** Adding a single low-dose primaquine on the first day of artemether-lumefantrine treatment reduced day 4 gametocyte prevalence compared to adding it on the last day or not at all, though the difference was not statistically significant, and was well tolerated.
- **Effect:** gametocyte prevalence on day 4 11% (AL+day1 PQ) vs 26% (AL+day3 PQ) vs 27% (AL only)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 15 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31964380/

### Long-term immunogenicity and immune memory response to the hepatitis B antigen in the RTS,S/AS01E malaria vaccine in African children: a randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31951771)
- **Journal:** Human vaccines & immunotherapeutics
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-01-17
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** infants 8-12 weeks old
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01E vaccination effectively primed for hepatitis B, producing a strong booster response to HepB vaccine and higher anti-CS antibody levels than controls, with an acceptable safety profile.
- **Effect:** geometric mean concentration of anti-HBs antibodies post-booster 46634.7 mIU/mL (RTS,S) vs 9258.2 mIU/mL (control) (95% CI 95% CI: 40561.3-53617.6 (RTS,S); 6925.3-12377.0 (control))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 48 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31951771/

### Efficacy and safety of artesuante-amodiaquine and artemether lumefantrine, the first line malaria treatment in six sentinel's sites of Côte d'Ivoire, West Africa.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33789028)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Côte d'Ivoire
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-01-01
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients older than 6 months with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 712
- **Impact:** Both artesunate-amodiaquine and artemether-lumefantrine remained highly efficacious for treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Côte d'Ivoire, though delayed parasite clearance was observed in a few patients.
- **Effect:** Day 42 PCR-adjusted ACPR 99.4% (AS+AQ) vs 98.8% (AL)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days

### Adherence to the referral advice after introduction of rectal artesunate for pre-referral treatment of severe malaria at the community level: a noninferiority trial in the Democratic Republic of the Congo.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31864376)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Democratic Republic of the Congo
- **Design:** noninferiority community trial, pre- and post-intervention design
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-12-21
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under 5 years, their mothers/caretakers, and community health workers
- **N:** 733
- **Impact:** Adherence to referral advice after the introduction of pre-referral rectal artesunate at the community level was non-inferior to pre-intervention adherence, and the intervention was found feasible and acceptable.
- **Effect:** adherence to referral advice (%) 84.3% (post-intervention) vs 94.1% (pre-intervention) (95% CI 80.6-88.1 (post); 91.7-96.4 (pre))

### A Randomized Controlled Trial of Three- versus Five-Day Artemether-Lumefantrine Regimens for Treatment of Uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum Malaria in Pregnancy in Africa.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31818818)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-02-21
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women and nonpregnant women with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 96
- **Impact:** Both the 3-day and extended 5-day artemether-lumefantrine regimens achieved 100% PCR-corrected cure rates and were well tolerated, with the extended regimen improving overall lumefantrine drug exposure in pregnant women.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected cure rate 100%
- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Resolving the cause of recurrent Plasmodium vivax malaria probabilistically.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31811128)
- **Journal:** Nature communications
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Thailand, Myanmar
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-12-06
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with recurrent Plasmodium vivax malaria on the Thailand-Myanmar border
- **N:** 1299
- **Impact:** Without primaquine radical cure, 3 in 4 patients relapse, whereas after supervised high-dose primaquine only 1 in 40 relapse, indicating a much lower true failure rate than previously estimated.
- **Effect:** relapse rate 75% without primaquine vs 2.5% (1 in 40) with supervised high-dose primaquine
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 1000 patient follow-up years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31811128/

### Ivermectin for causal malaria prophylaxis: a randomised controlled human infection trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31808594)
- **Journal:** Tropical medicine & international health : TM & IH
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-01-02
- **Class:** MDA; endectocide
- **Population:** healthy volunteers
- **N:** 12
- **Impact:** Ivermectin at 0.4 mg/kg had no significant effect on time to parasitaemia, indicating no clinically relevant prophylactic activity against pre-erythrocytic stages of P. falciparum.
- **Effect:** time to parasitaemia no significant difference between ivermectin and placebo
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** until day 11-12 post infection
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31808594/

### Gametocyte clearance in children, from western Kenya, with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria after artemether-lumefantrine or dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine treatment.

- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 0.5 to 12 years with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 334
- **Impact:** No significant difference was found between artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine in clearing gametocytes, with gametocytes persisting up to 42 days in a minority of children in both arms.
- **Effect:** mean duration of gametocyte carriage 4.5 days (AL) vs 5.1 days (DP)

### Biannual versus annual mass azithromycin distribution and malaria seroepidemiology among preschool children in Niger: a sub-study of a cluster randomized trial.

- **Class:** monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** preschool children ages 1-5 years
- **N:** 991
- **Impact:** Targeted biannual azithromycin distribution was associated with lower malaria seroprevalence (62.7%) compared to annual distribution (68.7%).

### Piperaquine concentration and malaria treatment outcomes in Ugandan children treated for severe malaria with intravenous Artesunate or quinine plus Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine.

- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-12-03
- **Trial period:** 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children treated for severe malaria
- **N:** 150
- **Impact:** Children with low day 7 capillary piperaquine concentration (<57 ng/mL) had a two-fold higher risk of treatment failure compared to those with higher concentrations.
- **Effect:** relative risk 2.1 (95% CI 1.4-3.1)
- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Assessing the impact of the addition of pyriproxyfen on the durability of permethrin-treated bed nets in Burkina Faso: a compound-randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31791332)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-12-02
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households in rural villages receiving bed nets
- **N:** 1199
- **Impact:** The PPF-permethrin net (Olyset Duo) maintained WHO bioefficacy criteria for 18 months versus 6 months for the standard LLIN, but both net types had poor survivorship and failed to meet WHO bioefficacy criteria by 3 years.
- **Effect:** difference in mean mosquito mortality 8.6% higher for PPF-permethrin nets (95% CI 2.6-14.6%)
- **Species:** Anopheles gambiae s.l. (vector); malaria species not specified
- **Follow-up:** 36 months

### Health education through mass media announcements by loudspeakers about malaria care: prevention and practice among people living in a malaria endemic area of northern Myanmar.

- **Type:** trial

- **Population:** people living in malaria endemic villages
- **N:** 270
- **Impact:** Loudspeaker health education announcements significantly improved malaria prevention and care-seeking practice scores and were associated with a declining trend in malaria morbidity compared to control villages.
- **Effect:** change in KAP scores greater improvement in intervention group vs control

### Counter-Selection of Antimalarial Resistance Polymorphisms by Intermittent Preventive Treatment in Pregnancy.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31677349)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Benin, Gabon, Kenya, Mozambique
- **Design:** randomized clinical trials, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2020-01-01
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2013
- **Class:** IPTp

- **N:** 914
- **Impact:** IPTp-mefloquine selected for lower prevalence of the pfmdr1 86Y polymorphism compared with IPTp-SP in HIV-uninfected women, while other resistance markers were similar between groups and no effect was seen in HIV-infected women, with no association between resistance markers and pregnancy outcomes.
- **Effect:** prevalence of pfmdr1 86Y polymorphism lower in IPTp-MQ group than IPTp-SP group

### Effect of Metofluthrin-Impregnated Spatial Repellent Devices Combined with New Long-Lasting Insecticidal Nets (Olyset® Plus) on Pyrethroid-Resistant Malaria Vectors and Malaria Prevalence: Field Trial in South-Eastern Malawi.

- **Impact:** Combined use of metofluthrin-impregnated spatial repellent devices with Olyset Plus LLINs reduced malaria infection rate in children and decreased the number of pyrethroid-resistant vector mosquitoes.

### Atovaquone-Proguanil in Combination With Artesunate to Treat Multidrug-Resistant P. falciparum Malaria in Cambodia: An Open-Label Randomized Trial.

- **Impact:** Atovaquone-proguanil with or without artesunate remained marginally effective (~90% efficacy) in Cambodia, with artesunate co-administration providing little additional blood-stage efficacy but reducing post-treatment gametocyte carriage.

### Impact of the addition of azithromycin to antimalarials used for seasonal malaria chemoprevention on antimicrobial resistance of Streptococcus pneumoniae.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31655020)
- **Journal:** Tropical medicine & international health : TM & IH
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial, placebo-controlled
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-11-13
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2018
- **Class:** SMC
- **Population:** children under seasonal malaria chemoprevention
- **N:** 5482
- **Impact:** Addition of azithromycin to seasonal malaria chemoprevention antimalarials was associated with a marked increase in pneumococcal resistance to azithromycin and erythromycin that persisted one year after the last dose.
- **Effect:** percentage of azithromycin-resistant pneumococcal isolates Burkina Faso: 4.9% to 25.6%; Mali: 7.6% to 68.5% (95% CI Burkina Faso pre: 2.4-9.9%, post: 17.6-35.7%; Mali pre: 3.8-14.4%, post: 55.1-79.4%)
- **Follow-up:** 1 year after stopping intervention

### Methylene Blue Against Falciparum Malaria in Burkina Faso

- **Trial period:** 2016-10 to 2016-12

- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated falciparum malaria, 6-59 months
- **N:** 100
- **Impact:** Methylene blue combined with AS-AQ could not be shown to be non-inferior to primaquine combined with AS-AQ for haemoglobin recovery at day 7, but MB cleared asexual parasites and gametocytes faster than PQ.
- **Effect:** mean haemoglobin difference between treatment groups at day 7 -0.352 g/dl (95% CI -0.832 to 0.128)

### Pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics of chloroquine and artemisinin-based combination therapy with primaquine.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31547827)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Brazil
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-09-23
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adult patients with acute vivax malaria
- **Impact:** All ACT and chloroquine regimens combined with short-course primaquine were well tolerated and effective, with most recurrences due to relapse of homologous hypnozoites and no clear association between drug exposure of the long-acting schizonticidal component and risk of relapse, though impaired CYP2D6 activity was more common among patients with relapse.
- **Effect:** Relative risk of related relapse (impaired vs normal CYP2D6 activity) RR = 1.23 (95% CI 0.88-1.72)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 63 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31547827/

### Safety and tolerability of artesunate-amodiaquine, artemether-lumefantrine and quinine plus clindamycin in the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Kinshasa, the Democratic Republic of the Congo.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31527899)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kinshasa, Democratic Republic of the Congo
- **Design:** randomized clinical trial, open-label (multi-arm, sequential treatment cohorts)
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-09-17
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 12 to 59 months with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 865
- **Impact:** Drug-related adverse events were common across treatment arms (54.7% ASAQ, 21.5% AL, 40.0% QnC, p<0.001) but did not increase over repeated ASAQ treatment courses, and no serious adverse events were judged related to study medication.
- **Effect:** proportion with at least one drug-related adverse event 54.7% (ASAQ) vs 21.5% (AL) vs 40.0% (QnC)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days (preRCT), 28 days (RCT and postRCT phases)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31527899/

### Reduction of malaria vector mosquitoes in a large-scale intervention trial in rural Burkina Faso using Bti based larval source management.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31521176)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** large-scale intervention trial, regression model, cluster-based comparison of two larviciding strategies
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-09-14
- **Class:** IPTp; IRS; larval source management
- **Population:** rural villages with malaria vector mosquito populations (female Anopheles spp.)
- **Impact:** Larval source management with Bti substantially reduced female Anopheles mosquito abundance, with greater reductions achieved when all breeding sites were treated compared to selective treatment of the most productive sites.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in female Anopheles spp. abundance 61% (selective treatment), 70% (comprehensive treatment) (95% CI 54-66% (selective); 64-74% (comprehensive))
- **Follow-up:** 2 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31521176/

### Effectiveness of intermittent screening and treatment for the control of malaria in pregnancy: a cluster randomised trial in India.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31406586)
- **Journal:** BMJ global health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** India
- **Design:** cluster randomised controlled trial, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-07-29
- **Trial period:** 2012 to 2015
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** pregnant women of all parities with gestational age 18-28 weeks
- **N:** 6868
- **Impact:** ISTp detected more infections than PCD but did not reduce the prevalence of placental malaria or improve pregnancy outcomes compared to passive case detection.
- **Effect:** Odds ratio for placental malaria (ISTp vs PCD) 1.34 (95% CI 0.78 to 2.29)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** Until delivery/birth outcomes assessed
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31406586/

### A Randomized Open-Label Evaluation of the Antimalarial Prophylactic Efficacy of Azithromycin-Piperaquine versus Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine in Pregnant Papua New Guinean Women.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31405866)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** open-label, randomized, parallel-group, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-09-23
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 122
- **Impact:** AZ-PQ showed no significant difference from SP in preventing malaria parasitemia up to day 42 or at delivery, was well tolerated (though with more gastrointestinal side effects and dizziness), and was associated with higher live birth weight.
- **Effect:** relative risk (blood smear positivity at delivery) 0.11 (95% CI 0.01 to 2.01)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days and at delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31405866/

### Declining responsiveness of childhood Plasmodium falciparum infections to artemisinin-based combination treatments ten years following deployment as first-line antimalarials in Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31383040)
- **Journal:** Infectious diseases of poverty
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial, multi-site, post-hoc comparative analysis, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-08-06
- **Trial period:** 2009 to 2015
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under 5 years and children under 16 years with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 1701
- **Impact:** Ten years after adoption of ACTs as first-line therapy in Nigeria, parasitological responses to artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine have significantly declined, with increased parasite positivity, clearance time, and recurrent infections over time.
- **Effect:** Asexual parasite positivity 2 days after treatment initiation (APPD2) increased from 5% to 26% (95% CI 1.9-2.0)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31383040/

### Efficacy and safety of intermittent preventive treatment and intermittent screening and treatment versus single screening and treatment with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine for the control of malaria in pregnancy in Indonesia: a cluster-randomised, open-label, superiority trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31353217)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Indonesia (Sumba and Papua)
- **Design:** cluster-randomised, open-label, three-arm, superiority trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-07-25
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2016
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** pregnant women, 16-30 weeks pregnant
- **N:** 2279
- **Impact:** Both IPT and IST with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine were associated with lower malaria prevalence at delivery compared with single screening and treatment, but IST results were confounded by lower baseline prevalence.
- **Effect:** relative risk IPT vs SST: RR 0.59; IST vs SST: RR 0.56 (95% CI IPT: 0.42-0.83; IST: 0.40-0.77)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31353217/

### An Extension to Study MALARIA-055 PRI (NCT00866619) to Evaluate the Long-term Efficacy, Safety and Immunogenicity of GSK Biologicals' Candidate Malaria Vaccine in Infants and Children in Africa
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT02207816)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Kenya, Tanzania
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2019-07-09
- **Trial period:** 2014-09-18 to 2017-01-31
- **Intervention(s):** Blood sampling; Malaria Vaccine 257049 (MALARIA-055 PRI); Meningococcal C Conjugate Vaccine (MALARIA-055 PRI); Cell-culture rabies vaccine (MALARIA-055 PRI); TritanrixHepB/Hib (MALARIA-055 PRI); Polio Sabin Oral Polio Vaccine (GSK) (MALARIA-055 PRI)
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** Infants aged 6-12 weeks and children aged 5-17 months at initial vaccination, followed as older children (5-7 years) and younger children (3-5 years) in this extension
- **N:** 3084
- **Impact:** Over the 3-year extension follow-up, severe malaria incidence remained low across all groups with no evidence of rebound in RTS,S/AS01 recipients, though clinical malaria incidence was higher in older RTS,S/AS01 recipients compared with controls in Nanoro.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy against severe malaria 53.7% (four-dose, older children); 23.3% (three-dose, older children); 32.1% (four-dose, younger children); 37.6% (three-dose, younger children) (95% CI -13.7 to 81.1; -67.1 to 64.8; -53.1 to 69.9; -44.4 to 73.0)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 additional years (up to 6-7 years postprimary vaccination)
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02207816

### The Advanced Development Pathway of the RTS,S/AS01 Vaccine.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31267502)
- **Journal:** Methods in molecular biology (Clifton, N.J.)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Africa
- **Design:** cluster randomized, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-01-01
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** young children and infants
- **N:** 15459
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01 showed 28% efficacy without booster and 36% with booster against uncomplicated malaria in children over a median follow-up of 48 and 38 months.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy 28% without booster, 36% with booster
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 48 months (children), 38 months (infants)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31267502/

### Clinical evaluation of the use of an mhealth intervention on quality of care provided by Community Health Workers in southwest Niger.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31263555)
- **Journal:** Journal of global health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Niger
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-06-01
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2016
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** Community Health Workers (Relais Communautaire) assessing children under five presenting with diarrhea, malaria and pneumonia
- **N:** 130
- **Impact:** mHealth-equipped Community Health Workers showed modestly improved quality of care, particularly better assessment of danger signs and referral decisions, but no significant improvement in actual treatment of malaria, pneumonia and diarrhea compared to paper-based controls.
- **Effect:** mean difference in QoC score 3.4% higher QoC score (mean difference of 0.83 points)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** March to October 2016
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31263555/

### Safety and tolerability of single low-dose primaquine in a low-intensity transmission area in South Africa: an open-label, randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31234865)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** South Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-06-24
- **Trial period:** 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 140
- **Impact:** Single low-dose primaquine added to artemether-lumefantrine was safe and well tolerated, with no significant difference in haemoglobin drop or adverse events compared to artemether-lumefantrine alone, supporting deployment without G6PD testing.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response rate 100%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31234865/

### A seven-year study on the effect of the pre-erythrocytic malaria vaccine candidate RTS,S/AS01 E on blood stage immunity in young Kenyan children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31168483)
- **Journal:** Wellcome open research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2b
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized, phase 2b, long-term follow-up (7-year study)
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-03-05
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** young children aged 5-17 months at enrollment, followed for 7 years
- **N:** 314
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01 E recipients had slightly lower antibody levels to blood-stage antigens (MSP1 42, AMA1, EBA175) than Rabies-vaccine controls during the first 48 months, and this reduction persisted for several years, but anti-merozoite antibody levels were unreliable correlates of clinical immunity in the absence of asymptomatic infection.
- **Effect:** antibody levels (IgG) to blood-stage antigens slightly lower in RTS,S/AS01 E arm vs Rabies control during first 48 months
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31168483/

### A randomized controlled trial of azithromycin and sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine as prophylaxis against malaria in pregnancy among human immunodeficiency virus-positive women.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31140565)
- **Journal:** Transactions of the Royal Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Ibadan, Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-08-01
- **Population:** HIV-positive pregnant women
- **N:** 140
- **Impact:** Azithromycin was comparable to sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine in preventing malaria parasitaemia at delivery among HIV-positive pregnant women, with no significant difference between groups.
- **Effect:** relative risk 0.89 (95% CI 0.28 to 2.82)
- **Follow-up:** from enrollment at 16 weeks gestation to delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31140565/

### Intermittent screening and treatment with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine and intermittent preventive therapy with sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine have similar effects on malaria antibody in pregnant Malawian women.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31133672)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-05-27
- **Trial period:** 2011
- **Class:** monoclonal antibody; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 681
- **Impact:** IST-DP and IPT-SP had similar effects on the development and maintenance of malaria antibody immunity in pregnant Malawian women, with minimal impact of treatment arm overall.
- **Effect:** antibody response change (no significant difference between arms)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** enrolment to delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31133672/

### Impact of combining indoor residual spraying and long-lasting insecticidal nets on Anopheles arabiensis in Ethiopia: results from a cluster randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31126286)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ethiopia (Adami Tullu district, south-central Ethiopia)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-05-24
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** villages/households in a malaria-endemic district; Anopheles arabiensis mosquito populations monitored
- **Impact:** Combining IRS and LLINs was as effective as IRS alone but more effective than LLINs alone in reducing Anopheles arabiensis densities and human biting rates.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio not specified numerically
- **Species:** P. falciparum (implied, none detected in mosquitoes)
- **Follow-up:** 2014-2016 malaria transmission seasons
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31126286/

### Concentration and avidity of antibodies to different circumsporozoite epitopes correlate with RTS,S/AS01E malaria vaccine efficacy.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31092823)
- **Journal:** Nature communications
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Tanzania, Burkina Faso, Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-05-15
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** African children and infants
- **N:** 1028
- **Impact:** Higher post-vaccination IgG magnitude to NANP and avidity to the C-terminus of CSP were significantly associated with RTS,S/AS01E vaccine efficacy against clinical malaria.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio 0.61 (NANP IgG magnitude); 0.07 (C-terminus avidity) (95% CI 0.48-0.76; 0.05-0.90)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31092823/

### Contribution of Functional Antimalarial Immunity to Measures of Parasite Clearance in Therapeutic Efficacy Studies of Artemisinin Derivatives.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31075171)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Southeast Asia (11 sites)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-08-01
- **Class:** monoclonal antibody; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with P. falciparum malaria receiving artesunate therapy
- **Impact:** Higher levels of IgG3, complement (C1q) fixation, and opsonic phagocytosis against merozoite antigens were associated with faster parasite clearance during artesunate therapy, varying by transmission intensity.
- **Effect:** mean reduction in parasite clearance half-life 0.47-1.16 hours
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 days post-treatment
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31075171/

### The impact of maternal antenatal treatment with two doses of azithromycin and monthly sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine on child weight, mid-upper arm circumference and head circumference: A randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31063503)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-05-07
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women and their children followed from birth to 5 years of age
- **N:** 1320
- **Impact:** IPTp with azithromycin and monthly sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine had a modest, 3-5-year positive impact on child weight, mid-upper arm circumference and head circumference, but not on weight-for-height Z-score, compared to control.
- **Effect:** difference in means (weight) and hazard ratio (underweight incidence) 0.12 kg at 1 month; 0.19 kg at 6 months; hazard ratio 0.74 for underweight by 5 years (95% CI 0.04-0.20; 0.05-0.33; 0.60-0.90)
- **Follow-up:** 60 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31063503/

### Safety profile of the RTS,S/AS01 malaria vaccine in infants and children: additional data from a phase III randomized controlled trial in sub-Saharan Africa.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (31012786)
- **Journal:** Human vaccines & immunotherapeutics
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** sub-Saharan Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-04-23
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** infants (6-12 weeks) and children (5-17 months)
- **N:** 15459
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01 vaccination was generally well tolerated but showed numerical imbalances in meningitis and cerebral malaria cases in children and higher all-cause mortality in vaccinated girls, considered likely chance findings warranting further evaluation.
- **Effect:** incidence of serious adverse events 24.2%-28.4% SAE incidence; 1.5%-2.5% fatal SAE incidence across groups
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31012786/

### Long-lasting insecticidal nets and indoor residual spraying may not be sufficient to eliminate malaria in a low malaria incidence area: results from a cluster randomized controlled trial in Ethiopia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30999957)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ethiopia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-04-18
- **Trial period:** 2014-09 to 2017-01
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** all age groups residing in surveyed households
- **N:** 34548
- **Impact:** Combining LLINs and IRS did not provide additional protection against malaria compared to LLINs or IRS alone or control, with similar clinical malaria incidence and anaemia prevalence across all four arms.
- **Effect:** malaria incidence per 1000 person-years 17.2 (LLIN+IRS), 16.1 (LLIN), 17.0 (IRS), 15.6 (control)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 121 weeks

### Population pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of the artesunate-mefloquine fixed dose combination for the treatment of uncomplicated falciparum malaria in African children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30999915)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-04-18
- **Trial period:** 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 472
- **Impact:** Population PK models showed large variability in AS/DHA and MQ exposure driven mainly by body weight, which is accounted for by the ASMQ fixed dose combination dosing, and no association was found between predicted MQ AUC and recrudescence.
- **Effect:** association between MQ AUC and recrudescence no association found

- **Published:** 2019-06-01
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** adults, adolescents, children, and infants
- **N:** 93
- **Impact:** PfSPZ Vaccine was safe and well tolerated across all age groups with no significant differences in adverse events, and immune responses differed by age, with infants showing highest antibody but no significant T-cell responses.

### Comparison of malaria treatment outcome of generic and innovator's anti-malarial drugs containing artemether-lumefantrine combination in the management of uncomplicated malaria amongst Tanzanian children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30975147)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-04-11
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6 to 59 months with uncomplicated malaria
- **N:** 200
- **Impact:** Generic Artefan and innovator Coartem showed equivalent and effective treatment outcomes for uncomplicated malaria in Tanzanian children, with no statistically significant difference in cure rates.
- **Effect:** PCR uncorrected cure rate 80% (Artefan) vs 75% (Coartem)
- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Phase II Efficacy Study of Artefenomel & Piperaquine in Adults & Children With P. Falciparum Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT02083380)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2/3
- **Place:** Benin, Burkina Faso, Democratic Republic of the Congo, Gabon, Mozambique, Uganda, Vietnam
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2019-04-10
- **Trial period:** 2014-07 to 2015-10
- **Intervention(s):** Artefenomel 800mg: piperaquine 640mg; Artefenomel 800mg: piperaquine 960mg; Artefenomel 800mg: piperaquine 1440mg
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adults and children with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 448
- **Impact:** The study evaluated single-dose artefenomel-piperaquine efficacy and found molecular markers of multidrug resistance, including kelch13 mutations in Vietnam and high proportions of Pfmdr1 and Pfpm2 gene amplifications in African isolates, raising concerns about emerging piperaquine resistance.
- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Differential Patterns of IgG Subclass Responses to Plasmodium falciparum Antigens in Relation to Malaria Protection and RTS,S Vaccination.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30930896)
- **Journal:** Frontiers in immunology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Ghana, Mozambique
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-03-15
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** African children and infants
- **Impact:** IgG3 and IgG1 cytophilic antibody responses to specific P. falciparum antigens were associated with protection from malaria while IgG2 was associated with malaria risk, and RTS,S vaccination altered naturally acquired immunity, affecting vaccine efficacy.
- **Effect:** association with protection
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 1 month after primary vaccination

### The prevalence and antifolate drug resistance profiles of Plasmodium falciparum in study participants randomized to discontinue or continue cotrimoxazole prophylaxis.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30897090)
- **Journal:** PLoS neglected tropical diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-03-21
- **Population:** HIV-infected individuals on antiretroviral treatment
- **Impact:** Discontinuing cotrimoxazole prophylaxis was associated with significantly higher prevalence of SP-resistance associated mutant haplotypes in P. falciparum compared to continuing cotrimoxazole, suggesting cotrimoxazole selects against SP-resistant parasites.
- **Effect:** prevalence of quintuple mutant haplotype (51I/59R/108N/437G/540E) 51.8% in STOP-CTX vs. 6.3% in CTX arm
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30897090/

### Efficacy of artemether-lumefantrine versus dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine for the treatment of uncomplicated malaria among children in Rwanda: an open-label, randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30892640)
- **Journal:** Transactions of the Royal Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Rwanda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-06-01
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2015
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated malaria
- **N:** 534
- **Impact:** Both artemether-lumefantrine and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine were highly efficacious in treating uncomplicated malaria in Rwandan children, with DHP showing a greater post-treatment prophylactic effect against new infections.
- **Effect:** PCR-adjusted ACPR proportion AL 98.3% vs DHP 98.9% at day 28; AL 97.3% vs DHP 98.4% at day 42
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days

### Efficacy of Primaquine in Preventing Short- and Long-Latency Plasmodium vivax Relapses in Nepal.

- **Impact:** Chloroquine plus 14-day primaquine significantly reduced P. vivax recurrences compared with chloroquine alone over 1 year of follow-up.

### Repeat Ivermectin Mass Drug Administrations for Control of Malaria: a Pilot Safety and Efficacy Study
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT02509481)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
