- **Phase:** Phase 2/3
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, United States
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2019-03-14
- **Trial period:** 2015-06 to 2015-11
- **Intervention(s):** Ivermectin; Albendazole
- **Class:** MDA; endectocide
- **Population:** village residents at least 90 cm in height; active case detection cohort of children aged 5 years or younger
- **N:** 2712
- **Impact:** Repeated mass administrations of ivermectin reduced cumulative malaria incidence among young children compared with a single dose, without significantly increasing harms.
- **Effect:** risk difference in episodes per child -0.49 (2.00 vs 2.49 episodes per child) (95% CI -0.79 to -0.21)
- **Follow-up:** 18 weeks
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02509481

### Seasonal malaria chemoprevention combined with community case management of malaria in children under 10 years of age, over 5 months, in south-east Senegal: A cluster-randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30865632)
- **Journal:** PLoS medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Senegal
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-03-13
- **Trial period:** 2011 to 2011
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children under 10 years of age (3 months to 9 years)
- **N:** 4546
- **Impact:** Seasonal malaria chemoprevention with community case management for children under 10 years given over 5 months substantially reduced malaria episodes, parasitaemia prevalence, and improved haemoglobin concentration compared to community case management alone.
- **Effect:** rate difference (malaria cases per 1,000 child-months) 110.8/1,000/month (under 5 years); 101.3/1,000/month (5-9 years) (95% CI 64.7-156.8 (under 5); 66.7-136.0 (5-9 years))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 5 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30865632/

### Maternal vitamin A supplementation delays time to first episode of parasitaemia and reduces malaria parasite densities among infants in rural communities in Ebonyi State, Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (33597439)
- **Journal:** Tropical biomedicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ebonyi State, Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized, placebo-controlled, mother-infant dyad field trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-03-01
- **Population:** pregnant women (at least 6 months gestation) and their infants up to 12 months old
- **N:** 152
- **Impact:** Maternal vitamin A supplementation delayed the time to first parasitaemia episode and reduced malaria parasite densities in infants compared to placebo.
- **Effect:** difference in parasite density and time to first parasitaemia
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33597439/

### Effects of a health educational intervention on malaria knowledge, motivation, and behavioural skills: a randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30786906)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-02-20
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT; ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal care
- **N:** 372
- **Impact:** The health educational intervention significantly increased knowledge, motivation, and behavioural skills scores related to malaria prevention among pregnant women compared to the control group.
- **Effect:** percentage difference in total scores between intervention and control groups 12.75% (knowledge), 8.55% (motivation), 6.35% (behavioural skills)
- **Follow-up:** 4 months post-intervention
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30786906/

### Malaria preventive behaviors among housewives in suburbs of Bandar-Abbas City, south of Iran: interventional design based on PRECEDE model.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30784362)
- **Journal:** Pathogens and global health
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Bandar-Abbas, Iran
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-02-20
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2016
- **Population:** housewives in suburbs of Bandar-Abbas City
- **N:** 172
- **Impact:** The PRECEDE model-based health education significantly improved knowledge, attitude, enabling and reinforcing factors, and malaria preventive behaviors among housewives compared to controls.
- **Effect:** mean score difference
- **Follow-up:** 2 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30784362/

### Sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine plus azithromycin may improve birth outcomes through impacts on inflammation and placental angiogenesis independent of malarial infection.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30783215)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-02-19
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 2012
- **Impact:** SPAZ was associated with lower delivery inflammatory markers (AGP and CRP) and may protect against adverse pregnancy outcomes by reducing inflammation and placental angiogenesis dysregulation, independent of malarial infection status.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio 1.52 (CRP-preterm birth, SPCQ); 4.35 (sEng-preterm birth, SPCQ); 2.21 (sFlt1-preterm birth, SPCQ); 1.39 (sEng-low birthweight, SPCQ); 1.46 (sFlt1:PlGF ratio-low birthweight, all women) (95% CI 1.03-2.25; 1.77-10.7; 1.09-4.48; 1.11-3.37; 1.11-1.90)
- **Follow-up:** from antenatal enrolment to delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30783215/

### Transmission-blocking Effects of Primaquine and Methylene Blue Suggest Plasmodium falciparum Gametocyte Sterilization Rather Than Effects on Sex Ratio.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30753355)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-09-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with Plasmodium falciparum gametocytemia
- **Impact:** Primaquine and methylene blue block malaria transmission by sterilizing gametocytes before affecting gametocyte density or sex ratio, rather than by skewing the sex ratio.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30753355/

### Adherence to intermittent preventive treatment for malaria in Papua New Guinean infants: A pharmacological study alongside the randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30726224)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-02-06
- **Class:** PMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** infants receiving intermittent preventive treatment for malaria during first year of life
- **N:** 206
- **Impact:** Pharmacokinetic analysis showed that only 39-50% of children in the SP-AQ arm received all three scheduled doses of amodiaquine as prescribed, indicating low medication adherence for the self-administered doses given by parents at home.
- **Effect:** proportion of children receiving full 3-dose regimen 39-50%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30726224/

### Optimal dosing of dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine for seasonal malaria chemoprevention in young children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30696903)
- **Journal:** Nature communications
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-01-29
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** young children aged 2.33-58.1 months
- **N:** 179
- **Impact:** Population-based simulations suggest that increasing dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine dosage and extending to four monthly doses could reduce malaria incidence by up to 58% during the high transmission season, especially benefiting small children who have lower drug exposures at standard dosing.
- **Effect:** predicted relative reduction in malaria incidence up to 58%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30696903/

### Seasonal malaria chemoprevention packaged with malnutrition prevention in northern Nigeria: A pragmatic trial (SMAMP study) with nested case-control.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30682069)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria (Madobi, Kano State)
- **Design:** pragmatic trial with nested case-control study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-01-25
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2014
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-24 months
- **N:** 7000
- **Impact:** Coverage of SMC was high regardless of LNS integration; LNS did not improve nutritional outcomes but SP-AQ (with or without LNS) reduced the odds of clinical malaria compared to no intervention.
- **Effect:** odds ratio SP-AQ only OR=0.23; SP-AQ plus LNS OR=0.22 (95% CI 0.09-0.6 (SP-AQ only); 0.09-0.55 (SP-AQ plus LNS))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** August to November 2014 (approximately 4 months)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30682069/

### Artemisia annua and Artemisia afra tea infusions vs. artesunate-amodiaquine (ASAQ) in treating Plasmodium falciparum malaria in a large scale, double blind, randomized clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30668322)
- **Journal:** Phytomedicine : international journal of phytotherapy and phytopharmacology
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Democratic Republic of Congo
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-12-05
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** malaria-infected patients (pediatric and adult)
- **N:** 957
- **Impact:** Artemisia annua and Artemisia afra tea infusions achieved higher D28 cure rates and fewer adverse effects than artesunate-amodiaquine (ASAQ) in treating P. falciparum malaria.
- **Effect:** D28 cure rate Pediatrics: 82% (A. afra), 91% (A. annua), 50% (ASAQ); Adults: 91% (A. afra), 100% (A. annua), 30% (ASAQ)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30668322/

### Susceptibility of Plasmodium falciparum to artemisinins and Plasmodium vivax to chloroquine in Phuoc Chien Commune, Ninh Thuan Province, south-central Vietnam.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30654808)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Vietnam
- **Design:** randomized, therapeutic efficacy study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-01-17
- **Trial period:** 2015 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with falciparum or vivax malaria
- **N:** 43
- **Impact:** Falciparum parasites remained artemisinin susceptible with 100% curative rate, while chloroquine treatment for vivax malaria had a 13.3% clinical failure rate.
- **Effect:** cure rate / failure rate 100% curative rate (falciparum); 13.3% failure rate (vivax)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 42 days (falciparum), 28 days (vivax)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30654808/

### Study to Assess the Incidence of Hemolysis, Safety, and Efficacy of Tafenoquine (SB-252263, WR238605) Versus Primaquine in Subjects With Plasmodium Vivax Malaria
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT02216123)
- **Journal:** The New England journal of medicine
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Brazil, Colombia, Ethiopia, Peru, Thailand, Vietnam
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2019-01-01
- **Trial period:** 2015-04-30 to 2016-11-04
- **Intervention(s):** Tafenoquine; Tafenoquine Placebo; Chloroquine; Primaquine; Primaquine Placebo
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with confirmed P. vivax parasitemia and normal G6PD enzyme activity, plus female patients with moderate G6PD deficiency
- **N:** 251
- **Impact:** Tafenoquine showed efficacy for radical cure of P. vivax malaria but was not shown to be noninferior to primaquine, with similar hemolysis safety outcomes between groups.
- **Effect:** odds ratio for recurrence (tafenoquine vs. primaquine) 1.81 (95% CI 0.82 to 3.96)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 180 days (6 months)
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02216123

### Ph 2B/3 Tafenoquine (TFQ) Study in Prevention of Vivax Relapse
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT01376167)
- **Journal:** The New England journal of medicine
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Bangladesh, Brazil, Cambodia, Ethiopia, India, Peru, Philippines, Thailand
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2019-01-01
- **Trial period:** 2014-04-24 to 2016-11-18
- **Intervention(s):** Chloroquine 600mg; Chloroquine 300mg; Tafenoquine 50mg; Tafenoquine 100mg; Tafenoquine 300mg; Tafenoquine 600mg; Primaquine 15mg; Chloroquine 600mg (Part 2 ); Chloroquine 300mg (Part 2 ); Tafenoquine 300mg (Part 2); Primaquine 15mg (Part2 )
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with microscopically confirmed P. vivax infection and normal G6PD activity
- **N:** 851
- **Impact:** Single-dose tafenoquine resulted in a significantly lower risk of P. vivax recurrence than placebo in patients with phenotypically normal G6PD activity.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio for risk of recurrence (tafenoquine vs placebo) 0.30 (95% CI 0.22 to 0.40)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01376167

### Comparison of the effectiveness of two-dose versus three-dose sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine in preventing adverse pregnancy outcomes in Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30618445)
- **Journal:** Journal of vector borne diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-01-01
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women with gestational ages of 16-24 weeks
- **N:** 210
- **Impact:** Three-dose SP for IPTp reduced peripheral and placental parasitaemia and low birth weight compared to two-dose SP, though maternal anaemia, preterm birth, clinical malaria and adverse effects were similar between groups.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio 0.15 (peripheral parasitaemia and LBW), 0.17 (placental parasitaemia) (95% CI 0.05-0.45 (peripheral parasitaemia); 0.06-0.51 (placental parasitaemia); 0.04-0.63 (LBW))
- **Follow-up:** until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30618445/

### Differential infectivity of gametocytes after artemisinin-based combination therapy of uncomplicated falciparum malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30568901)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomised controlled trial
- **Status:** published

- **Trial period:** 2005 to 2007
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** volunteers with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine significantly increased gametocyte infectivity to Anopheles gambiae, while artesunate-sulfadoxine/pyrimethamine decreased infectivity.
- **Effect:** gametocyte infectivity to Anopheles gambiae increased infectivity (AL, AS-AQ) vs decreased infectivity (AS-SP)

### The effect of mobile phone text message reminders on health workers' adherence to case management guidelines for malaria and other diseases in Malawi: lessons from qualitative data from a cluster-randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30567603)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** cluster-randomized trial with qualitative sub-study (in-depth interviews, thematic and framework analysis)
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-12-19
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** health workers (clinicians and drug dispensers) in health facilities managing childhood illnesses including malaria
- **N:** 105
- **Impact:** Text-message reminders were well accepted by health workers but had little effect on adherence to case management guidelines due to health systems barriers such as high workload, stock-outs, and lack of supervision.
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30567603/

### Controlled Human Malaria Infection of Healthy Adults With Lifelong Malaria Exposure to Assess Safety, Immunogenicity, and Efficacy of the Asexual Blood Stage Malaria Vaccine Candidate GMZ2.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30561539)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Gabon
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-09-01
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** healthy adults with lifelong malaria exposure
- **N:** 50
- **Impact:** GMZ2 vaccination was well tolerated and immunogenic but showed no protective efficacy against malaria upon controlled human malaria infection, though baseline vaccine-specific antibody concentrations were associated with protection.
- **Effect:** proportion with malaria after CHMI 2/5 control, 6/10 GMZ2-Alhydrogel, 2/8 30 µg GMZ2-CAF01, 5/11 100 µg GMZ2-CAF01
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 13 weeks after last vaccination to CHMI, plus post-CHMI monitoring
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30561539/

### Parasite reduction ratio one day after initiation of artemisinin-based combination therapies and its relationship with parasite clearance time in acutely malarious children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30522524)
- **Journal:** Infectious diseases of poverty
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-12-07
- **Trial period:** 2017
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** acutely malarious children
- **N:** 919
- **Impact:** PRRD1 correlated significantly with PCT and PRRD2, was higher in DHP- and AA-treated than AL-treated children, and PCT estimates derived from PRRD1 regression equations agreed well with microscopy-determined PCT, supporting its use in therapeutic efficacy studies.
- **Effect:** correlation between PRRD1/PRRD2 and PCT significant negative correlation between PRRD1/PRRD2 and PCT; significant positive correlation between PRRD1 and PRRD2
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30522524/

### Cost-effectiveness of a combined intervention of long lasting insecticidal nets and indoor residual spraying compared with each intervention alone for malaria prevention in Ethiopia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30498400)
- **Journal:** Cost effectiveness and resource allocation : C/E
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ethiopia
- **Design:** cluster randomized controlled trial, cost-effectiveness analysis
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-11-22
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** residents of Adami Tullu district exposed to malaria transmission
- **Impact:** Trial-based analysis found that routine practice dominated combined LLIN+IRS and single interventions due to lower costs and comparable effectiveness, while literature-based analysis suggested LLIN alone could be cost-effective.
- **Effect:** incremental cost-effectiveness ratio (USD per DALY averted) 1403 (combined intervention); 207 (LLIN alone)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2014-2016
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30498400/

### Malaria parasite clearance from patients following artemisinin-based combination therapy in Côte d'Ivoire.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30464545)
- **Journal:** Infection and drug resistance
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Côte d'Ivoire
- **Design:** randomized, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-10-26
- **Trial period:** 2016 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients aged at least 6 months with uncomplicated malaria
- **N:** 120
- **Impact:** Both artesunate + amodiaquine and artemether + lumefantrine rapidly cleared parasites and remained highly efficacious, with PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response of 100% and 98.07% at day 42, respectively.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response 100% (AS+AQ), 98.07% (AL)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30464545/

### Population Pharmacokinetics of Mefloquine Intermittent Preventive Treatment for Malaria in Pregnancy in Gabon.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30455233)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Gabon
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial, population pharmacokinetic analysis
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-01-29
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 264
- **Impact:** Mefloquine IPTp given as single or split-dose regimens showed adequate exposure but concentrations at delivery were below previously suggested prophylactic threshold levels, and split-dosing significantly increased bioavailability by 5%.

- **Follow-up:** through delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30455233/

### Comparative Efficacy of Artemether-Lumefantrine and Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine for the Treatment of Uncomplicated Malaria in Ugandan Children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30418593)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2019-03-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 599
- **Impact:** DHA-PQ resulted in a lower risk of recurrent parasitemia than AL, but recrudescence was uncommon and similar with both regimens, with neither treatment selecting for drug-resistance-associated genetic polymorphisms.
- **Effect:** risk of recurrent parasitemia at day 42 26.0% (DHA-PQ) vs 47.0% (AL)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30418593/

### A Balanced Proinflammatory and Regulatory Cytokine Signature in Young African Children Is Associated With Lower Risk of Clinical Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30380038)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Mozambique
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published

- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** young African children/infants followed from birth to 4 years
- **Impact:** Higher proinflammatory and regulatory cytokine concentrations during the second year of life were associated with reduced clinical malaria incidence up to age 4, and chemoprophylaxis altering early malaria exposure significantly affected later T-helper cytokine production.
- **Effect:** association with incidence of clinical malaria
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** from birth to 4 years of age

### Baseline exposure, antibody subclass, and hepatitis B response differentially affect malaria protective immunity following RTS,S/AS01E vaccination in African children.

- **Impact:** Cytophilic IgG1/IgG3 responses to CSP C-terminus/NANP and anti-HBsAg antibodies induced by RTS,S/AS01E were associated with protection, while higher baseline malaria exposure and non-cytophilic IgG responses to CSP were associated with increased malaria risk.

### Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of Tafenoquine for the Treatment of Plasmodium Vivax in Adults

- **Published:** 2018-09-29
- **Trial period:** 2003-09-15 to 2005-01-10
- **Intervention(s):** Tafenoquine; Chloroquine + Primaquine; tafenoquine; Chloroquine + Primaquine
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adults with microscopically confirmed P. vivax malaria
- **N:** 70
- **Impact:** Tafenoquine was associated with more frequent mild keratopathy than chloroquine/primaquine, but there was no evidence of clinically relevant ocular toxicity with either treatment.
- **Effect:** proportion with post-baseline keratopathy 31.8% (TQ) vs 0% (CQ/PQ) (95% CI 5.7-30.8)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 90 days
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01290601

### The impact of packaging and messaging on adherence to malaria treatment: Evidence from a randomized controlled trial in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30177864)
- **Journal:** Journal of development economics
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-09-01
- **Population:** malaria patients receiving antimalarial treatment
- **Impact:** Stickers with short, targeted messages on packaging increased adherence by 9% and reduced untaken pills by 29%, while specialized pictorial packaging had no significant effect compared to standard packaging.
- **Effect:** increase in adherence 9% increase in adherence; 29% reduction in untaken pills
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30177864/

### A pilot randomized controlled trial to compare the effectiveness of two 14-day primaquine regimens for the radical cure of vivax malaria in South India.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30176897)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** South India
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-09-03
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Patients with vivax malaria
- **N:** 50
- **Impact:** Both high-dose (0.5 mg/kg) and conventional-dose (0.25 mg/kg) 14-day primaquine regimens showed similarly low relapse rates, supporting the effectiveness of the currently recommended lower dose regimen for radical cure of vivax malaria in South India.
- **Effect:** Recurrence rate 4/50 (8%) overall, 2 in each group
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30176897/

### Experimental huts trial of the efficacy of pyrethroids/piperonyl butoxide (PBO) net treatments for controlling multi-resistant populations of Anopheles funestus s.s. in Kpomè, Southern Benin.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30175242)
- **Journal:** Wellcome open research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** experimental hut trial, WHO cone and tunnel tests, released/recapture experiment
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-06-13
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** wild free-flying multi-resistant Anopheles funestus s.s. and An. coluzzii mosquito populations
- **Impact:** Pyrethroid-PBO combination LLINs (Type 1 and Type 3) produced significantly higher mortality against multi-resistant An. funestus s.s. and An. coluzzii than conventional pyrethroid-only LLINs.
- **Effect:** mosquito mortality rate Type 1/3 (PBO nets): >80% mortality vs Type 2: 65.53±8.33% and Type 4: 71.25±7.92% mortality with An. funestus; 77-87% mortality with An. coluzzii
- **Species:** mixed
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30175242/

### Microbial larvicides for mosquito control: Impact of long lasting formulations of Bacillus thuringiensis var. israelensis and Bacillus sphaericus on non-target organisms in western Kenya highlands.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30151171)
- **Journal:** Ecology and evolution
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized, controlled, three-arm field trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-07-06
- **Class:** larval source management
- **Population:** natural mosquito larval habitats and cohabiting non-target aquatic organisms
- **N:** 300
- **Impact:** FourStar and LL3 larvicides significantly reduced malaria vector density but did not significantly alter abundance, richness or diversity of non-target aquatic organisms in treated habitats.
- **Effect:** density of non-target organisms per habitat per sampling occasion 1.82 (control), 2.11 (LL3), 2.05 (FourStar) at 21 weeks
- **Follow-up:** 5 months post-treatment
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30151171/

### Wall-type and indoor residual spraying application quality affect the residual efficacy of indoor residual spray against wild malaria vector in southwest Ethiopia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30126433)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ethiopia
- **Design:** randomized, entomological field trial comparing wall surface types and spray application quality
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-08-20
- **Class:** IRS
- **Population:** houses with different mud wall surfaces (smooth, rough, painted) in a semi-urban/rural community
- **N:** 33
- **Impact:** Painted wall surfaces and standard (guideline-compliant) spray application showed significantly higher and more sustained residual efficacy of propoxur against Anopheles gambiae s.l. compared to rough/smooth surfaces and routine spray application.
- **Effect:** mortality rate >80% (standard spray) vs <80% (routine spray, except painted wall)
- **Follow-up:** 17 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30126433/

### Molecular Detection of Residual Parasitemia after Pyronaridine-Artesunate or Artemether-Lumefantrine Treatment of Uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum Malaria in Kenyan Children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30105967)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-10-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 146
- **Impact:** Both qPCR and db-PCR-NALFIA detected substantial residual submicroscopic parasitemia at day 7 after microscopically successful treatment with pyronaridine-artesunate or artemether-lumefantrine, with no significant difference between the two treatments, and db-PCR-NALFIA positivity was associated with treatment failure while qPCR positivity was not.
- **Effect:** proportion with residual parasitemia at day 7 (qPCR) 37.1% (AL) vs 46.1% (PA) (95% CI 0.312-1.578)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30105967/

### Efficacy of Olyset Duo, a bednet containing pyriproxyfen and permethrin, versus a permethrin-only net against clinical malaria in an area with highly pyrethroid-resistant vectors in rural Burkina Faso: a cluster-randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30104047)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-08-10
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2015
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 5 years
- **N:** 2157
- **Impact:** PPF-treated LLINs (Olyset Duo) provided greater protection against clinical malaria and reduced entomological inoculation rate compared with standard permethrin-only LLINs in an area with highly pyrethroid-resistant vectors.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio 0.88 (95% CI 0.77-0.99)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 transmission seasons (2014-2015)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30104047/

### Treatment of adults with acute uncomplicated malaria with azithromycin and chloroquine in India, Colombia, and Suriname.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30050349)
- **Journal:** Research and reports in tropical medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** India, Colombia, Suriname
- **Design:** double-blind, randomized, non-inferiority, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-10-13
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adults with acute uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **Impact:** Azithromycin 1 g plus chloroquine QD for 3 days was inferior to established comparator agents (SPCQ in India, AP in Colombia/Suriname), but increasing azithromycin dose to 2 g improved response rates to 97%.
- **Effect:** parasite clearance rate difference India: 84% (AZCQ) vs 94% (SPCQ); Colombia/Suriname: 57% (AZCQ) vs 99% (AP) (95% CI India: -22.6 to 0.8; Colombia/Suriname: -52 to -32)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30050349/

### School-aged children based seasonal malaria chemoprevention using artesunate-amodiaquine in Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29988270)
- **Journal:** Parasite epidemiology and control
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized, open-label, phase 3 pilot trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-02-07
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2014
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** school-aged pupils
- **N:** 200
- **Impact:** Monthly artesunate-amodiaquine given as seasonal malaria chemoprevention to school-aged children reduced clinical malaria cases substantially compared to no intervention, with only 3 cases in the ASAQ arm versus 20 in the control arm.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 85% (95% CI 80.1-89.9)
- **Follow-up:** 7 months (October 2013 to April 2014)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29988270/

### Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Studies to Assess Safety and Prophylactic Efficacy of Naphthoquine-Azithromycin Combination for Malaria Prophylaxis in Southeast Asia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29987144)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Southeast Asia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-08-27
- **Population:** healthy volunteers and villagers in a low-endemicity area
- **N:** 480
- **Impact:** Monthly single doses of 400 mg or 800 mg naphthoquine-azithromycin were well tolerated and provided over 90% prophylactic efficacy against malaria infections, including 100% efficacy against P. vivax and P. ovale.
- **Effect:** prophylactic efficacy 81.63% (400 mg) and 90.59% (800 mg) against P. falciparum; 100% against P. vivax and P. ovale; >90% overall (intention-to-treat)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 1 month (efficacy trial); 2 months (safety trial)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29987144/

### Intermittent screening and treatment with artemether-lumefantrine versus intermittent preventive treatment with sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine for malaria in pregnancy: a facility-based, open-label, non-inferiority trial in Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29976228)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-07-06
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2014
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT; ITN/LLIN; diagnostic
- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal clinic
- **N:** 459
- **Impact:** ISTp-AL was associated with significantly lower third-trimester maternal parasitaemia and lower risk of low birthweight compared to IPTp-SP, with no significant difference in severe anaemia risk, though ISTp-AL was associated with more frequent fever complaints.
- **Effect:** risk difference -3.96% (parasitaemia); -1.53% (low birthweight); -1.75% (severe anaemia) (95% CI -7.76 to -0.16 (parasitaemia); -1.54 to -1.15 (low birthweight); -4.16 to 0.66 (severe anaemia))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** through delivery (third trimester assessment at 36-40 weeks gestation)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29976228/

### Efficacy of two artemisinin-based combinations for the treatment of malaria in pregnancy in India: a randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29973212)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** India (Ranchi and Jamshedpur, Jharkhand; Rourkela, Odisha)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-07-04
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women in second or third trimester with P. falciparum mono-infection
- **N:** 248
- **Impact:** Both AS+SP and AS+MQ were safe and effective, with high ACPR rates in intention-to-treat and per-protocol analyses, though vomiting was more common with AS+MQ.
- **Effect:** Adequate clinical and parasitological response (ACPR) 96.8% (AS+SP) vs 95.1% (AS+MQ) (95% CI 92.0-99.1% (AS+SP); 89.7-98.2% (AS+MQ))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 63 days post-treatment, plus follow-up at delivery and day 42 postpartum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29973212/

### Effectiveness of pregnant women's active participation in their antenatal care for the control of malaria and anaemia in pregnancy in Ghana: a cluster randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29921302)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-06-19
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; diagnostic
- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal clinics for the first time
- **N:** 14
- **Impact:** The intervention allowing pregnant women active participation via point-of-care malaria and anaemia testing showed no significant beneficial effect on anaemia, parasitaemia, low birth weight, or pregnancy complications compared to routine care.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk ratio 0.97 (anaemia at 8 weeks); 0.92 (anaemia at 36-40 weeks); 1.17 (parasitaemia at 8 weeks); 0.83 (parasitaemia at 36-40 weeks); 0.93 (low birth weight); 0.77 (pregnancy complications) (95% CI 0.78-1.22; 0.63-1.34; 0.68-2.04; 0.27-2.57; 0.44-1.97; 0.17-3.52)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** until delivery (approximately 36-40 weeks gestation)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29921302/

### Comparison of the Cumulative Efficacy and Safety of Chloroquine, Artesunate, and Chloroquine-Primaquine in Plasmodium vivax Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29889239)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Thailand-Myanmar border
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-10-01
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2012
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Patients with uncomplicated P. vivax malaria
- **N:** 644
- **Impact:** Chloroquine delayed vivax malaria relapses compared to artesunate, while adding primaquine radical cure reduced total recurrences by 92.4%.
- **Effect:** reduction in total recurrences with primaquine radical cure 92.4% (95% CI 4.19-4.85)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 1 year
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29889239/

### Therapeutic and Transmission-Blocking  Efficacy of Dihydroartemisinin/Piperaquine and Chloroquine against Plasmodium vivax Malaria, Cambodia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29798745)
- **Journal:** Emerging infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Cambodia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-08-17
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with Plasmodium vivax malaria
- **Impact:** Dihydroartemisinin/piperaquine cleared asexual P. vivax parasites faster, blocked human-to-mosquito transmission more effectively, and better prevented recurrences beyond 2 months compared with chloroquine.
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** >2 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29798745/

### Caregivers' compliance with referral advice: evidence from two studies introducing mRDTs into community case management of malaria in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29720163)
- **Journal:** BMC health services research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** cluster randomised trials
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-05-02
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children under-5 with fever seen by community health workers
- **N:** 21720
- **Impact:** Compliance with CHW referral advice was low (<10%) in both moderate-to-high and low transmission settings, with nearly all children given pre-referral rectal artesunate failing to comply with referral.
- **Effect:** proportion complying with referral <10%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29720163/

### Efficacy and tolerability of artesunate-amodiaquine versus artemether-lumefantrine in the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria at two sentinel sites across Côte d’Ivore.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29717574)
- **Journal:** Annals of parasitology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Côte d'Ivoire
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-01-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 240
- **Impact:** Both artesunate-amodiaquine and artemether-lumefantrine were highly effective and well-tolerated for treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria, with ASAQ showing slightly higher cure rates than AL.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected cure rate ASAQ 95.8% vs AL 92.5% (day 28); ASAQ 95% vs AL 92.5% (day 42)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29717574/

### Wash-resistance of pirimiphos-methyl insecticide treatments of window screens and eave baffles for killing indoor-feeding malaria vector mosquitoes: an experimental hut trial, South East of Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29653593)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** experimental hut trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-04-13
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** Not applicable (entomological/experimental hut study, not human participants)
- **Impact:** Window screens and eave baffles co-treated with pirimiphos-methyl and a polyacrylate binding agent retained high insecticidal efficacy (≥94% mortality) against Anopheles arabiensis even after 20 washes, comparable to indoor residual spraying with pirimiphos-methyl.
- **Effect:** mosquito mortality rate ≥94%
- **Follow-up:** 20 washes (accelerated ageing)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29653593/

### Study to Evaluate Immunogenicity of the Hepatitis B Antigen of the GSK Biologicals' Candidate Malaria Vaccine (257049)
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT01345240)
- **Journal:** Human vaccines & immunotherapeutics
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Ghana
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2018-04-13
- **Trial period:** 2011-11-17 to 2013-01-09
- **Intervention(s):** GlaxoSmithKline (GSK) Biologicals' candidate Plasmodium falciparum malaria vaccine 257049; Engerix-B™ vaccine; Infanrix/Hib™ vaccine; Polio Sabin™ vaccine; Rotarix™ vaccine; Synflorix™ vaccine; Measles vaccine; Yellow fever vaccine
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** healthy infants aged 8-12 weeks
- **N:** 705
- **Impact:** Co-administration of RTS,S/AS01 with routine pediatric vaccines (PHiD-CV, HRV, DTaP/Hib) demonstrated non-inferior immune responses and an acceptable safety profile compared to hepatitis B vaccine co-administration.
- **Effect:** non-inferiority of seroprotection rates non-inferiority criteria met for HBV seroprotection and for PHiD-CV (9/10 serotypes), HRV, and pertussis antigens
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** through booster doses at 18 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01345240

### Pyronaridine-artesunate or dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine versus current first-line therapies for repeated treatment of uncomplicated malaria: a randomised, multicentre, open-label, longitudinal, controlled, phase 3b/4 trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29606364)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3b/4
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Guinea, Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-03-29
- **Trial period:** 2011 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adults and children aged 6 months and older with microscopically confirmed Plasmodium spp malaria
- **N:** 4710
- **Impact:** Pyronaridine-artesunate and dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine were non-inferior to first-line ACTs (artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine) for malaria treatment and retreatment and were well tolerated.
- **Effect:** rate ratio (2-year malaria incidence, ITT) RR 1.05 (pyronaridine-artesunate vs artemether-lumefantrine); RR 0.97 (vs artesunate-amodiaquine); RR 1.22 (dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine vs artemether-lumefantrine); RR 1.25 (vs artesunate-amodiaquine) (95% CI 0.94-1.17; 0.87-1.07; 1.06-1.41; 1.02-1.50)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 years (up to day 42 per episode)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29606364/

### Negligible Impact of Mass Screening and Treatment on Mesoendemic Malaria Transmission at West Timor in Eastern Indonesia: A Cluster-Randomized Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29579195)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** West Timor, Indonesia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-10-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** schoolchildren and community residents in households, microscopy-negative at enrollment
- **N:** 1870
- **Impact:** Two or three rounds of mass screening and treatment over 3 months did not reduce malaria transmission compared to no MST, largely due to high rates of untreated microscopically subpatent infections.
- **Effect:** risk ratio for incidence density of microscopically patent malaria 1.00 (MST3 vs MST0); 1.22 (MST2 vs MST0) (95% CI MST3: .53-1.91; MST2: .42-3.55)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 6 months (3 months during MST plus 3 months following)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29579195/

### Small-scale field evaluation of push-pull system against early- and outdoor-biting malaria mosquitoes in an area of high pyrethroid resistance in Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29568808)
- **Journal:** Wellcome open research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** partially randomized cross-over design, small-scale field evaluation
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-11-22
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; spatial repellent
- **Population:** adult male volunteers used as mosquito collectors in experimental huts and local houses
- **Impact:** The push-pull system significantly reduced outdoor-biting of An. arabiensis and Mansonia species and indoor/outdoor biting of An. arabiensis in local households, but had no significant effect on An. funestus or Culex.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in biting 30% (outdoor, An. arabiensis, huts); 41.5% (Mansonia); 48% (indoor, An. arabiensis, households); 25% (outdoor, An. arabiensis, households)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29568808/

### A Competing-Risk Approach for Modeling Length of Stay in Severe Malaria Patients in South-East Asia and the Implications for Planning of Hospital Services.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29562258)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** South-East Asia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-09-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adult severe malaria patients
- **N:** 1217
- **Impact:** Compared to quinine, artesunate increased the incidence of discharge and decreased the incidence of death, and conventional Kaplan-Meier analysis overestimated cumulative incidence compared to the competing-risk model.
- **Effect:** subdistribution hazard ratio (discharge) 1.24 (95% CI 1.09-1.40)
- **Follow-up:** until discharge or death during hospitalisation
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29562258/

### Child Health Outcomes After Presumptive Infection Treatment in Pregnant Women: A Randomized Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29472491)
- **Journal:** Pediatrics
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-03-01
- **Population:** pregnant women and their children
- **N:** 1320
- **Impact:** Provision of monthly sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine plus azithromycin during pregnancy reduced childhood stunting, improved child development scores, and may have reduced postneonatal mortality compared with standard 2-dose SP.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio for 5-year cumulative incidence of stunting 0.70 (95% CI 0.60 to 0.83)
- **Follow-up:** 60 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29472491/

### Exploring the impact of house screening intervention on entomological indices and incidence of malaria in Arba Minch town, southwest Ethiopia: A randomized control trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29452110)
- **Journal:** Acta tropica
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Arba Minch Town, southwest Ethiopia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-02-13
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; diagnostic
- **Population:** household members in Arba Minch Town, including 477 participants followed for clinical malaria
- **N:** 92
- **Impact:** House screening with wire mesh on doors and windows reduced indoor vector density by 48% and reduced the incidence of P. falciparum malaria by 61% compared to unscreened control houses.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy (incidence of P. falciparum infection) 61% (95% CI 18-83)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 6 months

### Chitinase-3-like 1 is a biomarker of acute kidney injury and mortality in paediatric severe malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29448936)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-02-15
- **Population:** children aged 1-10 years with severe malaria
- **Impact:** Elevated plasma CHI3L1 levels were associated with acute kidney injury and were an independent risk factor for in-hospital and 6-month mortality in children with severe malaria.
- **Effect:** relative risk (in-hospital death, per log CHI3L1) 4.10 (95% CI 1.32-12.75)

### Safety and Immunogenicity of GSK Biologicals' Malaria Vaccine 257049 When Administered on 7 Schedules to African Infants
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT01231503)
- **Journal:** The Pediatric infectious disease journal
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2018-05-01
- **Trial period:** 2011-01-13 to 2014-04-30
- **Intervention(s):** GSK Biological's Investigational Malaria Vaccine 257049; Engerix-B; Tritanrix HepB Hib; BCG; OPV; Rouvax
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** neonates and infants aged 1 to 7 days at enrollment, followed to 18 months
- **N:** 480
- **Impact:** Initiating RTS,S/AS01E vaccination after 6 weeks of age (e.g., 10-, 14- and 26-week schedule) improved anti-CS antibody responses compared with the standard 6-, 10-, 14-week schedule, while neonatal vaccination was well tolerated but produced a lower immune response.
- **Effect:** anti-CS antibody GMC ratio 1.80 (10-,14-,26-week schedule) and 0.59 (≤7 days, 10, 14 weeks schedule) (95% CI 1.24-2.60 and 0.38-0.92)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01231503

### Impact of treatment and re-treatment with artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine on selection of Plasmodium falciparum multidrug resistance gene-1 polymorphisms in the Democratic Republic of Congo and Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29390014)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase III
- **Place:** Democratic Republic of Congo, Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-02-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 12-59 months with P. falciparum malaria
- **Impact:** Treatment and re-treatment with AL or ASAQ had limited impact on selection of Pfmdr1 variants and haplotypes associated with partner drug resistance, though Pfmdr1 86Y was associated with reduced risk of AL treatment failure.
- **Effect:** Risk ratio (RR) for AL treatment failure associated with Pfmdr1 86Y allele RR = 0.34 (95% CI 0.11-1.05)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29390014/

### A randomized, control and comparative study of polyherbal formulation use for Malaria (Plasmodium falciparum).
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29386156)
- **Journal:** Pakistan journal of pharmaceutical sciences
- **Type:** trial
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-01-01
- **Population:** Patients with Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 100
- **Impact:** Malarina, a polyherbal formulation, was as effective as Quinine Bisulphate in treating malaria symptoms and was safe and well tolerated.
- **Species:** mixed
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29386156/

### Efficacy and safety of artemisinin-based combination therapy and chloroquine with concomitant primaquine to treat Plasmodium vivax malaria in Brazil: an open label randomized clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29361939)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Brazil
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-01-24
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with Plasmodium vivax malaria
- **N:** 264
- **Impact:** All three treatment regimens (two ACTs and chloroquine, each with primaquine) achieved cure rates above 90% at 28 and 42 days, meeting WHO efficacy criteria, though rates dropped below 90% by day 63 with confidence intervals still including 90%.
- **Effect:** cure rate >90% at day 28 and 42; <90% at day 63 (95% CI 95% CI included 90% for all three treatments at day 63)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 63 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29361939/

### Prospective Clinical Trial Assessing Species-Specific Efficacy of Artemether-Lumefantrine for the Treatment of Plasmodium malariae, Plasmodium ovale, and Mixed Plasmodium Malaria in Gabon.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29311086)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Gabon
- **Design:** prospective, single-arm, open-label, phase clinical trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-02-23
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated P. malariae or P. ovale monoinfections or mixed Plasmodium infections
- **N:** 72
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine showed high per-protocol efficacy (90-100%) against P. malariae, P. ovale curtisi, P. ovale wallikeri, and mixed Plasmodium infections without major safety concerns.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected ACPR rate 94.2% (day 42), 96.6% (day 28) (95% CI 87.7-100 (day 42); 91.9-100 (day 28))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29311086/

### "We were being treated like the Queen": understanding trial factors influencing high paediatric malaria treatment adherence in western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29304798)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial (original trial); qualitative follow-up with focus group discussions
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-01-05
- **Trial period:** 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** caregivers of children treated for malaria with artemether-lumefantrine
- **Impact:** Both SMS reminder intervention and control arms achieved over 97% adherence to artemether-lumefantrine, and qualitative follow-up identified quality of care and trial procedures as key contributors to high adherence.
- **Effect:** adherence rate 97%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 5 months post-trial completion
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29304798/

### Intravenous artesunate plus Artemisnin based Combination Therapy (ACT) or intravenous quinine plus ACT for treatment of severe malaria in Ugandan children: a randomized controlled clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29281988)
- **Journal:** BMC infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-12-28
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with severe malaria
- **N:** 300
- **Impact:** Intravenous artesunate led to faster parasite clearance than intravenous quinine, but both arms had high rates of parasitological treatment failure at 42 days, mostly due to re-infection rather than recrudescence.
- **Effect:** median days to parasite clearance 2 days (AS) vs 3 days (QNN)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29281988/

### Factors contributing to anaemia after uncomplicated falciparum malaria in under five year-old Nigerian children ten years following adoption of artemisinin-based combination therapies as first-line antimalarials.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29258448)
- **Journal:** BMC infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized, comparative
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-12-19
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under 5 years with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 959
- **Impact:** Ten years after ACT adoption, anaemia remains common before and shortly after treatment in Nigerian children, with drug-attributable falls in haematocrit >4% being common and significantly lower in anaemic compared to non-anaemic children.
- **Effect:** half-time of decline in drug-attributable fall in haematocrit deficits 2.2 days (95% CI 1.9-2.6)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29258448/

### Inhibition of merozoite invasion and transient de-sequestration by sevuparin in humans with Plasmodium falciparum malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29244851)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase I/II
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-12-15
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **Impact:** A single sevuparin infusion was safe and well tolerated, decreased circulating ring-stage infected erythrocytes, and caused transient appearance of mature-stage infected erythrocytes in the circulation, indicating inhibition of merozoite invasion and transient de-sequestration.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29244851/

### Cost-effectiveness analysis of introducing malaria diagnostic testing in drug shops: A cluster-randomised trial in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29244829)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-12-15
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** drug shop customers with fever
- **Impact:** Introduction of mRDTs in drug shops improved malaria diagnosis and appropriate treatment with ACTs, at low incremental health sector cost but higher household costs compared to presumptive diagnosis.
- **Effect:** incremental cost per patient appropriately treated of malaria US$0.55 (health sector); US$3.83 (societal)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29244829/

### Impact of insecticide resistance in Anopheles arabiensis on malaria incidence and prevalence in Sudan and the costs of mitigation.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29229808)
- **Journal:** Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences of the United States of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Sudan
- **Design:** cluster-randomized trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-12-11
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** children aged 1 to <10 years in cohorts monitored for malaria incidence
- **Impact:** IRS with deltamethrin provided no additional protection against malaria compared to LLINs alone, while IRS with bendiocarb showed some evidence of additional protection.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio (interaction IRR for LLIN+IRS vs LLIN alone by insecticide) Deltamethrin IRR=1.0; Bendiocarb interaction IRR=0.55 (95% CI Deltamethrin: 0.36-3.0; Bendiocarb: 0.40-0.76)
- **Follow-up:** 3 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29229808/

### A Randomized Trial Evaluating the Prophylactic Activity of DSM265 Against Preerythrocytic Plasmodium falciparum Infection During Controlled Human Malarial Infection by Mosquito Bites and Direct Venous Inoculation.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29216395)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase II
- **Place:** United States
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2018-02-01
- **Population:** healthy adult volunteers undergoing controlled human malarial infection
- **N:** 24
- **Impact:** DSM265 given 3 or 7 days before controlled human malarial infection was safe and well tolerated, delayed time to parasitemia compared with placebo, but sterilely protected only one third of participants.
- **Effect:** proportion sterilely protected 2 of 6 in each cohort (1/3 overall)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7-10 days post-CHMI
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29216395/

### A village level cluster-randomized entomological evaluation of combination long-lasting insecticidal nets containing pyrethroid plus PBO synergist in Southern Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29162120)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-11-21
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2015
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** villages with indoor-resting Anopheles mosquito populations exposed to pyrethroid-resistant vectors
- **N:** 16
- **Impact:** Deltamethrin+PBO LLINs showed lower Anopheles gambiae s.l. sporozoite rates than deltamethrin-only LLINs during high transmission seasons, but no significant difference overall in sporozoite rate, and no reduction in indoor resting density or parity rates; permethrin+PBO showed no improvement over permethrin alone.
- **Effect:** sporozoite rate 3.97% (deltamethrin) vs 3.04% (deltamethrin+PBO) overall; 3.70% vs 1.95% during high transmission season (95% CI 95% CI 2.91-5.02 (deltamethrin), 2.21-3.87 (deltamethrin+PBO); seasonal: 2.56-4.84 vs 1.18-2.72)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 years (2014-2015)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29162120/

### Chloroquine-Primaquine versus Chloroquine Alone to Treat Vivax Malaria in Afghanistan: An Open Randomized Superiority Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29141719)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Afghanistan
- **Design:** open-label, randomized controlled trial, superiority, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-10-26
- **Trial period:** 2009 to 2014
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** patients aged 6 months and older with microscopy confirmed P. vivax infection, G6PD normal in CQ+PQ group
- **N:** 593
- **Impact:** Adding primaquine to chloroquine significantly reduced P. vivax recurrence compared to chloroquine alone, with greatest protection in the first 6 months.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio 0.37 (95% CI 0.25-0.54)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29141719/

### Efficacy of intermittent preventive treatment and insecticide treated nets on malaria parasitaemia in pregnancy among Igbo women in southeastern Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29097640)
- **Journal:** Journal of vector borne diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-01
- **Class:** chemoprevention; ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** pregnant women (Igbo women)
- **N:** 270
- **Impact:** Use of IPT-SP alone or combined with ITN was associated with significantly reduced malaria parasitaemia prevalence in pregnant women compared to no intervention.
- **Effect:** Odds Ratio 1.88 (IPT vs none)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29097640/

### Cost-effectiveness of artemisinin-naphthoquine versus artemether-lumefantrine for the treatment of uncomplicated malaria in Papua New Guinean children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29084540)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** open-label, randomized, parallel-group, phase 3, cost-effectiveness analysis
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-10-30
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 0.5-5.9 years with Plasmodium falciparum and/or Plasmodium vivax infections
- **N:** 231
- **Impact:** Artemisinin-naphthoquine was more effective than artemether-lumefantrine for treating uncomplicated malaria in PNG children but was more costly, making it potentially not cost-effective at current prices.
- **Effect:** incremental cost per treatment success $10.46 (falciparum), $14.83 (vivax) higher cost per treatment success for AN vs AL; $12,165 (girls) and $12,469 (boys) cost per life year saved
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29084540/

### Impact of a malaria intervention package in schools on Plasmodium infection, anaemia and cognitive function in schoolchildren in Mali: a pragmatic cluster-randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29081992)
- **Journal:** BMJ global health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** pragmatic cluster-randomised trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-06-28
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** primary schoolchildren, longitudinal cohort aged 9-12 years, cross-sectional surveys in all classes
- **N:** 80
- **Impact:** A school-based malaria intervention package including LLINs, education and intermittent parasite clearance (IPCs) dramatically reduced malaria parasitaemia and gametocyte carriage, decreased anaemia, and improved sustained attention in schoolchildren.
- **Effect:** odds ratio (malaria parasitaemia) OR 0.005 (95% CI 0.002 to 0.011)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 20 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29081992/

### γδ T Cells Are Required for the Induction of Sterile Immunity during Irradiated Sporozoite Vaccinations.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29079696)
- **Journal:** Journal of immunology (Baltimore, Md. : 1950)
- **Type:** trial
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-10-27
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** malaria-naive individuals and malaria-endemic population vaccinees; mouse model
- **Impact:** γδ T cells are required for the induction of sterile immunity following irradiated sporozoite (PfSPZ) vaccination, acting through support of CD8α+ dendritic cell accumulation and protective CD8 T cell responses.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29079696/

### Safety and effectiveness of mass drug administration to accelerate elimination of artemisinin-resistant falciparum malaria: A pilot trial in four villages of Eastern Myanmar.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29062913)
- **Journal:** Wellcome open research
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Myanmar
- **Design:** pilot trial, non-randomized, controlled (two intervention villages received MDA, two control villages followed pre-MDA)
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-09-06
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** residents of villages with ≥30% malaria prevalence
- **Impact:** MDA with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine and single low-dose primaquine was safe and reduced asymptomatic P. falciparum prevalence and anopheline vector positivity, though the effect on prevalence was not sustained beyond three months and symptomatic incidence was unchanged.
- **Effect:** malaria prevalence (%) intervention vs control 0.4% vs 2.7% at 3 months post-MDA (95% CI 0.04-1.3 vs 1.7-4.1)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 24 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29062913/

### Assessment of Efficacy and Safety of Arterolane Maleate-Piperaquine Phosphate Dispersible Tablets in Comparison With Artemether-Lumefantrine Dispersible Tablets in Pediatric Patients With Acute Uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum Malaria: A Phase 3, Randomized, Multicenter Trial in India and Africa.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29020247)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** India, Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-10-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pediatric patients aged 6 months to 12 years with acute uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 859
- **Impact:** Arterolane maleate-piperaquine phosphate dispersible tablets showed efficacy and safety comparable to artemether-lumefantrine in treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in children.
- **Effect:** difference in cure rate (day 28, per-protocol) 1.48% (95% CI 0.04%-2.91%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29020247/

### A Molecular Assay to Quantify Male and Female Plasmodium falciparum Gametocytes: Results From 2 Randomized Controlled Trials Using Primaquine for Gametocyte Clearance.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28931236)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Kenya, Mali
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-08-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Individuals with P. falciparum gametocytemia treated for malaria
- **Impact:** Primaquine's early sterilizing effect on P. falciparum infectivity does not appear to be explained by preferential clearance of male gametocytes, but may instead relate to effects on gametocyte fitness.
- **Effect:** Percent of baseline gametocyte density Females: 0.05% (IQR 0.0-0.7%); Males: 3.4% (IQR 0.4-32.9%) of baseline at day 7 in Kenya
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7 days (Kenya); 48 hours (Mali)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28931236/

### Studies of a Candidate Aminoquinoline Antimalarial (AQ-13)
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT01614964)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE2
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2017-09-12
- **Trial period:** 2013-08 to 2016-01
- **Intervention(s):** AQ-13 Treatment; Coartem Treatment
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adult Malian men (≥18 years) with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 66
- **Impact:** AQ-13 showed similar cure rates to artemether plus lumefantrine in the per-protocol analysis (meeting non-inferiority), but the intention-to-treat analysis did not meet the non-inferiority criterion, though no AQ-13 treatment failures occurred.
- **Effect:** difference in proportion cured (per-protocol) -6.1% (95% CI -14.7 to 2.4)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 weeks (1 week inpatient plus 5 weeks outpatient)

### RTS,S/AS01E Malaria Vaccine Induces Memory and Polyfunctional T Cell Responses in a Pediatric African Phase III Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28878775)
- **Journal:** Frontiers in immunology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Africa
- **Design:** phase 3, randomized, controlled, immunogenicity substudy
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-08-23
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children (pediatric participants)
- **N:** 179
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01E vaccination induced significantly higher frequencies of polyfunctional CSP- and HBsAg-specific CD4+ T cells producing multiple cytokines compared to baseline and to rabies-vaccinated controls.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 1 month post primary vaccination
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28878775/

### Comparison of effectiveness of two different artemisinin-based combination therapies in an area with high seasonal transmission of malaria in Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28822205)
- **Journal:** Annals of parasitology
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized, open-label trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Patients with uncomplicated falciparum malaria recruited all year round
- **Impact:** Cure rates for both Artemether-Lumefantrine and Artesunate-Amodiaquine were higher during the dry season than the rainy season, largely due to a higher incidence of new infections (reinfections) during the rainy season.
- **Effect:** PCR-adjusted cure rate AL: 85.9% (dry) vs 75.0% (rainy); ASAQ: 93.3% (dry) vs 80.7% (rainy)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28822205/

### RTS,S/AS01 Malaria Vaccine Efficacy is Not Modified by Seasonal Precipitation: Results from a Phase 3 Randomized Controlled Trial in Malawi.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28775306)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-08-03
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children (5-17 months) and infants (6-12 weeks)
- **N:** 1544
- **Impact:** Increased rainfall was associated with higher malaria rates, but there was no evidence that seasonal precipitation modified RTS,S/AS01 vaccine efficacy.
- **Effect:** rate ratio for malaria per 1-inch increase in monthly rainfall 12.6% increase (children), 15.9% increase (infants) (95% CI 9.6-15.6% (children); 12.8-18.9% (infants))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 4 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28775306/

### Preliminary efficacy investigations of oral fipronil against Anopheles arabiensis when administered to Zebu cattle (Bos indicus) under field conditions.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28760483)
- **Journal:** Acta tropica
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-07-29
- **Trial period:** 2015-05 to 2015-07
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** cattle (Zebu, Bos indicus) and indoor-resting Anopheles mosquitoes in sleeping huts
- **N:** 4
- **Impact:** Oral fipronil treatment of cattle significantly reduced indoor resting density of Anopheles arabiensis in treatment plots compared to control plots, though effects on An. gambiae s.s. were not significant and An. funestus s.s. density increased over time.
- **Effect:** efficacy (reduction in indoor resting density) 89% at 3 weeks; 64% at 4 weeks post-treatment (95% CI T1 density 0.05-0.10 (3 weeks); 0.08-0.14 (4 weeks))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 4 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28760483/

### Effects of liver-stage clearance by Primaquine on gametocyte carriage of Plasmodium vivax and P. falciparum.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28732068)
- **Journal:** PLoS neglected tropical diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-07-21
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children
- **Impact:** Primaquine treatment reduced the incidence of P. vivax gametocytes by 73%, but had no effect on gametocyte density or proportion of infections carrying gametocytes, and relapses contributed substantially to ongoing P. vivax gametocyte carriage.
- **Effect:** percent reduction in incidence of Pv gametocytes 73% (95% CI 0.25-0.62)

### Mass Azithromycin and Malaria Parasitemia in Niger: Results from a Community-Randomized Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28722569)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Niger
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-07-19
- **Population:** children in trachoma trial communities
- **N:** 24
- **Impact:** Additional mass azithromycin distributions during the low-transmission season did not significantly reduce malaria parasitemia prevalence or parasite density compared with annual mass azithromycin alone after 3 years.
- **Effect:** difference in parasitemia prevalence not significant
- **Follow-up:** 3 years
