### Haemoglobin changes and risk of anaemia following treatment for uncomplicated falciparum malaria in sub-Saharan Africa.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28645255)
- **Journal:** BMC infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** sub-Saharan Africa
- **Design:** individual-patient data pooled analysis of randomized controlled trials, mixed effect models
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-06-23
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated falciparum malaria, mostly children under 5 years old
- **N:** 8897
- **Impact:** Most patients experienced an early drop in haemoglobin after treatment start followed by a linear recovery through day 28, with the degree of the early dip determined by pre-treatment parasitaemia and parasite clearance rate rather than the specific treatment used.
- **Effect:** Percentage change in haemoglobin concentration -12.8% nadir drop in under 5s (day 0 to ~35h); +13.8% increase from baseline by day 28
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28645255/

### Safety and Efficacy of Adding a Single Low Dose of Primaquine to the Treatment of Adult Patients With Plasmodium falciparum Malaria in Senegal, to Reduce Gametocyte Carriage: A Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28605472)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Senegal
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-08-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Adults with Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 274
- **Impact:** Adding a single low dose of primaquine to ACT substantially reduced gametocyte carriage but caused a greater drop in hemoglobin among G6PD-deficient patients.
- **Effect:** Reduction in gametocyte carriage 73% (95% CI 24-90)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28605472/

### Single dose primaquine to reduce gametocyte carriage and Plasmodium falciparum transmission in Cambodia: An open-label randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28591198)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Cambodia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-06-07
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Cambodian adults with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 101
- **Impact:** A single 45mg dose of primaquine added to DHP rapidly and significantly reduced gametocytemia compared to DHP alone, which failed to reduce gametocytemia and prevent transmission to mosquitoes.
- **Effect:** gametocyte clearance by day 14 primaquine group gametocyte-free by day 14; significantly less likely to harbor gametocytes by day 7 vs DHP-alone
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 14 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28591198/

### Predicting RTS,S Vaccine-Mediated Protection from Transcriptomes in a Malaria-Challenge Clinical Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28588574)
- **Journal:** Frontiers in immunology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Design:** controlled human malaria infection challenge trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-05-23
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** healthy adults
- **Impact:** Transcriptome analysis identified NF-κB and IFN-γ signaling pathway genes that predict RTS,S vaccine-mediated protection against controlled human malaria infection.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28588574/

### Dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine versus artesunate-amodiaquine for treatment of malaria infection in pregnancy in Ghana: an open-label, randomised, non-inferiority trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28556586)
- **Journal:** Tropical medicine & international health : TM & IH
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-07-03
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** second/third trimester pregnant women with confirmed asymptomatic Plasmodium falciparum parasitaemia
- **N:** 417
- **Impact:** Dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine was non-inferior to artesunate-amodiaquine for treating malaria infection in pregnancy, with fewer adverse events and no safety concerns.
- **Effect:** adjusted difference in parasitological efficacy (DHA-PPQ minus ASAQ) day 28: 3.5%; day 42: 3.9% (95% CI day 28: -1.5 to 8.5; day 42: -2.7 to 10.4)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days post-treatment and 6 weeks post-partum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28556586/

### Effect of IPTp on Plasmodium falciparum antibody levels among pregnant women and their babies in a sub-urban coastal area in Ghana.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28549426)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-05-26
- **Class:** monoclonal antibody; IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women and their infants
- **N:** 320
- **Impact:** IPTp-SP was associated with a decline in antibody levels from enrolment to delivery and may have reduced malaria exposure, but this did not affect antibody transfer to the fetus or protection of infants from clinical malaria at 6 months.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months after birth
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28549426/

### Comparative analysis of the safety and tolerability of fixed-dose artesunate/amodiaquine versus artemether/lumefantrine combinations for uncomplicated falciparum malaria in pregnancy: a randomized open label study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28533699)
- **Journal:** Clinical pharmacology : advances and applications
- **Type:** trial
- **Design:** randomized, open-label
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-05-09
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women in second and third trimester with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria, plus nonpregnant and nonmalarious pregnant controls
- **N:** 155
- **Impact:** Both artesunate/amodiaquine and artemether/lumefantrine were well-tolerated and safe in the second and third trimester of pregnancy, though adverse event rate was higher with AA (70%) than AL (10%) and mild, non-clinically significant elevations in liver enzymes occurred.
- **Effect:** Adverse event rate AA 70% vs AL 10%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 4 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28533699/

### Artemisinin-based combination therapy in pregnant women in Zambia: efficacy, safety and risk of recurrent malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28511713)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Zambia, Nchelenge district, Luapula Province
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-05-16
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women in second or third trimester with malaria
- **N:** 900
- **Impact:** Dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine had the lowest treatment failure and re-infection rates compared to artemether-lumefantrine and mefloquine-artesunate, making it preferable in high-transmission settings.
- **Effect:** PCR-adjusted treatment failure rate / hazard ratio for re-infection AL 4.7%, MQAS 1.3%, DHAPQ 0.8%; HR for re-infection AL 4.71, MQAS 1.59 (vs DHAPQ) (95% CI AL 2.7-8.0; MQAS 0.4-3.7; DHAPQ 0.2-3.0; HR AL 3.10-7.2; HR MQAS 1.02-2.46)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 63 days active follow-up, then at delivery and 1 year post-delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28511713/

### Safety and efficacy of the choline analogue SAR97276 for malaria treatment: results of two phase 2, open-label, multicenter trials in African patients.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28472957)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Gabon, Benin, Tanzania, Kenya
- **Design:** open-label, multicenter, phase 2, age de-escalating
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-05-04
- **Trial period:** 2008 to 2012
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** African adults and children with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 133
- **Impact:** SAR97276A monotherapy showed insufficient efficacy compared to artemether-lumefantrine and was not further developed for malaria treatment, though it was generally well tolerated.
- **Effect:** cure rate Adults IM single dose 59%, IV single dose 77%, 3-day IM adults 90%, 3-day IM children 68%; Study 2 teenagers required rescue therapy in 8/8 (once daily) and 5/8 (twice daily) vs 4/4 cured with artemether-lumefantrine
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28472957/

### Cost-effectiveness analysis of malaria rapid diagnostic tests for appropriate treatment of malaria at the community level in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28453718)
- **Journal:** Health policy and planning
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomised controlled trial, cost-effectiveness analysis, two-site comparison
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-06-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children under 5 with fever
- **Impact:** Use of mRDTs by CHWs improved appropriate treatment of malaria with ACT and was cost-effective in the moderate-to-high transmission area, but was a higher-cost, less cost-effective intervention in the low transmission area.
- **Effect:** incremental cost per appropriately treated child (health sector perspective) US$3.0 (moderate-to-high transmission area); US$13.3 (low transmission area)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28453718/

### The impact of an intervention to introduce malaria rapid diagnostic tests on fever case management in a high transmission setting in Uganda: A mixed-methods cluster-randomized trial (PRIME).
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28288172)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-03-13
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children under five years with fever attending public health centers, and health workers
- **N:** 1400
- **Impact:** The PRIME intervention using mRDTs did not significantly reduce inappropriate treatment of malaria compared to control health centers.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk ratio 0.81 (95% CI 0.56-1.17)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 rounds of interviews over the study period (2010-13)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28288172/

- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28272979)

### Acceptability of insecticide-treated clothing for malaria prevention among migrant rubber tappers in Myanmar: a cluster-randomized non-inferiority crossover trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28241830)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Myanmar
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-02-28
- **Trial period:** 2015 to 2015
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** migrant rubber tappers
- **N:** 234
- **Impact:** ITC was found to be non-inferior to untreated clothing on seven out of eight acceptability indicators, with high overall acceptability among migrant rubber tappers.
- **Effect:** non-inferiority (acceptability indicators) non-inferior on 7 of 8 indicators
- **Follow-up:** 6 weeks

### The effect of price on demand for and use of bednets: evidence from a randomized experiment in Madagascar.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28207055)
- **Journal:** Health policy and planning
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Madagascar
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-03-01
- **Class:** vaccine; treatment/ACT; ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households eligible for insecticide-treated bednet distribution
- **Impact:** Increasing the price of ITNs by $0.55 significantly reduced both demand and effective coverage by 23.1 percentage points.
- **Effect:** percentage point change in demand and effective coverage per $0.55 price increase -23.1% (95% CI 19.6-26.6)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28207055/

### Early rising asexual parasitaemia in Nigerian children following a first dose of artemisinin-based combination treatments of falciparum malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28143417)
- **Journal:** BMC infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-31
- **Trial period:** 2015
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with acute falciparum malaria
- **N:** 416
- **Impact:** ERAP occurred in about half of children treated with ACTs and appears to result from rapid mobilization of parasites from deep tissue following the first dose, with retained parasites subsequently eliminated.
- **Effect:** proportion with ERAP 205/416 (49.3%) (95% CI 81-130.1)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28143417/

### Efficacy of text-message reminders on paediatric malaria treatment adherence and their post-treatment return to health facilities in Kenya: a randomized controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28122622)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-25
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2016
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children below five years of age with uncomplicated malaria
- **N:** 1677
- **Impact:** SMS reminders significantly increased post-treatment return to the health facility on day 3 and day 28, but had no significant effect on adherence to artemether-lumefantrine, which was already high in both groups.
- **Effect:** odds ratio for return to facility on day 3 OR 1.55 (95% CI 1.15-2.08)
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28122622/

### Vitamin A and Zinc Supplementation Among Pregnant Women to Prevent Placental Malaria: A Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Trial in Tanzania.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28115667)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-23
- **Population:** HIV-negative primigravid or secundigravid pregnant women in first trimester
- **N:** 2500
- **Impact:** Zinc supplementation reduced the risk of histopathology-positive placental malaria, but neither vitamin A nor zinc affected PCR-positive malaria, small for gestational age births, or prematurity.
- **Effect:** risk ratio 0.64 (95% CI 0.44-0.91)
- **Follow-up:** until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28115667/

### Efficacy and safety of intermittent preventive treatment in schoolchildren with sulfadoxine/pyrimethamine (SP) and SP plus piperaquine in Democratic Republic of the Congo: a randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28108368)
- **Journal:** International journal of antimicrobial agents
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Democratic Republic of the Congo
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-17
- **Class:** IPTsc; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** schoolchildren
- **N:** 616
- **Impact:** SP plus piperaquine (SP/PQ) significantly increased haemoglobin and reduced anaemia, malaria parasitaemia and clinical malaria compared to control, while SP alone showed smaller and mostly non-significant effects.
- **Effect:** Hb change (g/dL) and percentage reduction in anaemia/parasitaemia/clinical malaria SP: Hb +0.20 g/dL; SP/PQ: Hb +0.39 g/dL; SP/PQ reduced anaemia by 28%, parasitaemia by 40%, clinical malaria by 58% (95% CI Hb SP: -0.61 to 0.47; Hb SP/PQ: 0.12-0.66; anaemia SP/PQ: 19-37%; parasitaemia SP/PQ: 26-52%; clinical malaria SP/PQ: 17-79%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28108368/

### Expanding access to parasite-based malaria diagnosis through retail drug shops in Tanzania: evidence from a randomized trial and implications for treatment.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28049481)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-03
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** suspected malaria patients seeking treatment at retail drug shops (ADDOs)
- **N:** 262
- **Impact:** Introduction of RDTs into accredited drug dispensing outlets substantially increased parasite-based malaria diagnosis and improved treatment adherence without requiring a price subsidy.
- **Effect:** Percentage point increase in pre-treatment testing increase from 0 to 65% of suspected malaria patients tested; overall parasite-based diagnosis increased from 19 to 74% in intervention districts vs 3 to 18% in control district (95% CI 60.8-69.6%)
- **Follow-up:** 1 year
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28049481/

### The effect of small solar powered 'Bͻkͻͻ' net fans on mosquito net use: results from a randomized controlled cross-over trial in southern Ghana.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28049477)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2017-01-03
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** Households in rural communities
- **N:** 83
- **Impact:** Provision of solar-powered net fans appeared to increase net use, but the increase was not statistically significant in the primary ITT or PP analyses; it was significant only in a secondary PP analysis.
- **Effect:** odds ratio ITT OR 3.24; PP OR 1.17; secondary PP OR 1.95
- **Follow-up:** 10 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28049477/

### Fixed-Dose Artesunate-Amodiaquine Combination vs Chloroquine for Treatment of Uncomplicated Blood Stage P. vivax Infection in the Brazilian Amazon: An Open-Label Randomized, Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27988484)

- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-10-20
- **Trial period:** 2012 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated blood stage P. vivax infection
- **N:** 380
- **Impact:** Fixed-dose artesunate-amodiaquine was superior to chloroquine in achieving adequate clinical and parasitological responses against P. vivax infection in the Brazilian Amazon.
- **Effect:** difference in adjusted-ACPR rate at day 28 6.4% (95% CI 95% CI 2.7-10.1%)

### Safety, Immunogenicity and Efficacy of Prime-Boost Vaccination with ChAd63 and MVA Encoding ME-TRAP against Plasmodium falciparum Infection in Adults in Senegal.

- **Place:** Senegal
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-12-15
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** healthy male adults living in a malaria endemic area
- **N:** 120
- **Impact:** The ChAd63-MVA ME-TRAP prime-boost vaccine was safe and immunogenic but did not show protective efficacy against P. falciparum infection compared to controls.

### Reactive Case Detection for Plasmodium vivax Malaria Elimination in Rural Amazonia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27941968)
- **Journal:** PLoS neglected tropical diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Brazil
- **Design:** observational cohort/reactive case detection study
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-12-12
- **Population:** residents of farming settlements including household and neighbor contacts of malaria index cases
- **N:** 1882
- **Impact:** Household and neighboring household members of index cases were significantly more likely to be parasitemic than control household members, but microscopy-based RCD missed about half of qPCR-detected infections and over a quarter of infections occurred in distant control households, mostly asymptomatic carriers.
- **Effect:** proportion of infections/biomass detected 49.5% of qPCR-detected infections and 76.8% of parasite biomass detected by microscopy-based RCD; 27.6% of qPCR-positive samples from control HHs
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27941968/

### Pre-referral Rectal Artesunate Treatment by Community-Based Treatment Providers in Ghana, Guinea-Bissau, Tanzania, and Uganda (Study 18): A Cluster-Randomized Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27941110)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana, Guinea-Bissau, Tanzania, Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-12-01
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under 5 years with febrile illness in villages remote from health facilities
- **N:** 54013
- **Impact:** Mothers trained to give prereferral rectal artesunate achieved higher treatment coverage than community health workers, but overall treatment access remained low and referral compliance and mortality varied by provider type.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 1.84 (adjusted for country); 1.67 higher average probability of treatment adjusting for heterogeneity (95% CI 1.20-2.83)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 19 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27941110/

### Evaluation of long-lasting microbial larvicide for malaria vector control in Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27903292)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized cluster trial, semi-natural and natural habitat evaluations
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-12-01
- **Class:** larval source management
- **Population:** Anopheles gambiae larvae and adult mosquito populations in highland and lowland sites
- **Impact:** The FourStar long-lasting biolarvicide briquet formulation significantly reduced mosquito pupal productivity and indoor/outdoor biting adult mosquito densities in western Kenya.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in pupal productivity and adult mosquito density 87-98% pupal reduction (semi-natural), 60-100% pupal reduction (natural), 76-82% indoor and 67-75% outdoor adult mosquito density reduction

### Oil-Fortified Maize Porridge Increases Absorption of Lumefantrine in Children with Uncomplicated Falciparum Malaria.

- **Type:** trial

- **Status:** published

- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under 5 years with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 33
- **Impact:** Oil-fortified maize porridge produced comparable lumefantrine absorption to milk when co-administered with artemether-lumefantrine, suggesting it can be an alternative to milk.

### Using WeChat official accounts to improve malaria health literacy among Chinese expatriates in Niger: an intervention study.

- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-11-24
- **Trial period:** 2014-07 to 2014-10
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** Chinese expatriates working in Niger
- **N:** 1441
- **Impact:** WeChat-based health education significantly improved malaria health literacy (knowledge, attitude, practice, skills) among Chinese expatriates compared to controls, and was associated with a decrease in reported malaria morbidity from 23.72% to 15.40%.
- **Effect:** change in health literacy rate health literacy rates increased from baseline (58.29% skills, 62% knowledge, 54% practice, 34% attitude) to above 70% for all four aspects post-intervention; greater improvement in intervention group vs controls

### Efficacy and safety of re-treatment with the same artemisinin-based combination treatment (ACT) compared with an alternative ACT and quinine plus clindamycin after failure of first-line recommended ACT (QUINACT): a bicentre, open-label, phase 3, randomised controlled trial.

### Referral Patterns of Community Health Workers Diagnosing and Treating Malaria: Cluster-Randomized Trials in Two Areas of High- and Low-Malaria Transmission in Southwestern Uganda.

- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-10-31
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2012
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children treated by community health workers for suspected malaria
- **Impact:** Community health workers using malaria rapid diagnostic tests and ACTs referred patients more frequently than those using presumptive diagnosis, but referral advice was not always given even when indicated.
- **Effect:** difference in referral frequency between intervention and control arms
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** January 2010 to July 2012
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27799650/

### Evaluation of SMS reminder messages for altering treatment adherence and health seeking perceptions among malaria care-seekers in Nigeria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27315831)
- **Journal:** Health policy and planning
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-16
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** malaria care-seekers who purchased malaria treatment from drug shops after RDT testing, including adults and sick children
- **N:** 686
- **Impact:** Basic and expanded SMS reminders improved treatment adherence among adults, but the expanded SMS worsened treatment adherence and increased unnecessary anti-malarial use among sick children, and lowered acceptance of drug retailers' administration of RDTs.
- **Effect:** odds ratio basic SMS adherence OR=1.53 (adults); expanded SMS adherence OR=1.42 (adults); expanded SMS adherence OR=0.26 (children) (95% CI 0.96-2.44 (basic, adults); 0.97-2.07 (expanded, adults); 0.10-0.66 (expanded, children))
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27315831/

### Communication for behavioural impact in enhancing utilization of insecticide-treated bed nets among mothers of under-five children in rural North Sudan: an experimental study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27756314)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Sudan
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-10-18
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2014
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** mothers of under-five children in rural areas
- **N:** 761
- **Impact:** The COMBI strategy significantly increased mothers' knowledge, positive attitudes, and utilization of LLINs in intervention villages compared to comparison villages.
- **Effect:** odds ratio for LLIN utilization 4.6 (95% CI 3.72-5.72)
- **Follow-up:** January 2013 to February 2014
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27756314/

### Host Biomarkers Are Associated With Response to Therapy and Long-Term Mortality in Pediatric Severe Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27703996)
- **Journal:** Open forum infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-arm, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-20
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with severe malaria aged 1-10 years
- **N:** 180
- **Impact:** Persistent endothelial activation and dysfunction, as reflected by biomarkers such as angiopoietin-2, predict survival and clinical recovery in children with severe malaria.
- **Effect:** association with in-hospital mortality (p-value) higher Ang-2, CXCL10, and sFlt-1 at admission in children who died
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27703996/

### Antibody titres and boosting after natural malaria infection in BK-SE36 vaccine responders during a follow-up study in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27703240)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase Ib
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-10-05
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Population:** children and adolescents 6-20 years old, vaccine responders
- **Impact:** Responders to BK-SE36 vaccination with higher anti-SE36 antibody titres had lower risk of malaria infection, and antibody titres were boosted after natural infection, suggesting anti-SE36 antibodies confer protection against malaria.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27703240/

### Cost-Effectiveness Analysis of Test-Based versus Presumptive Treatment of Uncomplicated Malaria in Children under Five Years in an Area of High Transmission in Central Ghana.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27695130)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-10-03
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children under five years with suspected malaria
- **N:** 32
- **Impact:** Introduction of malaria rapid diagnostic tests reduced unnecessary ACT prescriptions and increased the proportion of children appropriately treated compared to clinical judgement, and was found to be cost-effective.
- **Effect:** proportion appropriately treated 70% versus 57%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 1 week post health centre visit
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27695130/

### Cost effectiveness of intermittent screening followed by treatment versus intermittent preventive treatment during pregnancy in West Africa: analysis and modelling of results from a non-inferiority trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27663678)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** West Africa
- **Design:** multi-centre, non-inferiority trial; cost-effectiveness analysis and modelling, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-09-23
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT; ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 1000
- **Impact:** At current SP efficacy levels observed in trial settings, switching from IPTp-SP to ISTp-AL would not be cost-effective due to higher costs and limited difference in outcomes, though ISTp-AL becomes more cost-effective as SP efficacy declines.
- **Effect:** Incremental cost-effectiveness ratio (US$ per DALY averted) -175.12 (95% CI -1166.29 to 1267.71)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27663678/

### Artemether-Lumefantrine versus Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine for Treatment of Uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum Malaria in Children Aged Less than 15 Years in Guinea-Bissau - An Open-Label Non-Inferiority Randomised Clinical Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27649561)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Guinea-Bissau
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-09-20
- **Trial period:** 2012 to 2015
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 15 years with uncomplicated P. falciparum mono-infection
- **N:** 312
- **Impact:** Dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine was not inferior to artemether-lumefantrine for treatment of uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Guinea-Bissau, with both drugs meeting WHO recommended efficacy thresholds.
- **Effect:** odds ratio (adequate clinical and parasitological response, AL vs DP) 0.22 (95% CI 0-0.68)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27649561/

### A Phase II pilot trial to evaluate safety and efficacy of ferroquine against early Plasmodium falciparum in an induced blood-stage malaria infection study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27624471)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase II
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-09-13
- **Population:** Male and non-pregnant female volunteers aged 18-50 years
- **N:** 8
- **Impact:** A single 800 mg dose of ferroquine was rapidly absorbed and effectively cleared induced P. falciparum parasitaemia, and was generally well tolerated apart from transient elevated transaminases in three subjects.
- **Effect:** parasite reduction ratio 162.9 (95% CI 95% CI 141-188)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27624471/

### Scheduled Intermittent Screening with Rapid Diagnostic Tests and Treatment with Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine versus Intermittent Preventive Therapy with Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine for Malaria in Pregnancy in Malawi: An Open-Label Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27622558)
- **Journal:** PLoS medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-09-13
- **Trial period:** 2011 to 2013
- **Class:** IPTp; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** HIV-seronegative pregnant women (paucigravidae and multigravidae)
- **N:** 1873
- **Impact:** Scheduled intermittent screening with RDTs and treatment with dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine was not superior to IPTp-SP and was associated with higher fetal loss and more malaria at delivery.
- **Effect:** relative risk RR = 1.04 (adverse live birth outcome, all women); RR = 1.19 (malaria at delivery, all women); RR = 2.06 (fetal loss) (95% CI 0.90-1.20; 1.07-1.33; 1.01-4.21)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** through delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27622558/

### Adding a single low-dose of primaquine (0.25 mg/kg) to artemether-lumefantrine did not compromise treatment outcome of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Tanzania: a randomized, single-blinded clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27565897)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-08-26
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients aged ≥1 year with acute uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria, excluding pregnant and lactating women
- **N:** 220
- **Impact:** Adding a single low-dose of primaquine (0.25 mg/kg) to artemether-lumefantrine did not compromise treatment outcome of uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria compared to AL alone.
- **Effect:** PCR-adjusted ACPR on day 28 105/105 (100%) AL+PQ vs 101/102 (99%) AL
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27565897/

### Supplementary effect and durability of prototype insecticide-treated eave curtains on indoor resting mosquitoes in Kadibo division, Western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (38601356)
- **Journal:** MalariaWorld journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomised controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-08-16
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** Households in traditional East African houses (occupants of study houses) in Ahero rice irrigation scheme
- **N:** 80
- **Impact:** Fully screened houses with insecticide-treated eave nets, curtains and door hangers combined with LLINs significantly reduced indoor resting malaria vector densities compared to houses with LLINs alone.
- **Effect:** Rate ratio (density reduction) 82% reduction in An. funestus (RR=0.18); 70% reduction in An. arabiensis (RR=0.30) (95% CI An. funestus: 0.09-0.36; An. arabiensis: 0.15-0.58)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 24 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38601356/

### The effect of mass mosquito trapping on malaria transmission and disease burden (SolarMal): a stepped-wedge cluster-randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27520594)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Rusinga Island, Lake Victoria, western Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-08-09
- **Trial period:** 2012 to 2015
- **Class:** diagnostic
- **Population:** all residents of Rusinga Island enrolled in the health and demographic surveillance system
- **N:** 34041
- **Impact:** Mass deployment of solar-powered odour-baited mosquito traps reduced malaria prevalence by about 30% but had an imprecise, non-significant effect on clinical malaria incidence.
- **Effect:** adjusted effectiveness (clinical incidence); relative reduction in prevalence 40.8% (clinical incidence); 29.8% lower prevalence (95% CI -172.8 to 87.1 (clinical incidence); 20.9-38.0 (prevalence))
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 2 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27520594/

### A phase 2b randomized, controlled trial of the efficacy of the GMZ2 malaria vaccine in African children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27477844)
- **Journal:** Vaccine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2b
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Gabon, Ghana, Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-07-28
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children 12-60 months old
- **N:** 1849
- **Impact:** GMZ2 vaccine reduced the incidence of malaria compared to control but with modest efficacy of 14% in the per-protocol analysis.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy (ATP analysis) 14% (95% CI 3.6%-23%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27477844/

### Population Pharmacokinetics and Pharmacodynamics of Lumefantrine in Young Ugandan Children Treated With Artemether-Lumefantrine for Uncomplicated Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27471317)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-07-28
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** young children treated for uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 105
- **Impact:** Children not receiving trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole with day 7 capillary lumefantrine concentrations below 200 ng/mL had a 3-fold higher risk of 28-day recurrent parasitemia, but this threshold did not predict recurrence in children receiving trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole.
- **Effect:** hazard ratio 3-fold higher hazard
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27471317/

### Effect of quinine and artesunate combination therapy on platelet count of children with severe malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27458993)
- **Journal:** Paediatrics and international child health
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-07-26
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 12 years with severe malaria
- **N:** 100
- **Impact:** A significantly higher proportion of children on quinine had a ≥20% fall in platelet count compared to those on ACT, and ACT was significantly more efficacious than quinine.
- **Effect:** proportion with ≥20% fall in platelet count 30.4% (quinine) vs 10.8% (ACT)
- **Follow-up:** 7 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27458993/

### A cluster randomized trial comparing deltamethrin and bendiocarb as insecticides for indoor residual spraying to control malaria on Bioko Island, Equatorial Guinea.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27448667)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Bioko Island, Equatorial Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-07-22
- **Class:** IRS
- **Population:** children aged 2-14 years living in households in clusters receiving IRS
- **N:** 2704
- **Impact:** Malaria prevalence was lower in the bendiocarb arm than the deltamethrin arm, but the difference was not statistically significant, suggesting bendiocarb may be more effective than deltamethrin for IRS on Bioko, likely due to vector resistance to deltamethrin.
- **Effect:** malaria prevalence (%) 16.8% bendiocarb vs 23.2% deltamethrin (95% CI 11.1-24.7 (bendiocarb); 16.0-32.3 (deltamethrin))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27448667/

### Comparison of artesunate-mefloquine and artemether-lumefantrine fixed-dose combinations for treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in children younger than 5 years in sub-Saharan Africa: a randomised, multicentre, phase 4 trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27430374)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 4
- **Place:** Burkina Faso, Kenya, Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-07-16
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated P falciparum malaria
- **N:** 945
- **Impact:** Fixed-dose artesunate-mefloquine was non-inferior to artemether-lumefantrine for treatment of uncomplicated P falciparum malaria in children under 5 years, with similar efficacy and safety profiles.
- **Effect:** treatment difference in PCR-corrected ACPR rate at day 63 1.23% (95% CI -2.84% to 5.29%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 63 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27430374/

### A comparative study of azithromycin and sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine as prophylaxis against malaria in pregnancy.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27424614)
- **Journal:** The Nigerian postgraduate medical journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-01
- **Class:** chemoprevention
- **Population:** antenatal clinic attendees (pregnant women)
- **N:** 200
- **Impact:** Azithromycin was comparable to sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine in preventing malaria in pregnancy, with similar rates of parasitaemia and tolerability between groups.
- **Effect:** proportion developing malaria 4.7% (SP) vs 6.2% (AZ)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27424614/

### Longitudinal effect of CD4 by cotrimoxazole use on malaria incidence among HIV-infected Ugandan adults on antiretroviral therapy: a randomized controlled study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27417903)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-07-15
- **Population:** HIV-infected adults on antiretroviral therapy with CD4 counts ≥250 cells/µl
- **N:** 2180
- **Impact:** CD4 count during follow-up, at enrolment, or at ART initiation was not associated with clinical malaria incidence and did not modify the effect of stopping cotrimoxazole on malaria risk.
- **Effect:** rate ratio close to 1
- **Follow-up:** minimum 1 year, median 2.5 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27417903/

### Intermittent Preventive Treatment with Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine in Ugandan Schoolchildren Selects for Plasmodium falciparum Transporter Polymorphisms That Modify Drug Sensitivity.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27401569)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-09-23
- **Trial period:** 2011 to 2012
- **Class:** IPTsc; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** schoolchildren
- **Impact:** Monthly dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine reduced malaria incidence by 96% compared to placebo but selected for pfmdr1 N86Y and pfcrt K76T polymorphisms associated with decreased drug sensitivity.
- **Effect:** reduction in malaria incidence 96%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 1 year
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27401569/

### Safety and Immunogenicity of GSK Biologicals' Investigational Malaria Vaccine in HIV Infected Infants and Children
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT01148459)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2016-07-07
- **Trial period:** 2010-07-30 to 2013-05-24
- **Intervention(s):** GSK Biological's Investigational Malaria Vaccine 257049; Human Diploid Cell Vaccine (HDCV) or Purified Vero Cell Rabies Vaccine (PVRV, Verorab) (Aventis Pasteur);; Purified Chick Embryo Cell Culture (PCEC) Rabies Vaccine (Rabipur or equivalent) (Novartis).
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** HIV-infected infants and children aged 6 weeks to 17 months with WHO stage 1 or 2 HIV disease
- **N:** 200
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01 was well tolerated in HIV-infected children with WHO stage 1 or 2 disease, with a similar overall rate of serious adverse events compared to rabies vaccine recipients and no deaths related to vaccination.
- **Effect:** relative risk of serious adverse events 1.1 (95% CI 0.8-1.6)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 14 months
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01148459

### Appropriate targeting of artemisinin-based combination therapy by community health workers using malaria rapid diagnostic tests: findings from randomized trials in two contrasting areas of high and low malaria transmission in south-western Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27383558)
- **Journal:** Tropical medicine & international health : TM & IH
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-08-09
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children under 5 years of age with fever seen by community health workers
- **N:** 2575
- **Impact:** Use of malaria rapid diagnostic tests by community health workers substantially improved the appropriate targeting of ACT treatment compared to presumptive diagnosis in both high and low malaria transmission areas of Uganda.
- **Effect:** proportion appropriately targeted ACT treatment 79.3% vs 30.8% (high transmission area); 90.1% vs 7.8% (low transmission area)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27383558/

### Efficacy of topical mosquito repellent (picaridin) plus long-lasting insecticidal nets versus long-lasting insecticidal nets alone for control of malaria: a cluster randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27371977)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases

- **Place:** Cambodia (Ratanakiri province)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-29
- **Trial period:** 2012 to 2013
- **Class:** spatial repellent
- **Population:** randomly selected individuals in study villages (cross-sectional surveys)
- **N:** 98
- **Impact:** Adding topical picaridin repellent to long-lasting insecticidal nets did not reduce malaria prevalence compared with nets alone, largely due to suboptimal daily repellent use.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio 1.01 (2012); 1.31 (2013) (95% CI 0.60-1.70 (2012); 0.81-2.11 (2013))
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 2 years (2012-2013 transmission seasons)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27371977/

### No Reduction in Hemoglobin Level in Severe Plasmodium falciparum Malaria Treated with Artesunate in Central Sudan.

- **Impact:** There was no significant difference in hemoglobin levels between patients treated with artesunate versus quinine for severe malaria.

### Seven-Year Efficacy of RTS,S/AS01 Malaria Vaccine among Young African Children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27355532)
- **Journal:** The New England journal of medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-01
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** young children
- **N:** 447
- **Impact:** RTS,S/AS01 was initially protective against clinical malaria but showed no overall efficacy over 7 years, with waning and eventually negative efficacy in high-exposure areas by year 5.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy 4.4% (intention-to-treat); 7.0% (per-protocol) (95% CI -17.0 to 21.9 (ITT); -14.5 to 24.6 (per-protocol))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7 years

### Therapeutic efficacy trial of artemisinin-based combination therapy for the treatment of uncomplicated malaria and investigation of mutations in k13 propeller domain in Togo, 2012-2013.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27334876)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Togo
- **Design:** open-label, multi-site, phase IV therapeutic efficacy trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-22
- **Trial period:** 2012 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 523
- **Impact:** Both artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine remained highly efficacious for treating uncomplicated malaria in Togo, with high PCR-corrected cure rates and low prevalence of k13 mutations.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response (ACPR) 96.3-100% (ASAQ), 97-100% (AL)

### Evaluate Azithromycin Plus Chloroquine And Sulfadoxine Plus Pyrimethamine Combinations For Intermittent Preventive Treatment Of Falciparum Malaria Infection In Pregnant Women In Africa

### Evaluation of a topical formulation of eprinomectin against Anopheles arabiensis when administered to Zebu cattle (Bos indicus) under field conditions.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27317557)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, pilot field trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-17
- **Trial period:** 2014-09 to 2014-10
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** Homes and cattle in Samia District, western Kenya; mosquito vector populations sampled indoors
- **N:** 100
- **Impact:** Topical eprinomectin treatment of cattle significantly reduced indoor resting density of Anopheles arabiensis by 38% at one week post-treatment compared to control, though the effect was not sustained at two weeks and other Anopheles species increased.
- **Effect:** percent reduction in indoor resting density 38% (95% CI Control mean 1.33 [1.08, 1.64]; Treatment 0.79 [0.56, 1.07])
- **Follow-up:** 2 weeks

### Safety and High Level Efficacy of the Combination Malaria Vaccine Regimen of RTS,S/AS01B With Chimpanzee Adenovirus 63 and Modified Vaccinia Ankara Vectored Vaccines Expressing ME-TRAP.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27307573)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase II
- **Place:** United Kingdom
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-15
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** healthy malaria-naive adults
- **N:** 37
- **Impact:** Both vaccination regimens showed high sterile protective efficacy against controlled human malaria infection, with combination and RTS,S/AS01B-alone groups showing similar high protection.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 82.4% (group 1), 75% (group 2)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months (repeat CHMI)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27307573/

### The Impact of an Intervention to Improve Malaria Care in Public Health Centers on Health Indicators of Children in Tororo, Uganda (PRIME): A Cluster-Randomized Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27273646)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tororo, Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-06
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children under 5 years of age in communities near public health centers
- **N:** 8766
- **Impact:** The PRIME intervention to improve malaria case management at health centers did not reduce prevalence of anemia or parasitemia in community children compared with control.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk ratio 1.01 (95% CI 0.91-1.13)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 18 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27273646/

### Clinical illness and outcomes in Nigerian children with late-appearing anaemia after artemisinin-based combination treatments of uncomplicated falciparum malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27246468)
- **Journal:** BMC infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-06-01
- **Trial period:** 2015
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 609
- **Impact:** Late-appearing anaemia occurred in 84 of 609 children treated with ACTs, was mostly mild and asymptomatic, and resolved uneventfully in most cases.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio AOR 2.0-7.5 for various predictors of LAA/failure of recovery (95% CI 1.0-22.9 (varies by predictor))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27246468/

### Persistence of Plasmodium falciparum parasitemia after artemisinin combination therapy: evidence from a randomized trial in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27197604)
- **Journal:** Scientific reports
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized trial, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-05-20
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **Impact:** Genetically diverse ring stage parasites persisted at low levels for up to 14 days after ACT or ACT-primaquine treatment despite no clinical treatment failures.
- **Effect:** prevalence of ring stage parasite transcripts >25% prevalence at day 14; >98% cleared within first 3 days
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 14 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27197604/

### Acute Kidney Injury Is Common in Pediatric Severe Malaria and Is Associated With Increased Mortality.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27186577)
- **Journal:** Open forum infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized, placebo-controlled
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-26
- **Trial period:** 2011 to 2013
- **Population:** children aged 1 to 10 years with severe malaria
- **N:** 180
- **Impact:** AKI was common (45.5%) in pediatric severe malaria and was associated with increased mortality, longer hospitalization, and neurodisability, particularly in children receiving placebo, while children receiving inhaled nitric oxide had higher rates of AKI but no significant association between AKI and mortality or neurodisability.
- **Effect:** relative risk of AKI in iNO vs placebo arm 1.36 (95% CI 1.03-1.80)
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27186577/

### Effects of an intervention on infant growth and development: evidence for different mechanisms at work.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27146248)
- **Journal:** Maternal & child nutrition
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-05-04
- **Population:** infants aged 9 to 18 months
- **Impact:** The intervention (lipid-based nutrient supplements plus malaria and diarrhoea treatment) had independent positive effects on both linear growth and neurodevelopment, suggesting different underlying mechanisms rather than mediation through growth alone.
- **Effect:** standardized mean difference (SD units) ~0.3 SD on LAZ and ~0.3 SD on motor, language and personal-social scores; effects decreased to 0.21, 0.27, and 0.29 respectively after adjusting for LAZ change
- **Follow-up:** 9 to 18 months of age
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27146248/

### Mobile Phone Short Message Service to Improve Malaria Pharmacoadherence in Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27145248)
- **Journal:** Journal of nursing scholarship : an official publication of Sigma Theta Tau International Honor Society of Nursing
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-05-03
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2014
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adult malaria patients
- **N:** 96
- **Impact:** SMS reminder messages did not significantly improve pharmacoadherence to malaria medication compared to no SMS reminders.
- **Effect:** chi-square association χ2 = 0.19, df = 1
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27145248/

### Clinical and Biological Efficacy of Mirasol-treated Fresh Whole Blood for the Prevention of Transfusion-transmitted Malaria
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT02118428)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, NA
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2016-04-01
- **Trial period:** 2014-03 to 2014-12
- **Intervention(s):** Mirasol-treated Whole Blood; Untreated Whole Blood
- **Population:** adult patients (aged ≥18 years) with blood group O+ requiring whole blood transfusion
- **N:** 227
- **Impact:** Treatment of whole blood with the Mirasol pathogen reduction system significantly reduced the incidence of transfusion-transmitted malaria compared with untreated whole blood.
- **Effect:** incidence of transfusion-transmitted malaria 4% (1/28) treated vs 22% (8/37) untreated
- **Species:** Plasmodium spp
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02118428

### In vivo efficacy of artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine for uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Malawi, 2014.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27113085)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Malawi
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-04-26
- **Trial period:** 2014 to 2014
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** febrile children aged 6-59 months with microscopy-confirmed uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 452
- **Impact:** Both artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine remained highly efficacious for treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Malawi, with PCR-corrected day 28 success rates over 99%.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected day 28 cumulative success rate 99% (ASAQ), 99.3% (AL) (95% CI 97.2-100% (ASAQ); 98.3-100% (AL))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27113085/

### Malaria illness mediated by anaemia lessens cognitive development in younger Ugandan children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27076184)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-04-14
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** infants enrolled at 4-6 months, followed to 2 and 3 years of age; HIV-exposed uninfected and HIV-unexposed uninfected children
- **N:** 469
- **Impact:** Malarial illness episodes negatively predicted cognitive performance at 2 and 3 years, an effect mediated by anaemia, and this relationship was stronger in HIV-exposed uninfected children, while dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine was highly protective against malaria and anaemia but trial arm itself was not associated with neurodevelopmental outcomes.
- **Effect:** association (regression coefficient) between malaria episodes and MSEL cognitive score negative association
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27076184/

### Interceptor® long-lasting insecticidal net: phase III evaluation over three years of household use and calibration with Phase II experimental hut outcomes.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27075874)
- **Journal:** Parasites & vectors
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-04-13
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** households in a malaria-endemic area of Tanzania
- **N:** 934
- **Impact:** Interceptor LN retained bioefficacy and insecticide content far better than conventionally treated nets over 36 months, with more than 80% meeting WHOPES Phase III criteria, leading to full WHO recommendation.
- **Effect:** percentage of nets meeting WHOPES efficacy criteria at 36 months 87% (Interceptor LN) vs 0% (CTN)
- **Follow-up:** 36 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27075874/

### The Impact of Hotspot-Targeted Interventions on Malaria Transmission in Rachuonyo South District in the Western Kenyan Highlands: A Cluster-Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27071072)
- **Journal:** PLoS medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-04-12
- **Trial period:** 2011 to 2012
- **Class:** MDA; ITN/LLIN; IRS; larval source management; diagnostic
- **Population:** individuals residing in serologically defined malaria hotspots and surrounding communities
- **N:** 4550
- **Impact:** Hotspot-targeted interventions modestly and transiently reduced parasite prevalence inside hotspots but had no significant effect on transmission in the surrounding evaluation zones.
- **Effect:** reduction in nPCR parasite prevalence inside hotspots 10.2% (95% CI -1.3 to 21.7%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 16 weeks post-intervention
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27071072/

### Effective Antimalarial Chemoprevention in Childhood Enhances the Quality of CD4+ T Cells and Limits Their Production of Immunoregulatory Interleukin 10.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27067196)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-04-10
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6 to 24 months
- **N:** 177
- **Impact:** Monthly dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine chemoprevention reduced malaria episodes and enhanced protective CD4+ T-cell responses while limiting immunoregulatory IL-10 production compared to no chemoprevention.
- **Effect:** reduction in malaria episodes 55% overall (97% among highly adherent) during intervention; 55% reduction in the year after cessation among highly adherent
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** additional year after cessation of chemoprevention (total ~2 years)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27067196/

### Risk factors for Plasmodium falciparum and Plasmodium vivax gametocyte carriage in Papua New Guinean children with uncomplicated malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (27056132)
- **Journal:** Acta tropica
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase III
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-04-04
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 0.5-5 years with uncomplicated malaria
- **N:** 230
- **Impact:** Low haemoglobin, low asexual parasite density and higher spleen grade were associated with pre-treatment gametocyte positivity, while post-treatment P. falciparum gametocytaemia was independently predicted by AN treatment, haemoglobin, asexual parasitaemia and day post-treatment, and P. vivax gametocytaemia was strongly predicted by presence of P. vivax asexual forms.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 4.09 (AN vs AL for post-treatment P. falciparum gametocytaemia) (95% CI 1.43-11.65)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** not stated (post-treatment follow-up days)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27056132/

### Monthly sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine combination versus daily proguanil for malaria chemoprophylaxis in sickle cell disease: a randomized controlled study at the Jos University Teaching Hospital.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (29944308)
- **Journal:** Nigerian journal of medicine : journal of the National Association of Resident Doctors of Nigeria
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Jos, Plateau State, Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-01
- **Class:** chemoprevention
- **Population:** patients with sickle cell disease in steady state (children and adults)
- **N:** 154
- **Impact:** Monthly sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine was more efficacious than daily proguanil in reducing malaria parasitaemia, clinical malaria attacks, and sickle cell crises among sickle cell disease patients.
- **Effect:** reduction in prevalence of malaria parasitaemia 14% (SP) vs 30% (Proguanil)
- **Follow-up:** 3 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29944308/

### Community point distribution of insecticide-treated bed nets and community health worker hang-up visits in rural Zambia: a decision-focused evaluation.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26939695)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, community-based evaluation
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-03-03
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2014
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** rural households in Rufunsa District, Zambia
- **N:** 560
- **Impact:** Community point distribution achieved high attendance and retention rates, and CHW hang-up visits modestly increased short-term ITN usage but had no medium-term effect on retention or usage.
- **Effect:** percentage of ITNs hanging/usage 65.6% hanging at 7-11 weeks vs 63.1% at 5-6 months; CHW visit associated with increased short-term usage
- **Follow-up:** 5-6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26939695/

### Can Rapid Diagnostic Testing for Malaria Increase Adherence to Artemether-Lumefantrine?: A Randomized Controlled Trial in Uganda.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26928828)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-29
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** patients purchasing a course of artemether-lumefantrine at private drug shops
- **N:** 1525
- **Impact:** Offering a free malaria rapid diagnostic test did not significantly improve adherence to artemether-lumefantrine, while patients who felt healthy by day two had lower odds of completing treatment.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio 1.07 (95% CI 0.734-1.57)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26928828/

### A Phase 3, Double-Blind, Randomized Study of Arterolane Maleate-Piperaquine Phosphate vs Artemether-Lumefantrine for Falciparum Malaria in Adolescent and Adult Patients in Asia and Africa.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26908796)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** India, Asia, Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-21
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adolescent and adult patients with uncomplicated P. falciparum monoinfection, aged 12-65 years
- **N:** 1072
- **Impact:** Arterolane maleate-piperaquine phosphate showed comparable efficacy and safety to artemether-lumefantrine for uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in adolescents and adults.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected ACPR (ITT) on day 28 92.86% (AM-PQP) vs 92.46% (A-L)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26908796/

### Incremental impact upon malaria transmission of supplementing pyrethroid-impregnated long-lasting insecticidal nets with indoor residual spraying using pyrethroids or the organophosphate, pirimiphos methyl.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26893012)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** quasi-randomized, cluster, longitudinal
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-18
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS; diagnostic
- **Population:** residents of population clusters (community members tested for malaria by community health workers)
- **N:** 14
- **Impact:** Supplementing pyrethroid LLINs with IRS using the organophosphate pirimiphos methyl (especially the CS formulation) provided greater and longer-lasting incremental protection against malaria than supplementing with pyrethroid IRS formulations.
- **Effect:** incremental protective efficacy (IPE) 0.63 (PM CS, first 3 months); 0.79 (PM CS over 6 months); 0.31 (lambdacyhalothrin); 0.23 (PM EC); 0.19 (deltamethrin) (95% CI 0.57-0.69 (PM CS 3 months); 0.75-0.83 (PM CS 6 months); 0.10-0.47 (lambdacyhalothrin); 0.15-0.31 (PM EC); -0.01-0.35 (deltamethrin))
- **Follow-up:** 29 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26893012/

### Tafenoquine treatment of Plasmodium vivax malaria: suggestive evidence that CYP2D6 reduced metabolism is not associated with relapse in the Phase 2b DETECTIVE trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26888075)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2b
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-18
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** P. vivax-infected patients treated with tafenoquine or primaquine plus chloroquine
- **N:** 198
- **Impact:** Reduced CYP2D6 metabolism (intermediate metabolizer phenotype) was associated with higher relapse rates in primaquine-treated patients but not in tafenoquine-treated patients, suggesting tafenoquine efficacy is less affected by CYP2D6 metabolism than primaquine.
- **Effect:** relapse rate by CYP2D6 metabolizer status Relapse in 50% of PQ-treated CYP2D6 IMs vs 17% of PQ-treated EMs who remained relapse-free; no significant difference in TQ-treated IMs vs EMs
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26888075/

### In Vivo Efficacy of Artesunate/Sulphadoxine-Pyrimethamine versus Artesunate/Amodiaquine in the Treatment of Uncomplicated P. falciparium Malaria in Children around the Slope of Mount Cameroon: A Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (28536372)
- **Journal:** Biomedicines
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Cameroon (Mount Cameroon slope)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial, open-label (implied), phase not applicable
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-15
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged six months to 10 years with uncomplicated mono-infected P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 177
- **Impact:** Both artesunate/sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine and artesunate/amodiaquine were highly effective and well-tolerated treatments for uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria, with PCR corrected cure rates of 85.9% and 90.2%, respectively.
- **Effect:** PCR corrected cure rate (ACPR) 88.1% overall; 85.9% (AS/SP) and 90.2% (AS/AQ) (95% CI 95% CI 83.1-93.1 overall; 78.2-93.6 (AS/SP); 83.8-96.6 (AS/AQ))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28536372/

### A Randomized Comparison of Chloroquine Versus Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine for the Treatment of Plasmodium vivax Infection in Vietnam.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26856909)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Vietnam
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-08
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** Vietnamese patients with symptomatic Plasmodium vivax mono-infections
- **N:** 128
- **Impact:** DHA-PPQ was non-inferior to chloroquine and provided faster fever and parasite clearance, while chloroquine remained efficacious for treating P. vivax malaria in southern Vietnam.
- **Effect:** absolute difference in adequate clinical and parasitological response 19% (95% CI 0-37%)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 至 day 33 and beyond (recurrent parasitemia monitoring)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26856909/

### Comparison of the safety and efficacy of fixed-dose combination of arterolane maleate and piperaquine phosphate with chloroquine in acute, uncomplicated Plasmodium vivax malaria: a phase III, multicentric, open-label study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26818020)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** India
- **Design:** phase III, multicentric, open-label, randomized
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-27
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients aged 13-65 years with confirmed mono-infection of P. vivax and fever or fever in the previous 48 h
- **N:** 317
- **Impact:** Fixed-dose combination of arterolane maleate and piperaquine phosphate was as effective and safe as chloroquine for treating acute uncomplicated P. vivax malaria, with similar cure rates and adverse event profiles.
- **Effect:** proportion aparasitaemic and afebrile at 72h (PP population) 100% (FDC) vs 99.3% (chloroquine) (95% CI 96.52-100.0)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 42 days (with day 28 cure rate assessment)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26818020/

### In Vivo Efficacy and Parasite Clearance of Artesunate + Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine Versus Artemether-Lumefantrine in Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26811430)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** Randomized, open-label, comparative, in vivo assay, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-25
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2014
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with uncomplicated falciparum malaria, at least 6 months of age
- **Impact:** Both AS + SP and AL showed high efficacy with no significant difference in cure rates or parasite clearance half-life, though children under 8 years had higher risk of treatment failure.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response 100% (AS+SP) vs 98.2% (AL)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days

### Intramuscular Artesunate for Severe Malaria in African Children: A Multicenter Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26757276)
- **Journal:** PLoS medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Africa (five African countries, seven sites)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-12
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with severe malaria, aged 0.5-10 years
- **N:** 1047
- **Impact:** The simplified three-dose intramuscular artesunate regimen was non-inferior to the standard five-dose intramuscular regimen, but the three-dose intravenous regimen did not show non-inferiority.
- **Effect:** difference in proportion with ≥99% parasitemia reduction at 24h three-dose IM: 78% vs five-dose IM: 79%; three-dose IV: 74% vs five-dose IM: 79% (95% CI three-dose IM vs five-dose: -7 to 5; three-dose IV vs five-dose: -12 to 1)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** at least 7 days post-admission (for delayed anemia assessment)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26757276/

### Malaria burden and anti-malarial drug efficacy in Owando, northern Congo.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26743431)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Owando, Republic of Congo
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-08
- **Trial period:** 2012 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** febrile children under 12 years with acute uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 167
- **Impact:** Both ASAQ and AL were highly efficacious against uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria, with PCR-corrected cure rates of 100% for ASAQ and 98.0% for AL, though ASAQ was associated with more adverse events.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response ASAQ 100%, AL 98.0%

### The Effect of Indoor Residual Spraying on the Prevalence of Malaria Parasite Infection, Clinical Malaria and Anemia in an Area of Perennial Transmission and Moderate Coverage of Insecticide Treated Nets in Western Kenya.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26731524)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** cluster-sampled, propensity score matched, comparison district design (quasi-experimental, repeated cross-sectional surveys)
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-05
- **Trial period:** 2008 to 2009
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** residents of randomly selected compounds, including children under 5
- **Impact:** After two rounds of indoor residual spraying, the IRS district had significantly lower prevalence of malaria parasitemia, clinical malaria, and (in children under 5) anemia compared to the non-IRS district.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 0.36 (parasitemia), 0.37 (clinical malaria), 0.30 (anemia in children under 5) (95% CI 0.22-0.59; 0.20-0.68; 0.13-0.71)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** from baseline through after second round of IRS (April-May 2009)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26731524/
