### [Comparison of the therapeutic efficiency and of the tolerance of the artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine combination in Niger].
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (30079656)
- **Journal:** Le Mali medical
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Niger
- **Design:** randomized, open-label, comparative, two-arm, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-01
- **Trial period:** 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** febrile patients with malaria
- **N:** 159
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine and artesunate-amodiaquine showed comparable efficacy and safety with no statistically significant difference between the two combinations.
- **Effect:** adequate clinical and parasitological response rate 94.8% (AL) vs 97.1% (AM)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30079656/

### Safety of insecticide-treated mosquito nets for infants and their mothers: randomized controlled community trial in Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26714758)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-12-30
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** newborns and their mothers
- **N:** 180
- **Impact:** ITNs were found to be sufficiently safe for use in newborns and their mothers, with no significant differences in side effects between groups except more frequent headaches in mothers of infants protected from birth.
- **Effect:** difference in frequency of headache higher in group A mothers
- **Follow-up:** 18 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26714758/

### Safety, Immunogenicity, and Protective Efficacy of Intradermal Immunization with Aseptic, Purified, Cryopreserved Plasmodium falciparum Sporozoites in Volunteers Under Chloroquine Prophylaxis: A Randomized Controlled Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26711509)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** United States
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-12-28
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody; chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** healthy adult volunteers under chloroquine prophylaxis
- **N:** 30
- **Impact:** Intradermal immunization with cryopreserved PfSPZ under chloroquine prophylaxis was safe but induced only minimal immune responses and did not protect against controlled human malaria infection.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 0%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** approximately 9 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26711509/

### [Evaluation of the efficacy and safety of three 2-drug combinations for the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in Senegal: artesunate-amodiaquine, dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine, and artemether-lumefantrine].
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26644184)
- **Journal:** Medecine et sante tropicales
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Senegal
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-01-01
- **Trial period:** October 2010 to February 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adults and children with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 393
- **Impact:** All three artemisinin-based combination therapies (AL, ASAQ, DHAPQ) showed high and similar efficacy with PCR-corrected ACPR at day 28 ranging from 89.2% to 92.8% and were well tolerated with no serious adverse events.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected ACPR at day 28 (ITT) 92.8% (AL), 89.2% (ASAQ), 91.1% (DHAPQ)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26644184/

### Summary of anti-malarial prophylactic efficacy of tafenoquine from three placebo-controlled studies of residents of malaria-endemic countries.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26610844)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-11-26
- **Trial period:** late 1990s to early 2000s
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** residents of malaria-endemic African countries naturally exposed to P. falciparum
- **N:** 519
- **Impact:** Tafenoquine 200-mg weekly regimen provided high prophylactic efficacy against P. falciparum malaria comparable to mefloquine in African residents.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 93.1% (95% CI 89.1-95.6%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 12-26 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26610844/

### Safety and efficacy of re-treatments with pyronaridine-artesunate in African patients with malaria: a substudy of the WANECAM randomised trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26601738)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3b/4
- **Place:** Mali, Burkina Faso, Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-10-23
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients aged ≥6 months and bodyweight ≥5 kg with uncomplicated microscopically confirmed Plasmodium spp malaria
- **N:** 1344
- **Impact:** Pyronaridine-artesunate showed similar safety and efficacy on first treatment versus re-treatment of subsequent malaria episodes, supporting its wider use as an alternative artemisinin-based combination therapy.
- **Effect:** adequate clinical and parasitological response (ACPR) at day 28 92.7% (pyronaridine-artesunate) vs 80.4% (artemether-lumefantrine) (95% CI 91.0-94.3 vs 77.8-83.0)
- **Species:** Plasmodium spp (mixed)
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26601738/

### Risks of Hemolysis in Glucose-6-Phosphate Dehydrogenase Deficient Infants Exposed to Chlorproguanil-Dapsone, Mefloquine and Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine as Part of Intermittent Presumptive Treatment of Malaria in Infants.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26599634)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-11-23
- **Class:** PMC
- **Population:** infants (asymptomatic, some with G6PD deficiency)
- **Impact:** Chlorproguanil-dapsone caused greater reductions in hemoglobin than sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine or mefloquine, and G6PD deficient infants had higher hemolysis risk overall, but G6PD deficiency did not specifically worsen chlorproguanil-dapsone-associated hemoglobin declines.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio for Hb <8 g/dL in homo-/hemizygous G6PD genotype 6.7 (95% CI 1.7 to 27.0)
- **Follow-up:** 28 days post-dose
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26599634/

### Artesunate/Amodiaquine Versus Artemether/Lumefantrine for the Treatment of Uncomplicated Malaria in Uganda: A Randomized Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26597254)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-11-23
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2014
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 602
- **Impact:** AS/AQ treatment resulted in fewer recurrent parasitemia episodes than AL treatment across all three sites.
- **Effect:** risk of recurrent parasitemia 28.6% (AS/AQ) vs 44.6% (AL)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26597254/

### Seasonal malaria chemoprevention in an area of extended seasonal transmission in Ashanti, Ghana: an individually randomised clinical trial.

- **Type:** trial

- **Place:** Ghana

- **Status:** published

- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** children aged 3-59 months
- **N:** 2400
- **Impact:** Seasonal malaria chemoprevention reduced the incidence of malaria in children during the rainy season compared to placebo.
- **Effect:** adjusted hazard ratio 0.62 (95% CI 0.41-0.93)
- **Follow-up:** rainy season (five SMC courses)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26578353/

### Introducing rapid diagnostic tests for malaria into registered drug shops in Uganda: lessons learned and policy implications.

- **Type:** trial

- **Impact:** Introducing RDTs into registered drug shops in Uganda was feasible and increased appropriate treatment of malaria with artemisinin-based combination therapy.

### Combination of Insecticide Treated Nets and Indoor Residual Spraying in Northern Tanzania Provides Additional Reduction in Vector Population Density and Malaria Transmission Rates Compared to Insecticide Treated Nets Alone: A Randomised Control Trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26569492)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-11-16
- **Trial period:** 2011 to 2012
- **Class:** vaccine; ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** households in study clusters (general population)
- **N:** 40
- **Impact:** Combining IRS with bendiocarb and ITNs reduced Anopheles gambiae s.l. density by 84% and EIR compared to ITNs alone in northern Tanzania.
- **Effect:** percentage reduction in mean An. gambiae s.l. density 84% (95% CI 56%-94%)

### Incidence of malaria by cotrimoxazole use in HIV-infected Ugandan adults on antiretroviral therapy: a randomised, placebo-controlled study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26558729)
- **Journal:** AIDS (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2016-02-01
- **Population:** HIV-infected adults stable on ART and CTX with CD4 cell count at least 250 cells/μl
- **N:** 2180
- **Impact:** Stopping cotrimoxazole prophylaxis in HIV-infected adults on ART was associated with a higher incidence of malaria compared with continuing cotrimoxazole, though the difference was smaller than previously reported and severe malaria was rare.
- **Effect:** rate ratio 3.47 (95% CI 2.74-4.39)
- **Follow-up:** median 2.5 years
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26558729/

### A randomized trial of the efficacy of artesunate and three quinine regimens in the treatment of severe malaria in children at the Ebolowa Regional Hospital, Cameroon.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26520401)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Cameroon
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-10-31
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 3 months to 15 years with severe P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 116
- **Impact:** Artesunate resulted in significantly shorter fever and parasite clearance times and higher parasitaemia reduction at H24 compared to quinine regimens, demonstrating greater efficacy in treating severe malaria in children.
- **Effect:** fever and parasite clearance time significantly shorter with artesunate
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** until clinical recovery (fever/parasite clearance)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26520401/

### Artesunate-amodiaquine versus artemether-lumefantrine for the treatment of acute uncomplicated malaria in Congolese children under 10 years old living in a suburban area: a randomized study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26511848)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal

- **Place:** Republic of Congo (Brazzaville)
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-10-29
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children under 10 years old with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria living in a suburban area
- **N:** 262
- **Impact:** Both artesunate-amodiaquine and artemether-lumefantrine showed excellent and similar efficacy in treating uncomplicated malaria in children, with sickle cell trait not affecting treatment outcomes.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response 97.0% (ASAQ) vs 96.4% (AL)
- **Species:** P. falciparum

- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26511848/

### Temporal changes in haematocrit following artemisinin-based combination treatments of uncomplicated falciparum malaria in children.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26502714)
- **Journal:** BMC infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-10-26
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 1180
- **Impact:** Following ACT treatment for uncomplicated falciparum malaria, haematocrit commonly increased or fell, with declines in haematocrit deficit from <30% being mono-exponential and similar between treatment groups.
- **Effect:** anaemia half-time 1.32 d (AA) versus 1.14 d (AL)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26502714/

### Efficacy of Olyset® Duo, a permethrin and pyriproxyfen mixture net against wild pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae s.s. from Côte d'Ivoire: an experimental hut trial.

- **Status:** published

- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** wild pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae s.s. mosquitoes
- **Impact:** Olyset Duo (permethrin plus pyriproxyfen) had a significant impact on fertility, reducing it by 7-12% relative to control, but no effect on fecundity of wild multi-resistant An. gambiae s.s.
- **Effect:** fertility reduction 7-12%

### Genetic Diversity and Protective Efficacy of the RTS,S/AS01 Malaria Vaccine.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26488565)
- **Journal:** The New England journal of medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-10-21
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** infants and children (5 to 17 months and 6 to 12 weeks of age)
- **N:** 4985
- **Impact:** The RTS,S/AS01 vaccine showed greater efficacy against malaria parasites whose circumsporozoite protein matched the vaccine allele than against mismatched parasites in children 5 to 17 months of age, but not in infants 6 to 12 weeks of age.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy (cumulative incidence) 50.3% (matched) vs 33.4% (mismatched) (95% CI 34.6-62.3 (matched); 29.3-37.2 (mismatched))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 1 year
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26488565/

### Antimalarial activity of artefenomel (OZ439), a novel synthetic antimalarial endoperoxide, in patients with Plasmodium falciparum and Plasmodium vivax malaria: an open-label phase 2 trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26448141)

- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Thailand
- **Design:** open-label, dose-escalation, phase 2a trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-10-05
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2012
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** adult patients with acute, uncomplicated P falciparum or P vivax malaria
- **N:** 82
- **Impact:** A single oral dose of artefenomel at 200-1200 mg rapidly cleared parasitaemia in both P falciparum and P vivax malaria with all doses similarly effective and generally mild, reversible adverse effects.
- **Effect:** parasite reduction rate per 24 h 0.90-1.88 (P falciparum), 2.09-2.53 (P vivax)
- **Species:** mixed

### Chemopreventive Therapy for Malaria in Ugandan Children
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT00948896)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Published:** 2015-09-24
- **Trial period:** 2010-06 to 2014-04
- **Intervention(s):** trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole (TS; TMP/SMX); sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine (SP); dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine (DP)
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children randomized at 6 months of age, followed to 24 months of age
- **N:** 600
- **Impact:** Monthly dihydroartemisinin-piperaquine chemoprevention reduced malaria incidence overall, with protective efficacy strongly dependent on piperaquine drug exposure, reaching nearly 100% protection in children with the highest exposure.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 54% overall (31% low, 67% medium, 97% high PQ exposure groups) (95% CI 95% CI 39-66% overall; 6-49% (low), 54-76% (medium), 89-99% (high))
- **Follow-up:** 18 months (6 to 24 months of age)
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT00948896

### Sustained Release Formulation of Primaquine for Prevention of Relapse of Plasmodium vivax Malaria: A Randomized, Double-Blind, Comparative, Multicentric Study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26366319)
- **Journal:** Malaria research and treatment
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Design:** randomized, double-blind, comparative, multicentric, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-08-20
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with microscopically confirmed P. vivax malaria who were aparasitemic and asymptomatic after chloroquine therapy
- **N:** 358
- **Impact:** Primaquine SR 15 mg and SR 30 mg showed comparable low relapse rates and safety to conventional primaquine 15 mg, with SR 30 mg showing significantly better drug compliance.
- **Effect:** relapse rate 3 relapses (2.86%) with conventional primaquine 15 mg vs 1 relapse (0.93%) with primaquine SR 30 mg
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26366319/

### Effect of Educational Outreach Timing and Duration on Facility Performance for Infectious Disease Care in Uganda: A Trial with Pre-Post and Cluster Randomized Controlled Components.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26352257)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-09-09
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2011
- **Population:** outpatients at health facilities receiving infectious disease care (emergency triage, malaria, pneumonia services)
- **N:** 36
- **Impact:** Delayed educational outreach combined with training improved several facility performance indicators similarly to immediate outreach, and extended less-frequent outreach maintained most gains with mixed effects on individual indicators.
- **Effect:** adjusted relative risk (aRR) aRR ranged from 0.67 to 2.97 across indicators (95% CI 99% CI varied by indicator, e.g. 1.11-2.15, 1.10-2.75, 0.55-0.82, 1.44-6.17)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 23 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26352257/

### Efficacy and Safety of Artesunate-Amodiaquine versus Artemether-Lumefantrine in the Treatment of Uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum Malaria in Sentinel Sites across Côte d'Ivoire.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26347849)

- **Status:** published

- **Population:** Patients aged 2 years and above with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 300
- **Impact:** Both Artesunate-Amodiaquine and Artemether-Lumefantrine showed high efficacy and good tolerability, with no significant difference between the two treatments.

### Ferroquine and artesunate in African adults and children with Plasmodium falciparum malaria: a phase 2, multicentre, randomised, double-blind, dose-ranging, non-inferiority study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26342427)
- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Gabon, Burkina Faso, Benin, Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-09-03
- **Trial period:** 2009 to 2010
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** African adults, adolescents, and children with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum monoinfection
- **N:** 326
- **Impact:** All three dose groups of ferroquine plus artesunate achieved high PCR-corrected cure rates (97-99%) at day 28 and met the non-inferiority hypothesis, with a safety profile consistent with malaria treatment.
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected cure rate at day 28 97% (2 mg/kg), 99% (4 mg/kg), 99% (6 mg/kg) ferroquine plus artesunate vs 79% ferroquine monotherapy (95% CI 90-100 (2 mg/kg); 93-100 (4 mg/kg); 93-100 (6 mg/kg); 68-88 (monotherapy))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 63 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26342427/

### Immunogenicity of the RTS,S/AS01 malaria vaccine and implications for duration of vaccine efficacy: secondary analysis of data from a phase 3 randomised controlled trial.

- **Journal:** The Lancet. Infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial

- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody

- **N:** 15459
- **Impact:** Waning anti-circumsporozoite antibody titres predict the magnitude and duration of RTS,S/AS01 vaccine efficacy against clinical malaria, with efficacy waning more rapidly at higher transmission intensity.

- **Species:** P. falciparum

### Inhaled Nitric Oxide as an Adjunctive Treatment for Cerebral Malaria in Children: A Phase II Randomized Open-Label Clinical Trial.

- **Status:** published

- **N:** 92
- **Impact:** Inhaled nitric oxide as an adjunctive treatment for cerebral malaria was safe and increased blood methemoglobin and plasma nitrate levels but did not produce a greater increase in plasma Ang-1 compared to nitrogen.

### Efficacy of a novel sublingual spray formulation of artemether in African children with Plasmodium falciparum malaria.

- **Status:** published

- **Population:** children weighing 5-15 kg with severe/complicated or uncomplicated malaria unable to tolerate oral medication
- **N:** 182
- **Impact:** Sublingual artemether resulted in significantly higher parasitological success rates and faster parasite clearance than intravenous quinine in African children with P. falciparum malaria.

### A Non-Inferiority, Individually Randomized Trial of Intermittent Screening and Treatment versus Intermittent Preventive Treatment in the Control of Malaria in Pregnancy.

- **Impact:** Intermittent screening and treatment with RDT and artemether-lumefantrine (ISTp-AL) was non-inferior to intermittent preventive treatment with sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine (IPTp-SP) in preventing low birth weight, anemia, and placental malaria.

### A randomized trial of artesunate-amodiaquine versus artemether-lumefantrine for the treatment of acute uncomplicated malaria in pregnancy.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26169336)
- **Journal:** International journal of gynaecology and obstetrics: the official organ of the International Federation of Gynaecology and Obstetrics
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-07-03
- **Trial period:** 2013 to 2013
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** symptomatic pregnant women (second/third trimester) with malaria parasitemia
- **N:** 150
- **Impact:** Artesunate-amodiaquine and artemether-lumefantrine showed similar efficacy for treating uncomplicated falciparum malaria in pregnancy, but adverse effects were more common with artesunate-amodiaquine.
- **Effect:** day-28 cure rate 98.5% vs 94.4%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26169336/

### Insights Into Circulating Cytokine Dynamics During Pregnancy in HIV-Infected Beninese Exposed to Plasmodium falciparum Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26101276)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-06-22
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** HIV-infected pregnant women
- **N:** 200
- **Impact:** In HIV-infected pregnant women, circulating IL-10 was associated with P. falciparum infection while IFN-γ appeared to mark anti-malarial protection, with cytokine levels changing between inclusion and delivery.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26101276/

### Mefloquine Versus Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine for Intermittent Preventive Treatment in Pregnancy: A Joint Analysis on Efficacy and Tolerability.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26055735)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-06-08
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 1601
- **Impact:** Mefloquine was found to be overall superior to sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine for intermittent preventive treatment in pregnancy when jointly assessing efficacy and tolerability.
- **Effect:** overall superiority (multiple outcome analysis)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26055735/

### Tailoring a Pediatric Formulation of Artemether-Lumefantrine for Treatment of Plasmodium falciparum Malaria.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26014953)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Africa
- **Design:** randomized, phase 3
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-05-26
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** African children with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 899
- **Impact:** AL Dispersible showed high PCR-corrected cure rates at day 28 comparable to crushed AL tablets (97.8% vs 98.5%).
- **Effect:** PCR-corrected cure rate 97.8% (AL Dispersible) vs 98.5% (crushed tablets)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26014953/

### Malaria preventive therapy in pregnancy and its potential impact on immunity to malaria in an area of declining transmission.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (26006260)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-05-26
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant Melanesian women
- **Impact:** Different antimalarial preventive chemotherapy regimens in pregnancy had limited impact on malaria immunity in this low-transmission setting, with antibodies to recombinant antigens declining similarly in both groups and no significant effect of regimen on antibody levels overall.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** enrolment to delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26006260/

### Gametocyte Clearance Kinetics Determined by Quantitative Magnetic Fractionation in Melanesian Children with Uncomplicated Malaria Treated with Artemisinin Combination Therapy.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25987625)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized, intervention trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-05-18
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum and/or Plasmodium vivax malaria
- **N:** 70
- **Impact:** Artemisinin-naphthoquine led to a larger initial rise in P. falciparum gametocyte density and slower clearance than artemether-lumefantrine, but was associated with less P. vivax recurrence and gametocytemia.
- **Effect:** median time to gametocyte density <2.5/μl 16 days (AL) vs 20 days (AN)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25987625/

### Costs and cost-effectiveness of a large-scale mass testing and treatment intervention for malaria in Southern Province, Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25985992)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-05-20
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** community members in moderate malaria transmission area
- **Impact:** Dry-season MTAT did not sufficiently reduce malaria transmission to enable transition to case-investigation elimination strategy, but it substantially reduced malaria illness and was highly cost-effective for malaria burden reduction.
- **Effect:** incremental cost-effectiveness ratio (cost per DALY averted) USD804
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25985992/

### Health and agricultural productivity: Evidence from Zambia.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25966452)
- **Journal:** Journal of health economics
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-04-28
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** rural farmers
- **N:** 516
- **Impact:** Farmers given access to free bed nets saw harvest value increase by US$76, about 14.7% higher than the average output value, compared to those without access.
- **Effect:** increase in harvest value US$76 (14.7% of average output value)
- **Follow-up:** 1 year
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25966452/

### Prime-boost vaccination with chimpanzee adenovirus and modified vaccinia Ankara encoding TRAP provides partial protection against Plasmodium falciparum infection in Kenyan adults.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25947165)
- **Journal:** Science translational medicine
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-05-01
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** healthy adult male volunteers
- **N:** 121
- **Impact:** Vaccination with ChAd63-MVA ME-TRAP reduced the risk of P. falciparum infection by 67% compared to controls during 8 weeks of monitoring.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy (Cox regression hazard reduction) 67% (95% CI 33-83%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 8 weeks
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25947165/

### [Comparison of the effectiveness of artemether and quinine for treatment of severe malaria in children, Bangui, Central African Republic].
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25925807)
- **Journal:** Bulletin de la Societe de pathologie exotique (1990)
- **Type:** trial
- **Place:** Central African Republic
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-02
- **Population:** children with severe malaria
- **N:** 212
- **Impact:** Artemether and quinine showed similar efficacy and safety in treating severe malaria in children, with high rates of clinical and parasitological improvement and low mortality.
- **Effect:** proportion with clinical/parasitological improvement at day 3 98.1% (artemether) vs 97.1% (quinine)
- **Follow-up:** 3 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25925807/

### Randomized Noninferiority Trial of Dihydroartemisinin-Piperaquine Compared with Sulfadoxine-Pyrimethamine plus Amodiaquine for Seasonal Malaria Chemoprevention in Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25918149)
- **Journal:** Antimicrobial agents and chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-04-27
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children 3 to 59 months old
- **N:** 1499
- **Impact:** Both DHAPQ and SPAQ were highly efficacious for seasonal malaria chemoprevention, though DHAPQ showed slightly higher risk of malaria attack than SPAQ, with an odds ratio of 1.33.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 1.33 (95% CI 1.02 to 1.72)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25918149/

- **Status:** published

- **Class:** vaccine

### Efficacy of sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine + artesunate, sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine + amodiaquine, and sulphadoxine-pyrimethamine alone in uncomplicated falciparum malaria in Mali.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25889633)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Mali
- **Design:** randomized, open-label trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-02-07
- **Trial period:** 2004 to 2005
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged six to 59 months with uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria
- **N:** 912
- **Impact:** SP + amodiaquine was as efficacious as SP + artesunate but both were more efficacious than SP alone in treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Mali.
- **Effect:** PCR-adjusted adequate clinical and parasitological response (cACPR) 99% (SP+AS), 100% (SP+AQ), 97.2% (SP alone)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25889633/

### Artemether-lumefantrine versus artemisinin-naphthoquine in Papua New Guinean children with uncomplicated malaria: a six months post-treatment follow-up study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25889150)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-21
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** young children with uncomplicated malaria
- **N:** 176
- **Impact:** Artemisinin-naphthoquine was associated with fewer clinical malaria episodes than artemether-lumefantrine over six months post-treatment.
- **Effect:** proportion with first clinical malaria episode within six months 32.8% (AM-LM) vs 16.4% (artemisinin-NQ)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25889150/

### Improving uptake and use of malaria rapid diagnostic tests in the context of artemisinin drug resistance containment in eastern Myanmar: an evaluation of incentive schemes among informal private healthcare providers.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25885581)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Myanmar
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, three-arm intervention trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-06
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** informal private healthcare providers and community members with fever in rural townships
- **N:** 6400
- **Impact:** Price subsidy combined with intensified information, education and counselling led to the highest uptake of RDT use and nearly doubled correct treatment following diagnostic test results compared to subsidy alone.
- **Effect:** proportion with correct treatment following diagnostic test result almost double compared to simple subsidy
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25885581/

### Comparison of azithromycin plus chloroquine versus artemether-lumefantrine for the treatment of uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria in children in Africa: a randomized, open-label study.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25881046)
- **Journal:** Malaria journal
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** sub-Saharan Africa
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-10
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children with uncomplicated Plasmodium falciparum malaria, 6-59 months of age
- **N:** 255
- **Impact:** Azithromycin plus chloroquine was not shown to be non-inferior to artemether-lumefantrine for treatment of uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in children, with lower cure rates and more adverse events in the AZCQ group.
- **Effect:** difference in PCR-corrected adequate clinical and parasitological response rate (MITT) -9.10 percentage points (AZCQ 89% vs AL 98%) (95% CI -16.02 to -2.18)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25881046/

### Enhanced protection against malaria by indoor residual spraying in addition to insecticide treated nets: is it dependent on transmission intensity or net usage?
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25811379)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-26
- **Trial period:** 2012
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** children 0.5-14 years old
- **N:** 50
- **Impact:** Adding indoor residual spraying to insecticide treated nets provided additional protection against malaria infection regardless of net usage levels or transmission intensity.
- **Effect:** Odds ratio OR = 0.38 (IRS protection among net users); OR = 0.43 (non-net users) (95% CI 0.26-0.57; 0.29-0.63)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 10 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25811379/

### Population-wide malaria testing and treatment with rapid diagnostic tests and artemether-lumefantrine in southern Zambia: a community randomized step-wedge control trial design.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25802434)
- **Journal:** The American journal of tropical medicine and hygiene
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-23
- **Class:** MDA; treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** community residents including children
- **Impact:** Mass test and treatment reduced malaria prevalence and outpatient case incidence but not enough to approach elimination levels.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio (prevalence); incidence rate ratio (outpatient cases) 0.47 (prevalence); 0.83 (incidence, 17% decrease) (95% CI 0.24-0.90 (OR); 0.68-1.01 (IRR))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25802434/

### The effect of single or repeated home visits on the hanging and use of insecticide-treated mosquito nets following a mass distribution campaign--a cluster randomized, controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25774676)
- **Journal:** PloS one
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-16
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** general population/households following mass ITN distribution campaign
- **N:** 1811
- **Impact:** Additional hang-up home visits did not significantly improve net hanging or ITN use compared to the control arm, as both increased similarly across all three study arms.
- **Effect:** proportion using ITN previous night 64.0% (one visit), 68.2% (two visits), 64.0% (control) (95% CI 60.8-67.2 (one visit); 63.8-72.2 (two visits))
- **Follow-up:** 7 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25774676/

### The impact of providing rapid diagnostic malaria tests on fever management in the private retail sector in Ghana: a cluster randomized trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25739769)
- **Journal:** BMJ (Clinical research ed.)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-03-04
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** clients of private drug retail shops with fever, both adults and children
- **N:** 4603
- **Impact:** Providing rapid diagnostic tests in private drug retail shops significantly reduced antimalarial dispensing to slide-negative clients without reducing treatment of true malaria cases, and appeared safe.
- **Effect:** adjusted risk ratio 0.41 (antimalarial use in slide-negative clients); 2.39 (appropriate treatment) (95% CI 0.29 to 0.58; 1.69 to 3.39)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25739769/

### Evaluation of intermittent preventive treatment of malaria against group B Streptococcus colonization in pregnant women: a nested analysis of a randomized controlled clinical trial of sulfadoxine/pyrimethamine versus mefloquine.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25722300)
- **Journal:** The Journal of antimicrobial chemotherapy
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Gabon
- **Design:** randomized controlled clinical trial, nested analysis
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-02-25
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 549
- **Impact:** Sulfadoxine/pyrimethamine-IPTp did not significantly reduce group B Streptococcus colonization prevalence compared with mefloquine-IPTp.
- **Effect:** prevalence mefloquine-IPTp: 18% vs sulfadoxine/pyrimethamine-IPTp: 21% (95% CI 14-22% (mefloquine); 15-27% (SP))
- **Follow-up:** until delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25722300/

### A Randomized Trial to Compare the Safety, Tolerability, and Effectiveness of 3 Antimalarial Regimens for the Prevention of Malaria in Nigerian Patients With Sickle Cell Disease.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25701866)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Nigeria
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-02-20
- **Class:** chemoprevention; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with sickle cell disease
- **N:** 270
- **Impact:** Intermittent preventive treatment with mefloquine-artesunate was well tolerated and more effective than daily proguanil in preventing malaria in patients with sickle cell disease, while sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine plus amodiaquine showed a less certain benefit.
- **Effect:** protective efficacy 61% (MQAS), 36% (SPAQ) (95% CI 3%-84% (MQAS); -40%-70% (SPAQ))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 14 months
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25701866/

### Impact of intermittent preventive treatment in pregnancy with azithromycin-containing regimens on maternal nasopharyngeal carriage and antibiotic sensitivity of Streptococcus pneumoniae, Haemophilus influenzae, and Staphylococcus aureus: a cross-sectional survey at delivery.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25673788)
- **Journal:** Journal of clinical microbiology
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Papua New Guinea
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2015-02-11
- **Class:** vaccine; IPTp; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** pregnant women
- **N:** 854
- **Impact:** Monthly SPAZ-IPTp significantly reduced maternal nasopharyngeal carriage of S. pneumoniae and H. influenzae at delivery compared to SPCQ, but was associated with an increased proportion of macrolide-resistant pneumococcal isolates.
- **Effect:** carriage prevalence (S. pneumoniae) 7.2% (SPAZ) vs 19.3% (SPCQ)
- **Follow-up:** at delivery
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25673788/

### IFNγ Responses to Pre-erythrocytic and Blood-stage Malaria Antigens Exhibit Differential Associations With Past Exposure and Subsequent Protection.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25520427)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2014-12-17
- **Class:** chemoprevention
- **Population:** children from 1 to 3 years of age
- **N:** 196
- **Impact:** IFNγ responses to blood-stage antigens were common and linked to recent exposure but not protective, while responses to pre-erythrocytic antigens were uncommon but associated with protection from subsequent malaria.
- **Effect:** adjusted hazard ratio 0.52 (pre-erythrocytic antigens); 0.96 (blood-stage antigens)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 1 to 3 years of age
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25520427/

### Efficacy of indoor residual spraying with dichlorodiphenyltrichloroethane against malaria in Gambian communities with high usage of long-lasting insecticidal mosquito nets: a cluster-randomised controlled trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25498847)
- **Journal:** Lancet (London, England)
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Gambia
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2014-12-09
- **Trial period:** 2010 to 2011
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 14 years
- **N:** 70
- **Impact:** Adding indoor residual spraying with DDT to LLINs did not significantly reduce clinical malaria incidence or vector density compared with LLINs alone.
- **Effect:** incident rate ratio 1.08 (95% CI 0.80-1.46)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 transmission seasons (2010-2011)
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25498847/

### Efficacy and safety of the mosquitocidal drug ivermectin to prevent malaria transmission after treatment: a double-blind, randomized, clinical trial.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25414262)
- **Journal:** Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America
- **Type:** trial
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2014-11-19
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; endectocide
- **Population:** asymptomatic Plasmodium falciparum parasite carriers
- **N:** 120
- **Impact:** Adding ivermectin to artemether-lumefantrine increased mosquito mortality and reduced estimated malaria transmission potential by 27% (single dose) and 35% (repeated dose) during the first week of treatment, without evidence of reduced Plasmodium infection rates among surviving mosquitoes.
- **Effect:** hazard rate ratio for An. gambiae mosquito mortality after single dose IVM 1.34 (95% CI 1.07-1.69)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25414262/

### Efficacy and safety of triple combination therapy with artesunate-amodiaquine-methylene blue for falciparum malaria in children: a randomized controlled trial in Burkina Faso.
- **Source / ID:** pubmed (25267980)
- **Journal:** The Journal of infectious diseases
- **Type:** trial
- **Phase:** Phase IIb
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** published
- **Published:** 2014-09-28
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 221
- **Impact:** Adding methylene blue to artesunate-amodiaquine significantly reduced gametocyte prevalence of P. falciparum compared to artesunate-amodiaquine alone, but was associated with lower hemoglobin and more vomiting.
- **Effect:** gametocyte prevalence on day 7 microscopy 1.2% vs 8.9%; QT-NASBA 36.7% vs 63.3%
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7 days
- **Link:** https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25267980/

### Immunogenicity and Safety Study of GSK Biologicals' Candidate Malaria Vaccine Given at 6, 7.5 and 9 Months of Age in Co-administration With Measles, Rubella and Yellow Fever (YF) Vaccines Followed by a Booster of the Malaria Vaccine.
- **Source / ID:** clinicaltrials (NCT02699099)
- **Type:** registration
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Ghana
- **Design:** RANDOMIZED, PARALLEL, PHASE3
- **Status:** results posted
- **Trial period:** 2017-05-10 to 2018-03-14
- **Intervention(s):** Vitamin A; Candidate Plasmodium falciparum malaria vaccine; MR-Vac; Stamaril
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** infants aged 6, 7.5 and 9 months at vaccination, receiving measles, rubella and yellow fever vaccines
- **N:** 699
- **Impact:** The study evaluated immunogenicity and safety of a candidate malaria vaccine given at 6, 7.5 and 9 months of age co-administered with measles, rubella and yellow fever vaccines, followed by a booster dose.
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02699099

### R21/Matrix-M Protects Against Dermal but not Against Venous Parasites in Human Challenge Studies
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR892703)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Design:** controlled human malaria infection, phase 2
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-08-08
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** healthy adult volunteers undergoing controlled human malaria infection
- **N:** 17
- **Impact:** R21/Matrix-M vaccination protected against intradermal sporozoite challenge (0/12 met treatment criteria) but provided no protection against direct venous inoculation of sporozoites (5/5 met treatment criteria).
- **Effect:** proportion protected (meeting treatment criteria) 0/12 (intradermal) vs 5/5 (direct venous inoculation)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.08.06.24311495

### Efficacy of Attractive Targeted Sugar Bait stations against malaria in Western Province Zambia: epidemiological findings from a two-arm cluster randomized Phase III trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR937451)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Zambia
- **Design:** two-arm cluster-randomized controlled trial, phase 3
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-11-08
- **Trial period:** 2022 to 2023
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** children aged 1-14 years (clinical malaria cohort) and individuals over six months (prevalence surveys)
- **N:** 70 clusters; 4494 children in cohort; 2536 individuals in cross-sectional surveys
- **Impact:** Attractive targeted sugar bait stations did not significantly reduce clinical malaria incidence or P. falciparum prevalence compared to control in rural western Zambia.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio 0.91 (95% CI 95% CI 0.72-1.15)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** monthly follow-up January-June 2022 and 2023 (two 6-month transmission seasons)
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-5187530/v1

### Evaluating malaria elimination strategies in military forces in Cambodia: a cluster-randomized clinical trial comparing monthly prophylaxis with focused screening and treatment
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR947640)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Cambodia
- **Design:** cluster-randomized, factorial (sub-randomized to permethrin-treated vs sham clothing)
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-11-28
- **Class:** treatment/ACT; diagnostic
- **Population:** military personnel (volunteers) along the Cambodian-Thai border
- **N:** 1050
- **Impact:** Monthly malaria prophylaxis (MMP) was safe and more effective than focused screening and treatment (FSAT) in reducing both P. falciparum and P. vivax positivity over 6 months.
- **Effect:** absolute risk reduction in Pf positivity MMP: 90% reduction (ARR 9%); FSAT: 66% reduction (ARR 4.9%)
- **Species:** mixed
- **Follow-up:** 6 months
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.11.26.24318027

### Safety and efficacy of the blood-stage malaria vaccine RH5.1/Matrix-M in Burkina Faso: interim results of a double-blind, randomised, controlled phase 2b trial in children
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR925537)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 2b
- **Place:** Burkina Faso
- **Design:** double-blind, randomised, controlled, phase 2b
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-10-16
- **Trial period:** 2023
- **Class:** vaccine
- **Population:** children aged 5-17 months
- **N:** 361
- **Impact:** RH5.1/Matrix-M vaccine was safe, well tolerated, and showed vaccine efficacy of 55% with a delayed third dose regimen and 40% with a monthly regimen against clinical malaria compared to pooled controls.
- **Effect:** vaccine efficacy 55% (delayed third dose); 40% (monthly regimen) (95% CI 20-75% (delayed); -3-65% (monthly))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 months post-third vaccination
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.10.15.24315473

### Artemether-lumefantrine-amodiaquine or artesunate-amodiaquine combined with single low-dose primaquine to reduce<i>Plasmodium falciparum</i>malaria transmission in Ouélessébougou, Mali: a five-arm, phase 2, single-blind, randomised clinical trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR953171)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Ouélessébougou, Mali
- **Design:** five-arm, single-blind, phase 2, randomised clinical trial, per protocol analysis
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-02-24
- **Trial period:** October 2022 to December 2022
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** asymptomatic P. falciparum microscopy-detected gametocyte carriers aged 10-50 years
- **N:** 100
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine, with or without amodiaquine, and artesunate-amodiaquine plus primaquine nearly eliminated mosquito infectivity within 48 hours, whereas artesunate-amodiaquine alone left substantial residual transmission.
- **Effect:** median percentage reduction in mosquito infection rate 100% (artemether-lumefantrine, artemether-lumefantrine-amodiaquine, artemether-lumefantrine-amodiaquine+primaquine, artesunate-amodiaquine+primaquine) vs 31.67% (artesunate-amodiaquine alone) (95% CI IQR 100-100 (primaquine/artemether-lumefantrine arms); IQR -10.9-100 (artesunate-amodiaquine arm))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 2 days (48 hours) post-treatment
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.02.23.24303266

### Artemisinin-naphthoquine plus lower-dose primaquine to treat and prevent recurrence of Plasmodium vivax malaria: an open-label randomized and non-inferiority trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR693635)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Place:** China-Myanmar border
- **Design:** open-label, randomized, non-inferiority trial
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2023-07-18
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** patients with P. vivax malaria infection
- **N:** 288
- **Impact:** The artemisinin-naphthoquine plus lower-dose primaquine over 3 days (ANPQ3) regimen was not non-inferior to chloroquine plus primaquine over 14 days (CQPQ14) in preventing recurrence, but cleared fever and parasites faster, had fewer severe adverse effects, and had better medication adherence.
- **Effect:** difference in recurrence-free patients (percentage points) by day 182 3.5 (95% CI -8.6 to 1.5)
- **Species:** P. vivax
- **Follow-up:** 365 days
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-3165342/v1

### Artemether-lumefantrine with or without single-dose primaquine and sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine plus amodiaquine with or without single-dose tafenoquine to reduce<i>Plasmodium falciparum</i>transmission: a phase 2 single-blind randomised clinical trial in Ouelessebougou, Mali
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR811905)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 2
- **Place:** Ouelessebougou, Mali
- **Design:** phase 2, single-blind, randomised clinical trial
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-02-28
- **Trial period:** 2021 to 2021
- **Class:** SMC; treatment/ACT
- **Population:** asymptomatic individuals aged 10-50 years with P. falciparum gametocytaemia
- **N:** 80
- **Impact:** Artemether-lumefantrine alone nearly completely prevented mosquito infections, whereas sulfadoxine-pyrimethamine plus amodiaquine left considerable post-treatment transmission that was reduced by adding tafenoquine.
- **Effect:** median percentage reduction in mosquito infection rate 100% (AL), 100% (AL+primaquine), 63.60% (SP+AQ), 100% (SP+AQ+tafenoquine) (95% CI AL: 97.2-100; AL+PQ: 100-100; SP+AQ: 0.62-100; SP+AQ+TQ: 100-100)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 7 days
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.02.27.24303370

### Malaria and other risk factors for malnutrition, and the impact of intermittent preventive therapy for malaria on nutritional status of school-age children in Tanzania. A cross-sectional survey and a randomized controlled open-label trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR785264)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** cross-sectional survey and randomized controlled open-label trial
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-01-04
- **Class:** IPTsc
- **Population:** school-age children (≤10 years)
- **Impact:** Intermittent preventive therapy with DP or ASAQ did not significantly affect change in weight, height, or BMI over time compared to the control group, though malaria at baseline was associated with higher odds of stunting.
- **Effect:** adjusted odds ratio (stunting associated with malaria) AOR=1.78 (95% CI 1.36-2.33)
- **Follow-up:** 20 months
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.01.03.24300756

### Will a lack of fabric durability be their downfall? Impact of textile durability on the efficacy of three types of dual-active-ingredient long-lasting insecticidal nets: A secondary analysis on malaria prevalence and incidence from a cluster-randomized trial in north-west Tanzania
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR809518)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** cluster-randomized trial, secondary analysis
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-02-22
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2022
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** community residents and a cohort of children
- **Impact:** Torn nets showed reduced protection against malaria, but Chlorfenapyr-PY and PBO-PY LLINs maintained superior protection compared to pyrethroid-only nets even when torn.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio 0.76 (good condition vs extremely torn); 0.37 (too-torn PBO-PY vs intact PY); 0.45 (too-torn Chlorfenapyr-PY vs intact PY); 0.49 (intact Chlorfenapyr-PY vs intact PY, year 2) (95% CI 0.63-0.92; 0.19-0.72; 0.33-0.97; 0.29-0.81)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 years (prevalence), 2 years (incidence)
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-3974056/v1

### Impact of textile durability on the efficacy of three types of dual-active-ingredient long- lasting insecticidal nets: A secondary analysis on malaria prevalence and incidence from a cluster-randomized trial in north-west Tanzania
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR784614)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** cluster-randomized trial, secondary analysis
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-01-09
- **Trial period:** 2019 to 2022
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN
- **Population:** community members and a cohort of children
- **Impact:** Torn Dual-AI LLINs (chlorfenapyr-PY and PBO-PY) still provided better protection against malaria case incidence than intact pyrethroid-only nets, though net textile damage was not significantly associated with malaria prevalence overall.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio (IRR) 0.76 (good condition vs extremely torn nets, all types); 0.37 (too-torn PBO-PY vs intact PY-only); 0.45 (too-torn Chlorfenapyr-PY vs intact PY-only); 0.49 (intact Chlorfenapyr-PY vs intact PY-only, year 2) (95% CI 0.63-0.92; 0.19-0.72; 0.33-0.97; 0.29-0.81)
- **Follow-up:** 3 years (prevalence), 2 years (incidence cohort)
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-3828176/v1

### Community benefits of mass distribution of three types of dual-active-ingredient long-lasting insecticidal nets against malaria prevalence in Tanzania: evidence from a 3-year cluster-randomized controlled trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR792557)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Tanzania
- **Design:** cluster-randomized controlled trial, secondary analysis, 3-year, 5 cross-sectional surveys
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-01-25
- **Trial period:** 2020 to 2022
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; diagnostic
- **Population:** children aged 6 months to 14 years
- **N:** 22479
- **Impact:** Community-level usage of more than 40% of dual-active-ingredient LLINs was associated with significant protection against malaria infection among non-net users compared to non-users in clusters with high pyrethroid-only LLIN usage.
- **Effect:** Odds Ratio chlorfenapyr-PY: 0.44; PBO-PY: 0.55; pyriproxyfen-PY: 0.61 (community usage >40% vs PY-only >40%) (95% CI 0.27-0.71; 0.33-0.94; 0.37-0.99)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 3 years
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.01.23.24301709

### Assessing the impact of group antenatal care on uptake of intermittent preventive treatment for malaria in pregnancy in Atlantique Department, Benin, 2021-2023: a cluster randomized controlled trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR938467)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** cluster-randomized controlled trial
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-11-12
- **Trial period:** 2021 to 2023
- **Class:** IPTp
- **Population:** pregnant women attending antenatal care
- **N:** 2539
- **Impact:** G-ANC did not significantly improve population-level ANC4 or IPTp3 coverage compared to individual ANC, but among women who actually participated in G-ANC, both ANC4 and IPTp3 uptake were significantly higher.
- **Effect:** odds ratio 1.8 (IPTp3), 1.9 (ANC4) (95% CI 1.2-2.6 (IPTp3), 1.4-2.5 (ANC4))
- **Follow-up:** 12 months
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-5357193/v1

### Feasibility and safety of integrating mass drug administration for helminth control with seasonal malaria chemoprevention among Senegalese children: a randomized controlled, observer-blind trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR723707)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Senegal
- **Design:** randomized, controlled, observer-blind, three-arm trial
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2023-09-12
- **Trial period:** 2022 to 2022
- **Class:** SMC; MDA
- **Population:** children aged 1-14 years
- **N:** 627
- **Impact:** Integrating MDA for schistosomiasis and STH with SMC was safe and feasible among Senegalese children, though vomiting was more common in the intervention groups than the control group.
- **Effect:** Odds ratio for severe anaemia OR = 0.81 (95% CI 0.13-5.00)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 6 days post-intervention (adverse events); pre- and post-intervention blood/stool sampling
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-3332221/v1

### LLIN Evaluation in Uganda Project (LLINEUP2) – Effect of long-lasting insecticidal nets (LLINs) treated with pyrethroid plus pyriproxyfen vs LLINs treated with pyrethroid plus piperonyl butoxide in Uganda: a cluster-randomised trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR890137)
- **Journal:** medRxiv
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Uganda
- **Design:** pragmatic, cluster-randomised trial
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2024-08-02
- **Trial period:** 2020 to 2023
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** residents of all ages in 32 high-burden districts not receiving indoor residual spraying; community surveys in children aged 2-10 years and 2-4 years for anaemia
- **N:** 64
- **Impact:** There was no significant difference in malaria incidence, parasite prevalence, or anaemia prevalence between clusters receiving pyrethroid-PBO LLINs and those receiving pyrethroid-pyriproxyfen LLINs two years after mass distribution.
- **Effect:** incidence rate ratio 1.06 (95% CI 0.91-1.22)
- **Follow-up:** 24 months
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.07.31.24311272

### Efficacy of Quinine Plus Clindamycin Versus Artemether-Lumefantrine For The Treatment of Uncomplicated Plasmodium Falciparum Malaria In Kenyan Children (CLINDAQUINE): An Open-Label Randomised Trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR389258)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Kenya
- **Design:** open-label, randomised, phase 3
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2021-08-31
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 6-59 months with uncomplicated falciparum malaria
- **N:** 384
- **Impact:** Quinine plus clindamycin was markedly less effective than artemether-lumefantrine for treating uncomplicated P. falciparum malaria in Kenyan children, with no evidence supporting its use.
- **Effect:** treatment difference in PCR-corrected ACPR rate -53.1% (95% CI -43.5% to -62.7%)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-819707/v1

### Durable wall lining for malaria control in Liberia: results of a cluster randomized trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR505826)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Liberia, Bomi County
- **Design:** cluster-randomized controlled trial, paired cluster design
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2022-06-14
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; diagnostic
- **Population:** children 2-59 months
- **N:** 40
- **Impact:** P. falciparum prevalence after 12 months was lower in intervention clusters (34.6%) than control clusters (40.1%), but the difference was not statistically significant.
- **Effect:** risk ratio 34.6% vs 40.1% prevalence
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 24 months
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-1731150/v1

### Community evaluation of VECTRONTM T500, a broflanilide insecticide, for indoor residual spraying for malaria vector control in Central Benin; a two arm non-inferiority cluster randomised trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR693689)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Not applicable
- **Place:** Benin
- **Design:** two-arm non-inferiority cluster randomised trial
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2023-07-18
- **Class:** ITN/LLIN; IRS
- **Population:** Households and communities in an area with pyrethroid-resistant Anopheles gambiae s.l. and high pyrethroid-only net coverage
- **N:** 16
- **Impact:** VECTRONTM T500 was non-inferior to Fludora® Fusion in reducing indoor and outdoor human biting rates and had prolonged residual efficacy on house walls lasting over 24 months.
- **Effect:** Incidence Rate Ratio (IRR) for human biting rate IRR = 0.54 (indoor), IRR = 0.75 (outdoor) (95% CI 0.22-1.35 (indoor), 0.41-1.38 (outdoor))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 18-24 months
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-3100156/v1

### Efficacy and Safety of Pyronaridine-Artesunate Versus Artemether-Lumefantrine in the Treatment of Acute Uncomplicated Malaria in Children in South-West Nigeria: An open- labelled randomized controlled trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR495797)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Nigeria (Ibadan, South-West Nigeria)
- **Design:** open-labelled, randomized controlled trial, phase 3
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2022-05-20
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** children aged 3 to 144 months with fever and microscopically confirmed Plasmodium falciparum malaria
- **N:** 172
- **Impact:** Pyronaridine-artesunate showed a significantly higher day-28 adequate clinical and parasitological response than artemether-lumefantrine in children with uncomplicated malaria in Nigeria.
- **Effect:** Day 28 adequate clinical and parasitological response (ACPR) 92.7% (PA) vs 71.1% (AL) (95% CI PA: 83.1-95.9; AL: 60.4-79.9)
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 28 days
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-1664143/v1

### Efficacy of Three Antimalarial Regimens for Uncomplicated Plasmodium Falciparum Malaria in Cambodia, 2009 – 2011: A Randomized Control Trial
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR436554)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase 3
- **Place:** Cambodia (Oddar Meanchey province)
- **Design:** randomized, open-label, parallel group controlled trial, phase 3
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2021-12-28
- **Trial period:** 2009 to 2011
- **Class:** treatment/ACT
- **Population:** subjects at least 5 years old with uncomplicated Falciparum malaria
- **N:** 211
- **Impact:** DHA/PPQ and ATQ/PG had acceptable efficacy against uncomplicated falciparum malaria, while AS/MQ efficacy was only 80.6% due to apparent mefloquine resistance.
- **Effect:** PCR-adjusted efficacy at 42 days 80.6% (AS/MQ), 97.2% (DHA/PPQ), 92.9% (ATQ/PG) (95% CI 70.8-90.5% (AS/MQ), 93.3-100% (DHA/PPQ), 86.1-99.6% (ATQ/PG))
- **Species:** P. falciparum
- **Follow-up:** 42 days
- **Link:** https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-1177741/v1

### Randomized clinical trial to assess the protective efficacy of a Plasmodium vivax CS synthetic vaccine
- **Source / ID:** europepmc (PPR317474)
- **Journal:** Research Square
- **Type:** preprint
- **Phase:** Phase II
- **Status:** preprint
- **Published:** 2021-04-29
- **Class:** vaccine; monoclonal antibody
- **Impact:** <title>Abstract</title>  <p>A randomized, double-blind, controlled clinical trial was conducted to assess the safety and protective efficacy of the <italic>Plasmodium vivax</italic> circumsporozoite (CS) protein.
